## A1-DL-003 — spa-split (A1) P2: the restore runbook, and RTO/RPO adopted as targets rather than claims

- **Date.** 2026-07-27
- **Type.** Decision record — docs/ops packet, bundled into A1 by owner directive (vault ADR-037 §4). Zero product code; DOM-parity-neutral.
- **Decision.** Write the database recovery path into `docs/RUNBOOK.md` as a single self-contained **Restore / disaster recovery** section, describing the **actual current** store (managed Cloud SQL PostgreSQL + pgvector, ADR-008) rather than the AlloyDB successor, and adopt explicit RTO/RPO targets:

  a. **RPO — 5–15 minutes, written as conditional, not asserted.** The figure holds only if Cloud SQL PITR is enabled on the live instance. This repo carries no infrastructure-as-code and no Cloud SQL configuration of any kind, so PITR status is not derivable from it; the runbook therefore states the target, the condition, the `gcloud sql instances describe` verification, and the fallback — **if PITR is off the real RPO is the age of the last automated backup, up to 24 hours.** The retention window (Cloud SQL default 7 days, configurable 1–35) is stated the same way: a recovery point outside it is unreachable at any price.

  b. **RTO — 2–4 hours, carrying its own disclaimer.** The runbook states, in the target itself, that **an untested RTO is an assumption, not an RTO**. No restore drill has ever been run on this project, so the number is a target and is not to be quoted as a guarantee until a drill has been run end-to-end and timed.

  c. **The pre-migration backup is the load-bearing habit.** Logical corruption — a bad Alembic migration or backfill run — is named as the likelier disaster than physical loss, because migrations are run **by hand** against the target database (no deploy-time release step exists). The standing pre-step records three facts, not one: backup id, `alembic current` from-revision, and the UTC start timestamp. PITR takes a timestamp, so a backup without a recorded time discards most of its value.

  d. **Restore sequence, every GCP step marked owner-run.** Stop the writers and pick the recovery point → PITR **clone to a new instance** (Cloud SQL never rewinds in place; the damaged instance is kept as evidence) → stand up and confirm `vector` extension plus a usable role → validate in three tiers before switching anything (migration state must equal the *intended* revision, not `head`; row counts plus the HNSW-index survival check, since that index is built by the offline backfill rather than by a migration and is the most plausible silent loss; app-level read smoke, explicitly **not** `pytest api`) → switch `DATABASE_URL` and the Cloud SQL attachment → return traffic through the existing **Promotion + rollback** gate → post-incident review that times the achieved RTO/RPO against these targets.

  e. **Erasure-suppression reconciliation is written in now as a marked no-op.** A restore rewinds past deletions as well as past damage, so once accounts and deletion semantics exist (vault ADR-010 / PDR-010, arriving with the authenticated spine) re-applying every erasure between the recovery point and now becomes a mandatory gate before traffic returns. No accounts, no deletion semantics and no suppression ledger exist today, so the step is a no-op — placed in the sequence so it is inherited rather than rediscovered.

  f. **Drill cadence: after each migration (light) plus a periodic timed full drill.** Owner action; nothing is automated and nothing in CI runs any part of the section. The full drill is named as the only thing that converts the RTO from an assumption into an RTO.
- **Rationale.** The engine spine has landed three Alembic revisions and a production Cloud SQL store, and migrations are applied by hand with no gate — so the project acquired a way to destroy family data before it acquired a written way to get it back. A recovery document is worth what it is worth **under stress**, which drove three shapes: it is self-contained (a stressed operator reads one section, not a link graph); it states conditions and verification commands instead of asserting facts the repo cannot support; and it distinguishes owner-run GCP actions from everything else on every step.
- **No dedicated ADR by design at the repo layer.** The packet realizes the vault infrastructure-overview note (2026-07-10 research) and the ADR-037 §4 bundling directive; the RTO/RPO figures are adopted here, not invented here.
- **Alternatives considered.**
  1. **Assert RPO 5–15 min flatly.** Rejected — unverifiable from this repo, and a wrong RPO in a runbook is worse than an honest unknown: it is discovered exactly when it cannot be fixed.
  2. **Describe the AlloyDB restore path as the primary one.** Rejected — nothing in this repo targets AlloyDB. It gets a short, explicitly-marked future note (PITR restores to a **new cluster** whose primary instance must be created manually, making the connection switch two-step) so the difference is known before it is needed, not during.
  3. **Author a full `## Database migrations` workflow section to hang the pre-migration backup on.** Rejected as scope expansion; the standing pre-step lives inside the restore section, with a factual statement of how migrations are actually run today.
  4. **Write the connection-switch step as if `api/cloudbuild.yaml` wired the database.** Rejected — it does not (no `--set-env-vars`, no `--set-secrets`, no `--add-cloudsql-instances`), so the step reads live state from the console instead. Assuming otherwise is precisely the failure that breaks a restore at 3 a.m.
  5. **Introduce fenced code blocks for the restore commands.** Rejected — `docs/RUNBOOK.md` contains none, and a packet with no restyling mandate does not unilaterally change the file's form. Recorded as a small, real usability cost.
  6. **Omit the Cloud SQL identifiers from "Live-infra divergence" entirely.** Rejected at owner review — the section carries a three-bullet stub with **empty** placeholders instead, so the gap is visible where the identifiers belong. Filling them is a deliberate owner act, not a doc default: the repository is temporarily public.
- **Supersedes.** None. Supersedes nothing in the ledger; it does supersede, in practice, the DR sketch in the historical `data/mvp_masterplan.md` §K (24 h RPO, a `scripts/pg_backup_before_migration.sh` that was never written), which was frozen by `M1-DL-001` and is not revived here.
- **Effects.**
  - **Changed (docs only):** `docs/RUNBOOK.md` (new **Restore / disaster recovery** section; three stale M3-era lines refreshed; a Cloud SQL stub added to **Live-infra divergence**), `docs/execution-map.md`, and this ledger. No `app/**`, no `api/**`, no CI, no pre-commit config.
  - **Three stale claims removed.** The build-path `/api` bullet, the `/api` FastAPI note and the dev-stack bullet each still said DB config / DB use / episodic schema "lands in M3", which M3 and M4 had already falsified. A restore document must not sit beside statements known to be false, so they now state current fact: the deployed surface is still only `/api/health` and opens no connection, the schema comes from three landed Alembic revisions, and the M3/M4 database consumers are the owner-run offline CLIs.
  - **No `docs/INVARIANTS.md` entry.** `AGENTS.md` §10 — enforced-only. This packet enforces nothing in code; a runbook section is a habit, not a gate. Empty by rule, not by omission.
  - **Operability: none** (ADR-013 / contract §4.7). Documentation only: no `theygrow_api.parameters` knob (so no `changed_in` surface), no `theygrow_api.signals` taxonomy signal, no new GA4 event, no change to production emission. `DATABASE_URL` is referenced as the existing bound config var, not introduced.
  - **§4-safe.** The section names the connection *variable* and its *location* (Cloud Run service config, or a Secret Manager secret referenced by `--set-secrets`) and never a value, host, user or password; no child PII appears in any command, and the app-level smoke is described against an environment variable rather than a file.
  - **Parity-neutral.** Docs-only diff; the A1 acceptance gate is untouched and unaffected by construction.
  - **Verification status.** The runbook has **not** been executed: no backup was taken, no clone was made, no drill was run. Every target in it is a target, and the RTO in particular remains an assumption until the first timed full drill — which the section says of itself.

---

## A1-DL-004 — spa-split (A1) P3: the out-of-line asset contract is version-in-path with copy-forward bumps, and the ship list gets a drift guard

- **Date.** 2026-07-27
- **Type.** Decision record — the packet's code and this entry land together. First extraction packet of A1; pure refactor, zero product change.
- **Decision.** Define the delivery contract for everything that leaves `app/index.html` during the split, and prove it end-to-end with the two lowest-coupling carriers in the file:

  a. **Version-in-path, not version-in-filename.** Extracted assets live in a versioned static mount `app/m/v{N}/`, served at `/m/v{N}/`. This is an instantiation of fork (a) of the hazard `A1-DL-002` deferred here (vault ADR-024, versioning-by-URL); the repo's existing instance of that rule puts the version in the *filename* (`kb-v1.json`, `icon-logo-192-v2.png`). Version-in-path was chosen because the split will land ~10 modules with relative imports between them: a bump is then one directory copy, not N coordinated renames, and the import graph inside the mount is untouched by it.

  b. **Bumps are copy-forward, never renames** — the binding condition from ADR-024's core rule that a published asset is never overwritten or removed in place. `app/m/v{N+1}/` is created as a full copy and changed there; `app/m/v{N}/` stays on disk **byte-untouched**, so a client holding cached HTML that references the old mount keeps resolving it for the cache lifetime. Retiring an old mount is a **separate owner cleanup**, never part of the bump packet (the vault ADR-024 OQ#1 posture, mirroring the orphaned icon placeholders). The mechanics, and the rule for when to bump, are written into `docs/RUNBOOK.md` → **Module mount (owner-run version bump)**; each bump's rationale goes in that packet's ledger entry.

  c. **A `CACHE_VERSION` bump alone does not refresh an existing mount URL** — the reason (b) is load-bearing rather than tidy. `cache.addAll` goes through the HTTP cache, so inside the 30-day `immutable` window the browser hands the new worker its stored copy without revalidating and the new cache generation is filled with the **stale** bytes, which then survive every subsequent bump. Changing the bytes at a published mount URL is therefore not a supported operation; the bump procedure states this first, not as a footnote.

  d. **`app/nginx.conf` is untouched.** The generic static location (`~* \.(js|css|…)$` → `public, immutable, max-age=2592000`) is not a hazard under version-in-path — it is exactly the semantics wanted, once the URL never carries changed bytes. So the packet adds no location, and the `app/tests/server.js` mirror and its drift guard need no change. `A1-DL-002`'s diagnosis stands as written; the resolution is to make the URLs worthy of the header rather than to weaken the header.

  e. **`app/Dockerfile` copies the mount root, not a version.** One line — `COPY m/ /usr/share/nginx/html/m/` — permanently discharges the second half of the `A1-DL-002` hazard: every later A1 packet, and every future version bump, ships without a Dockerfile edit. This is the one step the KB-artifact bump checklist requires that this scheme eliminates.

  f. **The two carriers.** The `<style>` block (`index.html:52`–`1651`, ~1,600 lines, no JS semantics) → `app/m/v1/app.css`, linked from the same head position so cascade order and render-blocking behaviour are unchanged; the service-worker-registration block (`:3415`–`3474`, 58 lines, self-contained and `window.load`-gated) → `app/m/v1/sw-register.js`, loaded as `<script type="module">`. Both are byte-exact slices of the shell, dedented one 8-space level. The 1,480-line stateful app script at `:1803`–`3284` stays **inline and byte-untouched**: this packet pays down delivery risk, and P4 pays down JS-scope risk, separately. `index.html` goes 3,476 → 1,818 lines.

  g. **`@charset "UTF-8";` is prepended to `app.css`** — the single deliberate deviation from a pure byte-slice. The block contains non-ASCII in **rendered** `content:` values (`'✓'`, `'Чат сообщества'`, `'▼'`, `'▶'`). Inline, their encoding was pinned by the shell's `<meta charset>`; extracted, it falls to the CSS document-encoding fallback — and the parity mirror sends `text/css; charset=utf-8` while production nginx sends `text/css` bare, so **no test in this repo can observe the difference**. One line removes a spec-fallback dependency whose failure mode is mojibake in production with a green gate. The extracted JS needs no equivalent: HTML mandates UTF-8 for module scripts regardless of Content-Type.

  h. **A ship-list drift guard, asserted in both directions.** `app/tests/server.js` serves from the `app/` directory on disk, so a file `app/Dockerfile` forgets to `COPY` passes the entire suite and 404s only in production. `app/tests/delivery-contract.spec.js` now parses the real `COPY` list and asserts that (1) everything the shipped shell references is shipped **and exists on disk**, and (2) everything the shipped shell references is **also precached by name**. Direction (2) is the one with teeth for (b): a copy-forward bump leaves both `/m/v1/` and `/m/v2/` shipped, so (1) alone would stay green while `index.html` pointed at `v2` and `OFFLINE_URLS` still listed `v1` — installed clients precaching the wrong asset, silently, for as long as the install lives. The parser fails **closed**: any `COPY` form it does not fully understand throws, because a skipped line is a loud false negative while a mis-parsed destination is a silent false positive — the exact bug the guard exists to prevent. `OFFLINE_URLS` is read textually with a loud throw on no-match (`app/sw.js` calls `self.addEventListener` and cannot be `require()`d), mirroring the `mutatedServiceWorker()` pattern.
- **Rationale.** `A1-DL-002` landed the gate that can falsify "nothing changed" and diagnosed the two delivery hazards that bite on the *first byte to leave the shell*, deferring both to "the split packets". Those hazards are not per-packet — they are properties of the seam every later packet will cross — so the first extraction's real job is to fix the seam, and the carriers are chosen to make that job reviewable rather than to maximise lines moved. The stylesheet is the largest contiguous block in the file with no JS semantics; the SW-registration block is the only JS block whose relocation is provably inert (block-scoped throughout, `window.load`-gated). Neither touches the global lexical scope the parity suite reaches through, which is precisely what P4 must confront.
- **No dedicated ADR by design at the repo layer.** The packet realizes vault **ADR-037** (the hybrid split), instantiates vault **ADR-024** (versioning-by-URL; mount shape and naming are not pinned by ADR-037 and are decided here), and operates the existing **ADR-019** / `PWA-DL-001` `CACHE_VERSION` knob. The vault-side ADR-024 annotation recording the chosen path is handled at milestone close.
- **Alternatives considered.**
  1. **Per-file version suffixes (`app-v1.css`), the literal `kb-v{N}.json` precedent.** Rejected — a ~10-module split would need N coordinated renames per release, and every relative import between modules would churn with them. Version-in-path bumps the whole graph in one copy.
  2. **A narrow non-immutable location for app modules (fork (b) of `A1-DL-002`).** Rejected — it buys freshness-by-request at the cost of a revalidation round-trip on every boot for every module, and it only exists to compensate for URLs that carry changing bytes. Making the URLs immutable-worthy is the cheaper invariant.
  3. **A narrow `^/m/v[0-9]+/` immutable location at `max-age=31536000`, mirroring the KB rule.** Rejected — 30 days is already safe when the URL never carries changed bytes, and it would add a location the drift-guard mirror must learn for no behavioural gain.
  4. **`.mjs` for the module.** Rejected — absent from the base image's `mime.types` *and* from the generic static regex, so it would be served `application/octet-stream` outside the immutable rule, and a non-JavaScript MIME blocks a module outright.
  5. **`COPY m/v1/` rather than `COPY m/`.** Rejected — it re-arms the no-wildcard hazard on every single bump, which is the thing this packet exists to retire.
  6. **`charset utf-8; charset_types …;` in `nginx.conf` instead of `@charset`.** Rejected — an `http`-level directive is **invisible** to the drift guard (which only brace-matches `location` blocks and collects `add_header`), so it would ship an unmirrored production behaviour. Recorded as a real, pre-existing gap in that guard's reach.
  7. **Extract the 1,480-line app script in the same packet.** Rejected — it changes JS scoping semantics and would land on top of a delivery seam that had never been exercised; a red gate would then be unattributable between the two.
  8. **Rely on the promotion-smoke curl alone to catch a missing `COPY`.** Rejected — that is a manual step run once per deploy by one person; the failure it catches is green-CI-then-production-404, which is exactly the class that deserves a test.
- **Supersedes.** None. Discharges the obligation `A1-DL-002` recorded as "deliberately deferred to the split packets, diagnosed here". Builds on `A1-DL-002` (the gate it is measured by), `VDK-DL-001` / `ICON-DL-001` / `A0-DL-001` (the versioning-by-URL and never-overwrite precedents it generalises), and `PWA-DL-001` (the `CACHE_VERSION` + `#updateBanner` delivery path it operates).
- **Effects.**
  - **Added (shipped):** `app/m/v1/app.css`, `app/m/v1/sw-register.js`.
  - **Changed (shipped):** `app/index.html` (two block replacements; every other byte verified identical), `app/sw.js` (`CACHE_VERSION` `v8`→`v9`, two `OFFLINE_URLS` entries), `app/Dockerfile` (one `COPY`).
  - **Changed (dev/CI):** `app/tests/delivery-contract.spec.js` (ship-list guard, live mount-header assertions, the ES-module-MIME assertion made real), `app/tests/server.js`, `app/tests/behavior.spec.js` (comment), `app/.dockerignore` (comment), `.pre-commit-config.yaml` (`m/` in both rewriting-hook excludes — every shipped `app/` file is excluded, and the version-agnostic prefix means future mount versions inherit it).
  - **Untouched, verified byte-identical:** `app/nginx.conf`, `app/kb-v1.json`, `app/manifest.json`, `app/offline.html`, `app/cloudbuild.yaml`, `app/playwright.config.js`, `app/tests/__baselines__/` (**zero baseline writes** — the acceptance criterion, not a side effect), `.github/workflows/ci.yml`.
  - **`CACHE_VERSION` bumped `v8`→`v9`, and honestly scoped.** It is required for *offline completeness* — the two new paths must enter the precache by name — but it is **not** load-bearing for the transition: a v8 client receives the new shell over the network-first navigation path, misses `/m/v1/app.css` in `theygrow-v8`, falls through to `fetch` and opportunistically caches it, so it works online and offline before v9 ever activates. `activate()` purges `theygrow-v8`. The bump is mechanical operation of the existing knob (`changed_in: PWA-DL-001`), not a new one.
  - **The parity suite got *stronger*, not just green.** `cache.addAll` is atomic, so the offline-boot test's existing precache probe (`/kb-v1.json` present) now implies both new paths are present too. Conversely a wrong path fails SW install entirely, which surfaces as a bare timeout rather than a named error — recorded here because the diagnostic is poor and the cause is not obvious.
  - **A mirror-only claim was found and corrected.** The pre-existing "ES module MIME" assertion checked `text/javascript` against `app/tests/server.js`'s own MIME table. The built container serves `.js` as **`application/javascript`** (nginx 1.31.3, base-image `mime.types`), for `/sw.js` as well as for the new module — so that assertion had never described production. Both are JavaScript MIME essences and both load a module, so nothing was broken; but the mirror now claims what production sends, and the assertion now checks the spec **class** (any JavaScript MIME essence), because the exact value lives in an unpinned base image that no test can parse. The exact production value is pinned by a new promotion-smoke curl instead.
  - **Operability: none** (ADR-013 / contract §4.7). No `theygrow_api.parameters` knob (the typed-config home is in `/api`, outside this front-only perimeter — the `VDK-DL-001` / `SLA-DL-001` precedent), no `theygrow_api.signals` taxonomy signal, no new GA4 event, **no change to production emission**: the extracted registration block contains no `trackEvent` call, and the install-prompt block that does is untouched and still inline. `/m/v{N}/` is versioning-by-URL — a new version is a new directory plus a code change, not a runtime knob — and `max-age=2592000` is a constant inside the immutable-pattern invariant, not a tunable.
  - **§4-safe.** No new network egress of any kind: both assets are same-origin static files on the existing delivery channel, and no third-party origin is added or contacted. No personal or family data is touched, and no telemetry surface changes.
  - **One real user-visible cost, recorded rather than worked around.** `type="module"` is deferred, and `DOMContentLoaded` — which boots the app — now waits for the module to be fetched and executed, so a same-origin round-trip joins the critical path (the 630 KB `kb-v1.json` fetch still dominates, and a failed module load fails fast rather than hanging). The obvious workaround, `async`, is a trap: an `async` module can execute after `load` has already fired, in which case the service worker is never registered at all. A cold first visit also pays one extra RTT for the stylesheet. Neither is observable to the parity gate; neither is claimed to be zero.
  - **Seam-honesty caveat** (M4-CLOSE discipline). The suite proves the extraction is refactor-equivalent in one pinned headless Chromium against a mirror of `nginx.conf`. It does **not** prove production `Content-Type` (nothing in this repo parses the base image's `mime.types`), real-device rendering, other engines, or on-device PWA install/splash after the version bump. Those stay owner on-device steps under the ADR-020 gate — which is why this packet additionally validated the **built container** directly: both mount paths curl'd for status/headers, and a headless load of the real image confirmed the stylesheet applied (`--skill-col-width` computed from real font metrics), the module executed, the worker reached `activated` on `theygrow-v9` with both paths precached, and zero console errors.
  - **Diagnosed here, deferred (the `A1-DL-002` posture):** `AGENTS.md` §7's live-deploy path enumeration and the parallel comment in `scripts/check-contract-integrity.sh` do not list `m/`. Both are contract-adjacent files; owner-confirmed at plan review that contract-file edits stay out of split packets, so the staleness is recorded for A1-CLOSE rather than fixed here. Also noted: the delivery drift guard cannot see `http`-level nginx directives (alternative 6); `app/.dockerignore` is not consulted by the ship-list guard, so a future ignore pattern matching `m/` would make `COPY m/` ship nothing with the guard still green (stated in the guard's own comment and in `A1-P3-INV-001`); and `app/package.json`'s `test:dom` script names a project that does not exist.

---

## A1-DL-005 — spa-split (A1) P4: a thin `core/` under the mount, a module entry point, and a parity seam that is a module export rather than a global

- **Date.** 2026-07-27
- **Type.** Decision record — the packet's code and this entry land together. Second extraction packet of A1; pure refactor, zero product change.
- **Decision.** Move the 1,480-line stateful app script out of `app/index.html` into a module graph under the existing mount, and solve the problem that move creates — the parity suite reached the app through global lexical bindings that module scope removes.

  a. **`core/` is five single-purpose modules, not one "core".** `app/m/v1/core/storage.js` (the localStorage layer), `core/state.js` (the profile store + both migrations), `core/kb-boot.js` (the `/kb-v1.json` fetch, the adapter, the derived bindings, the honest failure path), `core/dom-utils.js` and `core/format.js` (stateless helpers). Each is 35–120 lines. Date/text formatting got its own file rather than being lumped into `dom-utils.js` under a name that would misdescribe it.

  b. **`core/storage.js` owns key identity and raw get/set/remove — and deliberately not parsing, defaults or logging.** Today's parse semantics are unevenly and load-bearingly different: the profiles read throws loudly on corrupt JSON, the accordion and ZPD-filter reads catch and `console.warn`, and the legacy value is parsed **only** when `profiles.length === 0`. Typed accessors that returned parsed values would relocate or flatten those differences — a behaviour change this packet is not allowed to make. So the eleven accessors are one-liners and every `JSON.parse` / `try` / `console` stays at the call site it already occupied. The module owns the seven keys the app script writes; the shell's other two (`ga_debug` in the `<head>` gtag shim, `iosInstallDismissed` in the install-prompt IIFE) belong to blocks that stay inline this packet, and the module says so.

  c. **Cross-module state travels as `export let` live bindings.** ES module imports are live views of the exporting binding, so `DATA` / `windowsBySkillId` / `MIN_MONTH` / `MAX_MONTH` / `CATEGORIES` / `profiles` / `currentProfileId` are exported as `let`, assigned **only** inside the owning module, and read byte-unchanged at ~50 call sites. Getter functions would have churned every one of those lines for no semantic gain. The consequence is a rule, not a convenience: an assignment to one of these names may only appear in its owning module (`initData`, `initProfileStore`, `setCurrentProfile`).

  d. **The test seam is the entry module's export surface, reached by `import('/m/v1/app.js')` — not a `window.*` namespace.** `app/m/v1/app.js` exports exactly seven names (`DATA`, `saveCompletedSkills`, and the five modal openers/closers) under a header comment declaring them a parity-gate seam and not a contract. No shipped code imports the entry, so the surface is reachable only by same-origin code that deliberately imports that URL; nothing is added to the global object, which is the property the split exists to buy. `import()` of an already-loaded URL returns the **same** module instance the page booted (the module map is keyed by resolved URL per document), so this is a handle on the live app rather than a second copy of it. The seam also **shrank**: `visual.spec.js` reached `profiles` / `currentProfileId` / `saveProfiles` to complete every skill and now goes through `saveCompletedSkills`, the app's own honest persist path.

  e. **A test-only hook was rejected on principle.** Gating the seam on a query flag or a build variant would make the tested build differ from the shipped build — forfeiting the one property the parity gate exists to provide. The seam ships because the gate must exercise the bytes that ship.

  f. **`CACHE_VERSION` stays `v9`, and `m/v1/` is extended in place.** Both were minted on this unmerged branch and have never been published, so no client holds either, and ADR-024's never-overwrite rule (which binds from first publication) is not engaged. `v8`→`v9` is the transition that will actually ship at A1 merge and it already carries the whole mount; a bump to `v10` here would mint a cache generation no client ever holds and would make the ledger claim a delivery event that never happened. The changed `sw.js` bytes still force a waiting worker on any dev client, which re-runs `addAll` against the same cache name and picks the new entries up.

  g. **The ship-list guard learns the module import graph — a third direction.** The shell now references exactly one JS entry; every `core/` module is reachable only through an `import` statement, which no HTML attribute mentions. Directions (1) and (2) from `A1-DL-004` would therefore stay green while a core module was missing from the `COPY` list or from `OFFLINE_URLS`, and it would 404 in production or on the first offline boot. The walker resolves static specifiers relative to their importer and requires every transitive dependency to be shipped **and** precached; it fails **closed** like the `COPY` parser — dynamic `import()`, an indented module statement, or a bare specifier throws rather than being skipped. Invariant `A1-P4-INV-001`.

  h. **Two multi-line template literals are NOT dedented.** The rest of the moved code is a byte-exact slice dedented one 8-space level (the `A1-DL-004` method), but the contents of the `#skillModalBody` and activity-card templates are string data that lands in the DOM verbatim: dedenting them changed the rendered markup. The DOM baselines caught it on the first run — the gate doing exactly its job — and the two ranges now move byte-untouched, at the cost of looking over-indented in `app.js`.
- **Rationale.** `A1-DL-004` paid down delivery risk with two provably inert carriers and left the stateful script inline on purpose, so that JS-scope risk would land in a packet of its own where a red gate is attributable. This is that packet. The core boundary is drawn where state and I/O actually live (storage, profile state, KB boot) rather than by line count, because the boundary's value is that a later packet can move a feature surface without re-deciding who owns `localStorage` or who may assign `DATA`.
- **No dedicated ADR by design at the repo layer.** The packet realizes vault **ADR-037** (the hybrid split) and operates inside the mount contract decided in `A1-DL-004`. The seam decision is a repo-level design call with no vault counterpart.
- **Alternatives considered.**
  1. **A frozen `window.__theygrowParity = {…}` namespace.** Rejected — it reintroduces the global surface the split exists to remove, and a global is trippable by any script on the page, whereas a module export requires a deliberate import of a specific URL. Equal in reach, worse in blast radius.
  2. **A test-only seam gated on `?parity=1` or a build variant.** Rejected — see (e); it breaks the identity between tested bytes and shipped bytes.
  3. **Drop the seam and drive everything through the UI.** Rejected — the 174-modal-body hash capture would have to open 174 modals by click, changing GA emission and modal-history state mid-capture, and the existing baselines could not be reused (baseline writes are the one thing this milestone must not do casually).
  4. **Split the feature code into per-surface modules in the same packet.** Rejected — `A1-P5` scope; it would put the core boundary and ~8 new module boundaries in one unattributable diff.
  5. **Typed accessors returning parsed values in `core/storage.js`.** Rejected — see (b).
  6. **Getter functions instead of `export let` live bindings.** Rejected — see (c).
  7. **`<link rel="modulepreload">` for the core graph.** Rejected here — it claws back the deferred-module round-trip but is a shell delivery change beyond this packet's declared shape, and it should be decided once the graph is final (P5/P6).
  8. **A `CACHE_VERSION` bump to `v10`.** Rejected — see (f).
- **Supersedes.** None. Builds on `A1-DL-004` (the mount and the ship-list guard it extends), `A1-DL-002` (the gate it is measured by) and `A1-DL-001` / `A0-DL-001` (the behaviour it freezes).
- **Effects.**
  - **Added (shipped):** `app/m/v1/app.js` (entry, 1,278 lines incl. the seam), `app/m/v1/core/{storage,state,kb-boot,dom-utils,format}.js` (35–120 lines each).
  - **Changed (shipped):** `app/index.html` (the inline `<script>` at `:204`–`:1685` replaced by a comment + one `<script type="module" src="/m/v1/app.js">`; every other byte verified identical — 1,818 → 339 lines), `app/sw.js` (six `OFFLINE_URLS` entries; `CACHE_VERSION` untouched).
  - **Changed (dev/CI):** `app/tests/delivery-contract.spec.js` (the import-graph direction), `app/tests/support/digest.js`, `app/tests/dom-parity.spec.js`, `app/tests/visual.spec.js`, `app/tests/behavior.spec.js` (seam call sites); **added** `app/tests/support/app-module.js`.
  - **Untouched, verified byte-identical:** `app/nginx.conf`, `app/Dockerfile` (`COPY m/` already ships the mount root — the property `A1-DL-004` bought), `app/kb-v1.json`, `app/manifest.json`, `app/offline.html`, `app/m/v1/app.css`, `app/m/v1/sw-register.js`, `app/cloudbuild.yaml`, `.pre-commit-config.yaml` (the exclude is the version-agnostic `m/` prefix), `app/tests/__baselines__/` (**zero baseline writes** — the acceptance criterion), `.github/workflows/ci.yml`.
  - **Strict mode is a real semantic delta, audited rather than assumed.** Module code is always strict; the inline script was sloppy. The moved code has no `var`, no octal literals, no `with`, no `arguments.callee`, no duplicate parameters and no implicit globals (every assignment target is declared), and its single `this` is inside an `addEventListener` callback, where the receiver is bound by the caller. The suite's `pageerror` / console-error guard is the dynamic backstop.
  - **Operability: none** (ADR-013 / contract §4.7). No `theygrow_api.parameters` knob (the typed-config home is `/api`, outside this front-only perimeter — the `VDK-DL-001` / `A1-DL-004` precedent), no `theygrow_api.signals` taxonomy signal, no new GA4 event, **no change to production emission**: all 14 `trackEvent` call sites moved into `app.js` semantics-identical — same names, same parameter objects, same triggers, same ordering relative to the surrounding logic (notably the two pre-`openSkillModal` emissions, `graph_navigate` and `activity_skill_open`, whose order is load-bearing because `openSkillModal` overwrites the source `h2`). `trackEvent` itself stays the inline `<head>` shim and resolves from module code as a global function property, which the inline `onclick` at `index.html:93` still requires.
  - **§4-safe.** No new network egress: six same-origin static files on the existing delivery channel, no third-party origin added or contacted. No personal or family data touched — the localStorage keys, value shapes and both migrations move verbatim — and no telemetry surface changes.
  - **A second boot-order cost, recorded rather than worked around.** `A1-DL-004` noted that `type="module"` is deferred and put a same-origin round-trip on the `DOMContentLoaded` path. This packet moves the `kbReady` fetch behind that same deferral: it used to start during HTML parse and now starts at module evaluation. The 630 KB `kb-v1.json` fetch still dominates, and the import graph is deliberately flat (entry → `core/*`, no barrel) so the five core files fetch in parallel rather than in a chain. `DOMContentLoaded` still fires *after* deferred modules execute, so the bootstrap listener is registered in time. Not zero; not claimed to be.
  - **The install-prompt IIFE now runs before the app code** — it is a classic parse-time script and the entry is deferred. Verified inert: it touches only `trackEvent` and DOM nodes above it, and reads no app state. It stays inline until A1-P5.
  - **The gate caught a real regression.** The first extraction dedented the two multi-line template literals along with everything else, changing the whitespace inside `#skillModalBody` and the activity cards. Three DOM baselines went red immediately and named the surface; see (h). This is recorded because it is the packet's best evidence that the threshold is worth its cost.
  - **Seam-honesty caveat** (M4-CLOSE discipline). The suite proves refactor-equivalence in one pinned headless Chromium against a mirror of `nginx.conf`. It does not prove production `Content-Type`, real-device rendering, other engines, or on-device PWA behaviour — those stay owner on-device steps under the ADR-020 gate, which is why the packet also validated the **built container** directly: all six new paths curl `200` with `public, immutable, max-age=2592000` and a JavaScript MIME, and a headless load of the real image built the full 174-row table, computed `--skill-col-width` from real font metrics, reached `activated` on `theygrow-v9` with all eight mount paths precached, exposed exactly the seven seam names, and logged zero console errors. The promotion-smoke curl in `docs/RUNBOOK.md` remains the only place the *production* `Content-Type` is ever observed.

---

## A1-DL-006 — spa-split (A1) P5: one module per surface over a shared core, an acyclic graph by construction, and a shell left byte-untouched

- **Date.** 2026-07-27
- **Type.** Decision record — the packet's code and this entry land together. Third and final extraction packet of A1; pure refactor, zero product change.
- **Decision.** Cut the 1,278-line entry module into per-surface modules, leaving `app/m/v1/app.js` as wiring and bootstrap only, and decide the two remaining shell questions `A1-DL-005` left open (the install-prompt IIFE, `modulepreload`) — both, on the evidence, in favour of not touching `app/index.html` at all.

  a. **The cut follows the call graph, not the surface list.** Eight modules under a new `app/m/v1/surfaces/` — `table.js`, `skill-completion.js`, `zpd-filter.js`, `skill-modal.js`, `profile.js`, `activities.js`, `onboarding.js`, `accordion.js` (43–205 lines each) — plus two new `core/` residents. The sketch's single "table/ZPD" surface was ~380 lines and mixed DOM construction with DOM mutation, so it split three ways; the mobile accordion and the Tier-1 urgency arithmetic are separate concerns the sketch did not name. Every file is under the ~300-line target with no exception.

  b. **`core/` gains what more than one surface reads, and nothing else.** `core/zpd.js` holds the readiness predicate (read by the table, the skill modal, the filter and the activities modal) and the ZPD-filter flag; `core/urgency.js` holds the pure window arithmetic behind the activities ranking. The flag went to `core/` rather than staying with the button that toggles it because the mobile accordion reads it too — leaving it in `surfaces/zpd-filter.js` would have made the filter and the accordion import each other, since `applyFilter()` synchronises the accordion last.

  c. **The graph is a DAG, and that is a decision, not an accident.** The single backward call — `switchProfile()` rebuilding the table — is inverted by passing the rebuild into `initProfiles()`, so `surfaces/profile.js` never imports `surfaces/table.js`. An ES-module cycle would in fact have evaluated correctly here (function declarations hoist; nothing is called at module-evaluation time), and the ship-list walker has a `seen` set so it would not even have hung — the cycle was rejected because its correctness is an argument every later packet would have to re-derive, not because it would have broken.

  d. **`init()` keeps its exact statement order.** Each surface exposes a `wireX()` holding its own listener block at the indentation it already had, and the entry calls the five of them in the positions the single-file `init()` used. Listener registration order relative to `buildTableBody()` is behaviour, and reordering it is exactly the invisible change this milestone's gate is least able to catch.

  e. **Nothing is re-indented.** Every moved function was top-level and stays top-level; every moved wiring block keeps its 4-space indent inside its `wireX()`. The `A1-DL-005` (h) hazard — dedenting a template literal whose content lands in the DOM verbatim — cannot recur this packet, because no line changes column. The whole diff's semantic delta is 25 `export ` prefixes and five changed lines, enumerated in Effects.

  f. **The install-prompt IIFE stays inline — the equivalence could not be proven, so it was not claimed.** `A1-DL-005` deferred it to "A1-P5" and verified only that it is inert with respect to app state. The load-bearing question is different: it is a classic parse-time script, and as a deferred module its `beforeinstallprompt` listener would register later. That event is never fired by headless Chromium, so no level of the parity suite can observe the property at issue. The likely argument — that the event cannot fire before service-worker registration on `window.load` anyway — is probably right, which is not the same as proven, and the failure mode is a silently missing install prompt in production with a green gate. Left inline; `core/storage.js` still records `iosInstallDismissed` as a key it does not own.

  g. **`modulepreload` is deferred to P6, and the JS graph is declared final as of this packet.** `A1-DL-005` alt #7 deferred the hints until "the graph is final" — a condition met only at the *end* of this packet, so hints authored inside it would describe a graph that was not yet final. Two further reasons found while planning: the hints are `href=` attributes, so `htmlAssetRefs()` would promote every preloaded module from `MODULE_DEPS` to `SHELL_REFS` and quietly change which direction of the guard covers them; and they would falsify `A1-P4-INV-001`'s "the shell references exactly one JS entry". P6 gets a diff whose only content is delivery hints plus that guard question.

  h. **`CACHE_VERSION` stays `v9` and `m/v1/` is extended in place**, on the `A1-DL-005` (f) reasoning unchanged: both were minted on this unmerged branch and have never been published, so ADR-024's never-overwrite rule is not engaged. Ten `OFFLINE_URLS` entries are added; the changed `sw.js` bytes still force a waiting worker on any dev client, which re-runs `addAll` against the same cache name.
- **Rationale.** `A1-DL-005` rejected doing the core boundary and the surface boundaries together because it "would put the core boundary and ~8 new module boundaries in one unattributable diff". With `core/` already drawn and the seam already a module export, the surface cut had almost no design left in it — which is why the packet's real decisions turned out to be the two shell questions, and why both resolved to *not editing the shell*. The result is that the milestone's largest restructuring is also the one packet with an empty `app/index.html` diff.
- **No dedicated ADR by design at the repo layer.** The packet realizes vault **ADR-037** (the hybrid split: feature modules over a thin `core/`, ~300-line target) and operates inside the mount contract decided in `A1-DL-004`. ADR-037 pins the direction, not the surface list; the cut is a repo-level design call.
- **Alternatives considered.**
  1. **Four modules, following the P3 sequence sketch literally.** Rejected — the "table/ZPD" file lands ~380 lines, over the ADR-037 target, and buries the mobile accordion inside the table surface.
  2. **Allow the `table ↔ profile` ES-module cycle.** Rejected — see (c). Correct, but only for reasons that are not visible at the import site.
  3. **Thread the ZPD-filter flag through function parameters instead of a `core/` live binding.** Rejected — it churns four call-site signatures to avoid a pattern `A1-DL-005` (c) already blessed.
  4. **Redistribute `init()` into per-surface `initX()` that each do state and wiring.** Rejected — see (d); it reorders listener registration relative to the first table build.
  5. **Move the install-prompt IIFE into a module.** Rejected — see (f).
  6. **Land `modulepreload` here.** Rejected — see (g).
  7. **A committed script for the moved-code multiset check.** Rejected — the check is a one-off review aid, not a gate; committing it would add machinery outside the packet's declared shape. Run as a command, with its output enumerated in the execution report.
- **Supersedes.** None. Discharges the "per-surface modules are P5" obligation recorded in `A1-DL-005` alt #4 and `docs/execution-map.md`. Builds on `A1-DL-005` (the `core/` boundary and the seam it re-wires), `A1-DL-004` (the mount and the ship-list guard) and `A1-DL-002` (the gate it is measured by).
- **Effects.**
  - **Added (shipped):** `app/m/v1/surfaces/{table,skill-completion,zpd-filter,skill-modal,profile,activities,onboarding,accordion}.js` (43–205 lines each), `app/m/v1/core/{zpd,urgency}.js` (36 and 43 lines).
  - **Changed (shipped):** `app/m/v1/app.js` (1,278 → 92 lines: imports, `init()`, the `DOMContentLoaded` bootstrap byte-unchanged, and the seam), `app/m/v1/core/storage.js` (the key-ownership comment, which pointed at this packet), `app/sw.js` (ten `OFFLINE_URLS` entries; `CACHE_VERSION` untouched).
  - **Changed (dev/CI):** none. **`app/tests/` is byte-identical** — the seam kept the same URL and the same seven names, so no spec and no helper moved.
  - **Untouched, verified byte-identical:** `app/index.html` (**the whole packet, and the strongest single claim in it**), `app/tests/**` including `app/tests/__baselines__/` (**zero baseline writes** — the acceptance criterion), `app/nginx.conf`, `app/Dockerfile` (`COPY m/` already ships the new subdirectory — the property `A1-DL-004` bought, now collected a second time), `app/kb-v1.json`, `app/manifest.json`, `app/offline.html`, `app/cloudbuild.yaml`, `app/m/v1/app.css`, `app/m/v1/sw-register.js`, the four other `core/` modules, `app/playwright.config.js`, `.pre-commit-config.yaml` (the exclude is the version-agnostic `m/` prefix), `.github/workflows/ci.yml`.
  - **The semantic delta is five lines, and they are named.** `isZpdFilterActive = JSON.parse(…)` and `isZpdFilterActive = !isZpdFilterActive` become `setZpdFilterActive(…)` (the `A1-DL-005` (c) owner-only-assignment rule); `initProfiles()` gains a `rebuildTable` parameter and stores it; `switchProfile()`'s `buildTableBody()` becomes `onProfileSwitched()`. Everything else moved byte-identical, verified by a sorted-line multiset diff whose residual is entirely additive — import headers, seven wrapper functions and their closing braces, and the thirteen `init()` call lines.
  - **No `docs/INVARIANTS.md` entry.** The ship-list guard covers the ten new files with no code change, so this packet enforces nothing new — empty by the `AGENTS.md` §10 rule, not by omission. `A1-P4-INV-001`'s Statement gained one clause naming `surfaces/`, because the diff made "every `core/` module is reachable only through an `import` statement" incomplete rather than wrong.
  - **Operability: none** (ADR-013 / contract §4.7). No `theygrow_api.parameters` knob (the typed-config home is `/api`, outside this front-only perimeter — the `VDK-DL-001` / `A1-DL-004` / `A1-DL-005` precedent), no `theygrow_api.signals` taxonomy signal, no new GA4 event, **no change to production emission**: all 14 `trackEvent` call sites redistribute across the surface modules with identical names, parameter objects and triggers, and both load-bearing pre-`openSkillModal` emissions still fire before the source `h2` is overwritten — `graph_navigate` in `surfaces/skill-modal.js`, `activity_skill_open` in `surfaces/activities.js`. `trackEvent` remains the inline `<head>` shim resolved as a global, which the inline `onclick` at `index.html:93` still requires.
  - **§4-safe.** No new network egress: ten same-origin static files on the existing delivery channel, no third-party origin added or contacted. No personal or family data touched — no localStorage key, value shape or migration moves.
  - **A third boot-order cost, recorded rather than worked around.** `A1-DL-004` put a same-origin round trip on the `DOMContentLoaded` path; `A1-DL-005` moved the `kbReady` fetch behind that same deferral and kept the graph flat so `core/*` fetched in parallel. That flatness is now gone: `core/*` sits one level deeper (entry → `surfaces/*` → `core/*`), costing one further round trip on a cold first visit. It is paid once — the service worker precaches all eighteen mount paths, so every subsequent boot is cache-first — and `modulepreload` in P6 is the intended discharge. Not zero; not claimed to be.
  - **Seam-honesty caveat** (M4-CLOSE discipline). The suite proves refactor-equivalence in one pinned headless Chromium against a mirror of `nginx.conf`. It does not prove production `Content-Type`, real-device rendering, other engines, or on-device PWA behaviour — and, specific to this packet, **it cannot observe `beforeinstallprompt` at all**, which is precisely why (f) went the way it did. The packet additionally validated the **built container** directly: all ten new paths curl `200` with `public, immutable, max-age=2592000` and a JavaScript MIME, and a headless load of the real image built the full 174-row table, computed `--skill-col-width` from real font metrics, reached `activated` on `theygrow-v9` with all eighteen mount paths precached, exposed exactly the seven seam names, and logged zero console errors.
  - **Carried, not fixed (the `A1-DL-004` posture):** `AGENTS.md` §7 and the comment in `scripts/check-contract-integrity.sh` still do not list `m/` — contract-file edits stay out of split packets by owner direction, so both remain A1-CLOSE items. Also still carried: the delivery drift guard cannot see `http`-level nginx directives; `app/.dockerignore` is not consulted by the ship-list guard; and `app/package.json`'s `test:dom` script names a project that does not exist.

---

## A1-DL-007 — spa-split (A1) P6: modulepreload hints for the final graph, and the ship-list guard re-rooted at what the shell executes

- **Date.** 2026-07-27
- **Type.** Decision record — the packet's code and this entry land together. Delivery-hints packet, not an extraction packet: no module is added, moved or edited; the JS graph declared final in `A1-DL-006` (g) is byte-untouched.
- **Decision.** Discharge the boot-order cost `A1-DL-004`, `A1-DL-005` and `A1-DL-006` each recorded and declined to pay, and settle the guard question the hints raise — deliberately, rather than by whichever direction happened to stay green:

  a. **Fifteen hints: the non-entry modules, and only those.** `app/index.html` gains one `<link rel="modulepreload">` per module of the `/m/v1/` graph that the shell does not already name in a `src=` attribute — the seven `core/` and eight `surfaces/` files, in `OFFLINE_URLS` order so the two lists diff by eye. The two entries (`app.js`, `sw-register.js`) are **not** hinted: the preload scanner already finds their `src=` while parsing, so a hint buys nothing, and hinting one is now a **failing** test — the empirical proof is in Validation, where adding a hint for `sw-register.js` reds direction (4) by name. Excluding them also makes the hint list exactly the walked graph, which is what lets `A1-P6-INV-001` be a set equality instead of a hand-maintained exception list. `app.css` is not hinted either: it is a stylesheet the `<head>` already links, and `modulepreload` is not the right `rel` for it.

  b. **The hints sit after the stylesheet link, and carry no `crossorigin`.** After, because the stylesheet is render-blocking and must not be pushed behind fifteen script fetches. No `crossorigin`, because module scripts are fetched in CORS mode and these are same-origin, so the hint's credentials mode matches the later module request — a mismatch would double-fetch every module. That is asserted, not assumed: the measurement below counts **17 module requests, 17 distinct, zero duplicates**, before and after.

  c. **The waterfall was measured against the real built container, and the recorded cost was worse than recorded.** Two images built from `app/Dockerfile` (pre- and post-edit), a cold first visit with the service worker blocked, 150 ms emulated latency, five runs, medians. **Before: four discovery waves**, last module response 822 ms, `DOMContentLoaded` 823 ms. **After: one wave**, 671 ms and 672 ms. The fourth wave is the finding: the graph is only two levels deep, so `A1-DL-006` predicted three: `core/urgency.js` — imported by `surfaces/activities.js`, the ninth of ten modules in wave 2 — arrived a full round trip after the other level-2 modules, because HTTP/1.1 serves at most six concurrent connections per origin and `activities.js` was in the second connection batch. The cost the three ledger entries recorded was a graph-depth cost; part of it was a connection-limit cost the graph does not show.

  d. **The walk is re-rooted at the shell's execution entries — the alternative would have deleted the invariant.** Hints are `href=` attributes, so `htmlAssetRefs()` sees them. Left alone, all fifteen would be promoted into `SHELL_REFS`; `MODULE_DEPS` is `moduleDependencies(...)` minus its own seeds, so seeding it from every `.js` the shell names would have left `MODULE_DEPS` **empty** — direction (3), the whole of `A1-P4-INV-001`, silently reduced to zero test cases with the suite green. `A1-DL-006` (g) predicted a change in *which* direction covers the modules; the real effect was the loss of one. Measured, not argued: seeding the old way yields 17 seeds and 0 dependencies, and the reconciled guard yields 15 (Validation). The fix distinguishes what the shell **executes** (`<script type="module" src>`, walked) from what it **hints** (`<link rel="modulepreload">`, not an evaluation root — `modulepreload` fetches and compiles, it never evaluates). Hints stay inside `SHELL_REFS`, because a hint at an unshipped path is a real defect directions (1) and (2) should catch.

  e. **A fourth direction, asserted as a set equality.** Every hint names a module in the walked graph, and every module in the walked graph is hinted. The second half is the load-bearing one: a stale hint after a mount bump is dead weight a reviewer would eventually notice, but a future module added without a hint costs a round trip that nothing in this repo measures. `A1-P6-INV-001`. The cost is accepted and stated: a later extraction must hint its new module or CI reds.

  f. **`A1-P4-INV-001`'s Statement said "exactly one JS entry", and that was already wrong before this packet.** `/m/v1/sw-register.js` has been a second `<script type="module">` since A1-P3, and the guard has always seeded the walk from both. The hints made the sentence conspicuous rather than false for the first time; the amendment names both entries instead of preserving the error. A side-find, recorded as one.

  g. **`CACHE_VERSION` stays `v9` and `m/v1/` is extended in place** — unchanged reasoning from `A1-DL-005` (f) and `A1-DL-006` (h): both were minted on this unmerged branch and publication is merge plus an ADR-020 promotion, so ADR-024's never-overwrite rule binds nothing here. This packet adds no URL at all, so `OFFLINE_URLS` and the `app/Dockerfile` `COPY` list are byte-untouched. On the one question the hints do raise — the shell change reaches installed users through the precached `/` entry — no bump is needed either: `app/sw.js` serves navigations **network-first** and re-fills the `/` key, so an online navigation delivers the new shell on its own, and an offline boot serves the previous shell, which degrades to the pre-P6 waterfall rather than breaking, because hints are only hints.
- **Rationale.** `A1-DL-005` alt #7 deferred the hints until the graph was final; `A1-DL-006` (g) declared it final and reserved P6 for "delivery hints plus that guard question". The guard question turned out to be the larger half of the packet: the hints themselves are fifteen lines whose effect a measurement settles in minutes, while the promotion they trigger would have removed an invariant's enforcement without removing the invariant. Doing them together in one diff is what makes the removal visible — a later packet adding hints would have found direction (3) already at zero and no reason to look.
- **No dedicated ADR by design at the repo layer.** The packet realizes vault **ADR-037** (the buildless split it closes out) and operates inside the mount contract decided in `A1-DL-004`. Delivery hints for a graph ADR-037 pins are a repo-level call.
- **Alternatives considered.**
  1. **Hint all eighteen mount paths.** Rejected — see (a). The entries are already parser-discovered, `app.css` is not a module, and the exception list would have to be maintained by hand forever.
  2. **Hint only the level-1 modules and rely on Chromium fetching a preloaded module's own dependencies.** Rejected — that recursion is a Chromium behaviour, not a guarantee shared by every engine that supports `modulepreload`. Fifteen hints cost ~200 bytes gzipped and are engine-independent.
  3. **Accept the `SHELL_REFS` promotion and rewrite `A1-P4-INV-001` to describe the weaker guard.** Rejected — see (d). That is "whichever direction stays green" wearing an invariant edit; the guard would have asserted nothing about imports at all.
  4. **Drop hints from `SHELL_REFS` entirely, so they are only checked by direction (4).** Rejected — a hint pointing at an unshipped or unprecached path is exactly the class of defect directions (1) and (2) exist for; narrowing them to buy a tidier partition would trade real coverage for symmetry.
  5. **Assert only that hints are a subset of the graph (no dead hints).** Rejected — see (e). It catches rot and misses omission, and omission is the invisible one.
  6. **Bump `CACHE_VERSION` to `v10`.** Rejected — see (g). It would purge a cache generation no user holds, on a branch that has published nothing.
  7. **Commit the waterfall measurement harness as a test.** Rejected — the `A1-DL-006` alt #7 posture: it is a one-off packet-time argument, and committing it would add timing machinery to a gate that is deliberately about equivalence, not performance. Run as a command, output enumerated here and in the execution report.
  8. **Fix `app/sw.js`'s now-false comment at `:25–30` in this packet.** Rejected by owner direction after being raised as a scope escalation — `app/sw.js` is outside this packet's file set, and all comment reconciliation lands together at A1-CLOSE (see Effects).
- **Supersedes.** None. Discharges the `modulepreload` obligation deferred by `A1-DL-005` alt #7 and `A1-DL-006` (g), and the boot-order cost recorded in `A1-DL-004`, `A1-DL-005` and `A1-DL-006`. Builds on `A1-DL-004` (the mount and the ship-list guard), `A1-DL-005` and `A1-DL-006` (the graph it hints) and `A1-DL-002` (the gate it is measured by).
- **Effects.**
  - **Changed (shipped):** `app/index.html` only — fifteen `<link rel="modulepreload">` lines plus a comment block in `<head>`, after the stylesheet link. No other line of the file moves.
  - **Changed (dev/CI):** `app/tests/delivery-contract.spec.js` — two new fail-closed parsers (`htmlModuleEntries`, `htmlPreloadHints`), a fail-closed classifier over every same-origin `.js` the shell names, the walk re-rooted from `SHELL_REFS.filter(.js)` to `EXEC_ENTRIES`, direction (4)'s two loops, and the header comment rewritten from three directions to four.
  - **Changed (docs):** `docs/INVARIANTS.md` (`A1-P3-INV-001` Scope; `A1-P4-INV-001` Statement / Enforced by / Landed in / Scope; new `A1-P6-INV-001`), `docs/execution-map.md`, `docs/RUNBOOK.md` (three claims this diff falsifies), this entry.
  - **Untouched, verified byte-identical:** **all of `app/m/v1/`** (the graph is final and this packet does not touch it), `app/sw.js` (**no `CACHE_VERSION` bump, no `OFFLINE_URLS` entry — this packet adds no URL**), `app/Dockerfile`, `app/nginx.conf`, `app/manifest.json`, `app/offline.html`, `app/kb-v1.json`, `app/cloudbuild.yaml`, `app/playwright.config.js`, `app/package.json`, every other spec under `app/tests/`, `app/tests/__baselines__/` (**zero baseline writes** — the acceptance criterion), `.pre-commit-config.yaml`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `AGENTS.md`.
  - **The execution-semantics claim is a proof, not an assertion.** A `modulepreload` link populates the module map; it is not a script element and creates no evaluation root. Evaluation is still triggered solely by the deferred `<script type="module" src="/m/v1/app.js">` and still proceeds in the graph's post-order, unchanged. The one observable consequence is timing: `core/kb-boot.js`'s module-evaluation `fetch('/kb-v1.json')` starts earlier in wall-clock because the graph resolves earlier — earlier, not reordered. The three-level parity suite is the empirical half, and it is green with zero baseline writes.
  - **A boot-order cost paid, and measured rather than claimed.** Four discovery waves to one; last module response 822 ms → 671 ms; `DOMContentLoaded` 823 ms → 672 ms. **The wall-clock gain is one round trip, not three, and that is stated rather than rounded up:** the container serves HTTP/1.1, so seventeen simultaneously-discovered requests still queue six at a time and the *transfer* stays about three deep even when *discovery* is flat. What the hints structurally remove is the discovery serialization; how much wall-clock that returns depends on a protocol this repo cannot observe. Measured on loopback with emulated latency, in one engine — a shape, not a production number.
  - **Operability: none** (ADR-013 / contract §4.7). No `theygrow_api.parameters` knob (the typed-config home is `/api`, outside this front-only perimeter — the `VDK-DL-001` / `A1-DL-004` / `A1-DL-005` / `A1-DL-006` precedent), no `theygrow_api.signals` taxonomy signal, no GA4 event added or retimed: the `trackEvent` shim and all fourteen call sites under `app/m/v1/surfaces/` are byte-untouched, and hints emit nothing.
  - **§4-safe.** No new network egress: fifteen hints naming same-origin files the shell already fetched on the existing delivery channel, no third-party origin added or contacted, no request that did not already happen. No personal or family data touched — no localStorage key, value shape or migration moves.
  - **Seam-honesty caveat** (M4-CLOSE discipline). The gate proves the hints changed no DOM, no pixel and no behaviour in one pinned headless Chromium. It does not prove any engine *honours* `modulepreload` — support differs across engines and none of that is observable here — and `A1-P6-INV-001` deliberately asserts set membership only, never that a hint helped. The performance claim rests entirely on the measurement above, with the caveats above.
  - **Carried, not fixed (the `A1-DL-004` posture):** three shipped comments this milestone has now falsified — `app/sw.js:25–30` ("the shell references only the entry"), `app/m/v1/app.js:16–21` (the TODO still calling `modulepreload` a P6 future) and `app/index.html:204–205` ("entry + `core/`", stale since A1-P5) — all land together at A1-CLOSE by owner direction, so comment reconciliation happens in one attributable place rather than half-consistently across packets whose file sets differ. Also still carried: `AGENTS.md` §7 and `scripts/check-contract-integrity.sh` do not list `m/`; the delivery drift guard cannot see `http`-level nginx directives; `app/.dockerignore` is not consulted by the ship-list guard; and `app/package.json`'s `test:dom` names a project that does not exist.

## A1-DL-008 — spa-split (A1) A1-CLOSE: the milestone's carried follow-ups discharged in one attributable place, and the ledger closed

- **Date.** 2026-07-27
- **Type.** Decision record — milestone close-out. Ledger, docs and comment reconciliation only: no product or behaviour change, no module added, moved or edited, no new capability, no `CACHE_VERSION` bump. The `A1-DL-007` graph and the P0–P6 ladder are frozen as shipped.
- **Decision.** Close milestone A1 `spa-split` in the repo ledger and discharge every follow-up the split packets deliberately routed here — each one fixed in this packet or re-deferred with a stated reason, none silently dropped:

  a. **The three shipped comments this milestone falsified are fixed together.** `app/sw.js` ("the shell references only the entry" — false since A1-P6, which made the shell *name* all fifteen non-entry modules in delivery hints), `app/m/v1/app.js` (the boot-order note still calling `modulepreload` a P6 future) and `app/index.html` ("entry + `core/`", stale since A1-P5 added `surfaces/`). This is `A1-DL-007` alternative 8 being executed, not re-decided: the comments waited so that comment reconciliation would land in one reviewable place rather than half-consistently across packets whose file sets differ.

  b. **Contract-file edits are permissible in this packet, and the gate is the evidence rather than the assumption.** `A1-DL-004` and `A1-DL-006` recorded the `AGENTS.md` §7 live-deploy path enumeration and the parallel comment in `scripts/check-contract-integrity.sh` as owner-directed A1-CLOSE items; this is the sanctioned place. Permission was *verified*: the gate scans nine contract + spine files for superseded stack names, the legacy pre-rename directory name and live-infra identifiers, and the added tokens match none of the three — confirmed by running `scripts/check-contract-integrity.sh` before and after the edit, and by `pre-commit run --all-files`.

  c. **That enumeration gains `kb-v1.json` as well as `m/`, in all three of its copies.** The third copy — `docs/RUNBOOK.md` — does not merely repeat the list, it *claims what the contract files enumerate*. Adding `app/kb-v1.json` there while `AGENTS.md` §7 lacked it would have replaced one false statement with another, so the two tokens move together across `AGENTS.md` §7, the `scripts/check-contract-integrity.sh` header comment and `docs/RUNBOOK.md`. The script's matching behaviour is untouched: `CONTRACT_FILES` never contained an `app/` path, so the comment documents a scope the code already had.

  d. **`AGENTS.md` §8 is corrected where this milestone falsified it, and only there.** "Static PWA at repo root (single `index.html` …)" was falsified twice over — by M2-P1 for the location and by this milestone for the shape — and it is the single claim in the contract that A1 most directly refutes. Both instances of it in §8 (the layout-block annotation and the **Current** paragraph) are corrected to shipped reality. The broader M1-era framing of §6 / §7 is *not* restructured; that stays deferred (g).

  e. **`CACHE_VERSION` stays `v9` although three shipped files change bytes.** Unchanged reasoning from `A1-DL-005` (f), `A1-DL-006` (h) and `A1-DL-007` (g): `v9` was minted on this unmerged branch and publication is merge plus an ADR-020 promotion, so no installed client holds it and ADR-024's never-overwrite rule binds nothing. This packet adds no URL, so `OFFLINE_URLS` and the `app/Dockerfile` `COPY` list are byte-untouched; the shell reaches installed users network-first without a bump; and the changed `sw.js` bytes still force a waiting worker on any dev client, which re-runs `addAll` against the same cache name. A bump would purge a cache generation nobody holds, for a diff that changes no executable byte.

  f. **The wording of a comment in this repo is load-bearing, and the constraint is recorded rather than rediscovered.** Every parser in `app/tests/delivery-contract.spec.js` is a regex over raw source and is comment-blind. `offlineUrls()` harvests **every single-quoted string** inside the `OFFLINE_URLS` array, so a comment there must contain no `'`; `moduleSpecifiers()` throws on `import(` anywhere in a module, comments included; `htmlAssetRefs()` / `htmlModuleEntries()` / `htmlPreloadHints()` read `href=` / `src=` / `<script>` / `<link rel=modulepreload>` shapes wherever they appear, including inside an HTML comment — the `<head>` comment at `app/index.html` already contains a `<script … src=…>` literal that `htmlModuleEntries()` parses, harmless only because the path equals the real entry and the result is a `Set`. The three comment rewrites were authored to those constraints and the constraints were then re-checked by grep, not assumed.

  g. **Re-deferred, with reasons — named here so none is lost:**
     1. **`core/state.js saveProfiles` (exported but called only inside its own module) and `core/format.js formatParentActivitiesText` (no caller, and none before the split).** Deleting either — or merely dropping the `export` keyword — changes shipped functional bytes under `app/m/v1/`, which this packet's acceptance constraint forbids and which is a product change by any reading. Belongs to a front packet or a mount bump. `core/format.js` already carries the honest no-caller note.
     2. **`app/.dockerignore` is not consulted by the ship-list guard**, so a future ignore pattern matching `m/` would make `COPY m/` ship nothing with the guard still green. Fixing it means teaching the guard to parse `.dockerignore` — new capability, not a close-out's work. The mitigation chosen in A1-P3 stands and is documented in three places (`A1-P3-INV-001` Scope, `docs/RUNBOOK.md`, and the `.dockerignore` file's own "never add a pattern matching `m/`").
     3. **The delivery drift guard cannot see `http`-level nginx directives** (`A1-DL-004` alternative 6). Same class: new guard capability.
     4. **`app/package.json`'s scripts bypass the pinned container entirely** — `npm run test:visual` on the host will legitimately mismatch every visual baseline. The bad project name in `test:dom` is fixed here because it named something that does not exist; re-routing the script surface through `scripts/parity-suite.sh` is a design change and is not.
     5. **The broader `AGENTS.md` M1-era refresh** (§6 / §7 still framed around M1 as the live milestone). Falsified by M2 onward, not by A1; a contract-refresh packet, not this one.

  h. **No new invariant, and no A1 invariant amendment.** `AGENTS.md` §10 makes `docs/INVARIANTS.md` enforced-only and a close-out enforces nothing new; A1-P6 already reconciled `A1-P3-INV-001`, `A1-P4-INV-001` and added `A1-P6-INV-001`, and re-reading all three against the shipped tree found no residual falsehood. The one `INVARIANTS.md` edit is a Scope honesty fix to `M1-P3-INV-001`, which pointed at a `.gitleaks.toml` allowlist that has never existed; enforcement is unchanged.
- **Rationale.** The split packets bought their attributability by *not* fixing things: each one kept its diff to a single change shape so a red gate would name the cause, and pushed everything else onto a list. That discipline only pays if the list is actually paid off somewhere, in full, in one place — otherwise "carried to A1-CLOSE" is a synonym for "dropped". This packet is that payment, and its main design constraint is honesty about the boundary: what this milestone falsified is fixed, what an earlier milestone falsified is named and deferred, and the one exception (the `kb-v1.json` token) exists because leaving it out would have made the sentence being edited false in a new way.
- **No dedicated ADR by design at the repo layer.** The packet closes the repo-side execution of vault **ADR-037** and decides nothing the vault owns. Ledger shape, deferral disposition and the comment-reconciliation venue are repo-level calls.
- **Alternatives considered.**
  1. **Close with no decision-log entry** (a legitimate shape for a decision-free close). Rejected — the close carries at least four real judgements ((b), (c), (e), (g)), and an unrecorded judgement is the thing this log exists to prevent.
  2. **Re-defer the contract-file edits a fourth time.** Rejected — they have no other home, and a deferral with no destination is a permanent divergence between the contract and the shipped tree.
  3. **Bump `CACHE_VERSION` to `v10` because shipped bytes changed.** Rejected — see (e). The rule is about *published* URLs, and nothing here has been published.
  4. **Fix the dead exports while the files are open.** Rejected — see (g)(1). "While we are in there" is exactly how a comment-only packet acquires an unattributable behaviour change.
  5. **Restructure `AGENTS.md` §6 / §7 out of their M1 framing.** Rejected — see (g)(5). Correcting a false statement is reconciliation; rewriting the section around a new milestone map is a contract packet with its own review.
  6. **Rewrite the three comments in their owning packets instead** (P4 / P5 / P6). Rejected at the time by owner direction (`A1-DL-007` alternative 8) and not reopened: the file sets differed, so the result would have been three partial reconciliations and no single place to check.
- **Supersedes.** None. Discharges the follow-ups recorded as carried by `A1-DL-004`, `A1-DL-005`, `A1-DL-006` and `A1-DL-007`, and closes the milestone opened by `A1-DL-001`. Builds on `A1-DL-002` (the gate that measures it), `A1-DL-004` (the mount contract), `A1-DL-007` (the guard whose parsers constrain the comment wording) and `PWA-DL-001` (the delivery path it declines to bump).
- **Effects.**
  - **Changed (shipped, comment-only):** `app/sw.js` (the `OFFLINE_URLS` header comment), `app/m/v1/app.js` (the BOOT ORDER note), `app/index.html` (the app-entry comment). No executable byte moves in any of the three: verified per file by a `git diff -U0` that contains only comment lines, and positively by stripping comment lines from before and after and diffing the residue to empty.
  - **Changed (contract + spine):** `AGENTS.md` (§7 path list; §8 layout annotation + **Current** paragraph), `scripts/check-contract-integrity.sh` (header comment only — no pattern, no `CONTRACT_FILES` entry, no control flow), `docs/RUNBOOK.md` (the guardrail path list), `docs/product/TechSpec.md` (the `/app` monorepo bullet), `docs/INVARIANTS.md` (`M1-P3-INV-001` Scope), `docs/execution-map.md` (P6 → Done @ beb16f5; the A1 block moved out of the milestone ladder into **Current state** as a closed track; the `A1-CLOSE` bullet), this entry.
  - **Changed (dev/CI):** `app/package.json` (`test:dom` repointed at the two real Playwright projects; the `@playwright/test` pin is untouched — `scripts/parity-suite.sh` parses that exact literal and refuses to run on a mismatch).
  - **Changed (dev-only comments):** `docker-compose.yml`, `scripts/dev/init-pgvector.sql` — "lands in M3" restated as landed, with the Alembic migrations named as the actual schema owner. No service, image, environment variable or SQL statement changes.
  - **Untouched, verified byte-identical:** all of `app/m/v1/` except the `app.js` comment block, **all of `app/tests/`** including `app/tests/__baselines__/` (**zero baseline writes** — the acceptance criterion), `app/Dockerfile`, `app/nginx.conf`, `app/manifest.json`, `app/offline.html`, `app/kb-v1.json`, `app/cloudbuild.yaml`, `app/playwright.config.js`, `app/icons/`, `.pre-commit-config.yaml`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `CLAUDE.md`.
  - **No new **Current** track is set.** The ladder returns to M1–M5 and the next-track choice remains the standing open owner decision the map already records at the M5 line. Inventing a successor here would be a planning decision this packet has no authority to make.
  - **Operability: none** (ADR-013 / contract §4.7). No `theygrow_api.parameters` knob (the typed-config home is `/api`, outside this front-only perimeter — the `VDK-DL-001` / `A1-DL-004`…`A1-DL-007` precedent), no `theygrow_api.signals` taxonomy entry, no GA4 event added, removed or retimed: the `trackEvent` shim and all fourteen call sites under `app/m/v1/surfaces/` are byte-untouched. Comments emit nothing.
  - **§4-safe.** No new network egress, no origin added or contacted, no request that did not already happen. No personal or family data touched: no localStorage key, value shape or migration moves, and no diagnostic artifact is produced.
  - **Seam-honesty caveat** (M4-CLOSE discipline). The parity suite proves this packet changed no DOM, no pixel and no behaviour in one pinned headless Chromium, and the image check proves the changed comment bytes reach the container. Neither proves anything about the *content* of the reconciled prose — that a statement now matches reality is a reviewer's judgement, and the owner's plan-review against the vault is where spec conformance is checked, not CI.
