## L2-DL-001 — L2 staging contour (= vault ADR-020 as amended): dedicated staging `/api` service, same-instance isolated database, production `/api` promotion gate

- **Date.** 2026-07-27
- **Type.** Deploy-topology + docs decision — A2-P1, the first packet of the A2 `l2-staging` milestone. Build-config + documentation only: no product code, no schema, no migration, no `/api` route, nothing under `app/`. *(Namespace note: this log's "Entry format" header describes ids as `M{N}-DL-{NNN}`; `L2-DL-001` opens a dedicated namespace for the L2 staging contour, sibling to `L1-DL-001` (the deploy-safety promotion gate) and deliberately **not** number-pinned to the vault ADR it cross-references. Fresh per-namespace sequence (`001`); the format header is intentionally left unedited, mirroring `L1-DL-001` / `PWA-DL-001` / `B1-DL-018`.)*
- **Decision.** Stand up the L2 staging contour for `/api` and give production `/api` a promotion gate:
  1. **A dedicated staging Cloud Run service**, deployed by a new, self-contained `api/cloudbuild.staging.yaml` on its own trigger. It builds the same `api/Dockerfile` and context as production but pushes a **distinct image name** (`api-staging`) into the **same** Artifact Registry repository — a second push to production's `api:$COMMIT_SHA` tag would rewrite the tag production's own build-config points at.
  2. **Staging takes traffic; production gets the gate.** The staging deploy carries no `--no-traffic`/`--tag`: staging *is* the smoke contour, so a zero-traffic staging revision would be a smoke target for a smoke target. Production `api/cloudbuild.yaml` gains `--no-traffic --tag sha-$SHORT_SHA` — the L1 shape, verbatim — so deploy stops meaning release for the backend as it already had for the PWA.
  3. **Staging is private and least-privileged.** `--no-allow-unauthenticated` (a pre-production contour never needs anonymous access, and it is the posture the A3 authenticated spine will want), a dedicated `--service-account`, and `--max-instances 2`.
  4. **Live identifiers stay out of the repository.** The Cloud SQL instance connection name, the `DATABASE_URL` secret name and the runtime service-account address arrive as **trigger substitutions** (`_CLOUDSQL_INSTANCE`, `_DB_SECRET_NAME`, `_STAGING_RUNTIME_SA`) declared with empty defaults. Cloud Build's default `MUST_MATCH` substitution option is kept, and a **step-0 guard** fails the build with a named message when any is empty.
  5. **Isolation is a documented, verified owner procedure, not an assumption.** `docs/RUNBOOK.md` gains a ten-step **Staging contour** stop-gate handoff, whose load-bearing steps are: pre-create the `vector` extension as the superuser; create the staging role with SQL rather than `gcloud sql users create`; revoke `PUBLIC`'s implicit `CONNECT` on the production database; and **verify the refusal by observation**.
  6. **`docs/RUNBOOK.md` gains a separate `/api` promotion procedure**, deliberately not a copy of the PWA's.
- **Rationale.** `L1-DL-001` rejected a standalone staging service on reasoning that was correct for what it governed: for a single-file static PWA, a per-revision `--no-traffic --tag` already yields an addressable zero-traffic revision of the same service, and a second service would duplicate infrastructure for no isolation gain. That reasoning does not survive contact with a database-backed backend. A zero-traffic revision of the production `/api` service still points at the **production database**, so there is nowhere to exercise a migration, a re-derivation or a retrieval change without doing it against real family data — and A2's whole purpose is to have that somewhere **before** the authenticated spine (PDR-010) puts `/api` on the critical path. Hence the vault amendment this entry is the repo-side definition site for: L1 (the promotion gate) stands unchanged and now covers `/api` too; L2 adds the contour L1 explicitly declined.

  The same-instance database (ADR-020's accepted default) is a cost decision that buys a real risk, and the mitigations are placed where each actually binds. Database-level: `PUBLIC` holds `CONNECT` on every PostgreSQL database by default, so without an explicit revoke the "staging has no grant on production" precondition is satisfied only on paper — and a role created the convenient way (`gcloud sql users create`) is granted `cloudsqlsuperuser`, which can `SET ROLE` its way around the barrier entirely. IAM-level: without `--service-account` the service would run as the default compute account, which on older projects carries project Editor and would undo at the IAM layer what the SQL grants build. Resource-level: staging and production share one instance's `max_connections`, so `--max-instances 2` paired with a role `CONNECTION LIMIT` is production-safety, not tidiness.

  The step-0 guard exists because the failure it prevents is **silent**, not noisy: an empty `--add-cloudsql-instances` parses to an empty list, so the deploy succeeds with no database attachment and `/api/health` stays green — a misconfiguration that would surface a packet or two later as a mystery. The `/api` promotion procedure is written fresh rather than copied because three of the PWA section's properties do not transfer: a stale `/api` revision has no visible signal (one static JSON body looks the same at any age), `--to-latest` returns the service to latest-traffic mode and can be defeated by a deploy landing between smoke and promotion, and the `/api` smoke is honestly one assertion because `GET /api/health` is the entire deployed surface.
- **Alternatives considered.**
  1. **Keep ADR-020's original answer — no staging service, gate the production service with `--no-traffic` alone.** Rejected on the grounds above: it isolates the *revision* and not the *data*, which is the only isolation a database-backed contour needs.
  2. **A separate Cloud SQL instance for staging.** Stronger isolation and rejected only on the owner-accepted ADR-020 cost default. The residual risk is recorded rather than dissolved: the shared instance couples backup/restore blast radius and billed disk floor, and both are now written into **Restore / disaster recovery**.
  3. **A second Artifact Registry repository for staging images.** Rejected — one more owner creation step and one more identifier to carry, for isolation that a distinct image *name* already provides.
  4. **Defer the Cloud SQL / secret wiring to A2-P2, where something first connects.** Genuinely tempting: nothing in this packet exercises the wiring, so it can be silently wrong for a whole packet. Rejected — it is owner-approved P1 scope, and deferring would narrow the packet rather than reduce the risk. The honest mitigation is documentation plus two positive owner checks (the observed connection refusal, the successful migration), not removal.
  5. **Hardcode the Cloud SQL instance connection name in the build-config.** Rejected — this repository is temporarily public, and `M1-P3-INV-002` confines live identifiers to `docs/RUNBOOK.md`, where the owner has chosen to keep them as placeholders. Substitutions keep the identifier in the trigger, which is also the more discoverable operational home.
  6. **`options: substitution_option: ALLOW_LOOSE`.** Rejected — it would mask a trigger-key typo by silently resolving it to empty, which is precisely the failure the step-0 guard exists to make loud. `MUST_MATCH` additionally fails when a trigger supplies a key the config does not declare: a free extra guard.
  7. **Pin the secret version instead of `:latest`.** More conservative and rejected on rot: every rotation would become a build-config edit plus a redeploy. The accepted cost is recorded in RUNBOOK — a rotation changes what new instances receive with no redeploy, so one revision can hold two credentials. Synthetic-only data bounds the blast radius.
  8. **Give staging `--allow-unauthenticated` for parity with production.** Rejected — a pre-production contour has no anonymous caller. The cost is carried explicitly instead of hidden: every staging check needs an identity token, so A2-P3's liveness assertion is not a bare `curl`, and staging does not validate production's public-ingress behaviour.
  9. **Build once and promote the digest** (one build, two tags) so staging validates the exact bits production will run. Correct, and rejected *here* only because it requires merging the two build-configs, breaking this packet's "production otherwise byte-untouched" constraint. Recorded as deferred work, and the limitation it leaves is stated plainly rather than papered over — see Effects.
  10. **Add an eval-only HTTP route so the eval can run "against the staging service" literally.** Rejected — that is M5's endpoint arriving early under an operational label. A2-P3 targets the staging *contour* (its database, plus the service's liveness), not a new surface.
- **Supersedes.** None. **Amends the applicability of `L1-DL-001` alternative 2** (which rejected a standalone staging service under ADR-020 as it then stood); that entry is historical and is left byte-untouched, and the L1 gate itself is unchanged and now extended to production `/api`. Cross-references vault **ADR-020 as amended** — this entry is the repo-side definition site for the L2 half, mirroring how `L1-DL-001` is the definition site for the L1 half. Builds on the M2-P3 build topology (per-app build-configs on path-filtered triggers), `M3-DL-002` (the Alembic migrations the handoff applies to the new database), and `A1-DL-003` (the restore procedure whose blast radius the shared instance widens). Cites ADR-008 (one managed PostgreSQL as source of truth), ADR-011 / ADR-014 (the per-egress clearance gates that stay unset in staging) and PDR-010 (the authenticated spine this contour precedes).
- **Effects.**
  - **Added:** `api/cloudbuild.staging.yaml` — the staging build-config (guard → build → push → deploy), with the three empty-default substitutions.
  - **Changed (live deploy path):** `api/cloudbuild.yaml` — three appended deploy args (`--no-traffic`, `--tag`, `sha-$SHORT_SHA`) and the Step-3 comment, which claimed "Real DB config lands with M3". That was false: M3 landed and production still deliberately carries no database configuration, because its consumers are owner-run offline CLIs rather than routes. Nothing else in the file moves.
  - **Changed (RUNBOOK):** the CI/CD and hosting bullets (two services → three); **Build + deploy path** (two build-configs → three, per-trigger `ignoredFiles` recorded, the production `--set-*` claim scoped to production, and the promotion-gate bullet rewritten from "No standalone staging *service* exists" to the L1 + L2 split with the amendment's reasoning); new `### Promotion + rollback (`/api`)`; new `## Staging contour (L2, owner-run)`; **Restore / disaster recovery** steps 1, 2 and 5 (an in-place backup restore is instance-wide and destroys `theygrow_staging` as collateral; a PITR clone now carries both databases; the `--set-*` claim scoped to production); **Live-infra divergence** gains the staging block and the note that the instance connection name now has a second operational home in the trigger; the guardrail path list gains `api/cloudbuild.staging.yaml`; and four `**Promotion + rollback**` cross-references disambiguated now that two such sections exist.
  - **Changed (harness config):** `.pre-commit-config.yaml` — both hygiene-hook `exclude` regexes widened to `cloudbuild(\.staging)?\.yaml` plus the header comment, so the new build-config sits inside the live-deploy-path exclusion the file already declares rather than being auto-rewritten by `trailing-whitespace` / `end-of-file-fixer`. `scripts/check-contract-integrity.sh` — **header comment only**: the hand-maintained out-of-scope enumeration gains the staging build-config. No pattern, no `CONTRACT_FILES` entry, no control flow changes.
  - **Contract-gate interaction, recorded so a later packet does not rediscover it at commit time:** `INFRA_RE` bans the substring `theygrow-api-repo` in every contract/spine file except `docs/RUNBOOK.md`, and the staging image path **contains** that token. It may therefore appear in the build-config (never scanned) and in RUNBOOK (exempt), but must **never** be quoted into `docs/execution-map.md` or `docs/product/TechSpec.md` at milestone close — name file paths there and link to RUNBOOK, as the L1 entry already does. `theygrow-api-staging` and `theygrow_staging` match no banned pattern.
  - **Operability: none** (ADR-013 / contract §4.7). No `theygrow_api.parameters` knob: the three substitutions are per-trigger infra identifiers deliberately kept *out* of the repository, which is the opposite of what the typed config surface is for, and deploy flags are topology rather than tunables. `PARAMETERS_SCHEMA_VERSION` stays `4`. No `theygrow_api.signals` taxonomy entry — a kind with no producer would violate `M4-P3-INV-003`; the eval scorecard in A2-P3 is what earns one.
  - **No `docs/INVARIANTS.md` entry** — this packet adds an owner-run `gcloud`/SQL procedure, not a machine-enforced test, lint rule, schema constraint or runtime guard (`AGENTS.md` §10, enforced-only). Precedent: `L1-DL-001` added none for the same reason.
  - **§4-safe.** No new egress carrying family data, and none carrying any data: staging is seeded from synthetic content only (A2-P2) and both provider-clearance flags stay unset, so the embedder and answers gates remain fail-closed. No credential, host, user, password or connection string enters the repository; the RUNBOOK gives URL *shapes* with placeholders. The handoff additionally warns against `alembic … --sql` into a shared log, since offline mode renders the URL with `hide_password=False`.
  - **Validation-honesty caveat** (M4-CLOSE discipline). This packet was validated entirely repo-locally: the real CI gate (`pre-commit run --all-files`, including `check-yaml` over the new build-config and the contract-integrity gate), a diff-scope check on the production build-config, and `git diff --stat -- app/` empty. **Nothing here proves the contour works.** That the staging service deploys, that the Cloud SQL socket resolves, that the secret's contents are a usable URL, and that the isolation barrier holds are owner-run steps 1–9 of the handoff — the deliverable is an executable handoff, not its execution. Two limitations are carried rather than closed: the deployed service exercises none of the database wiring it declares (no route constructs `Settings`), and **staging validates the source tree at a commit, not the image production will run** — the two triggers run independent builds of a Dockerfile pinned to the mutable `python:3.12-slim` tag with `>=` dependency ranges and an unused `uv.lock`. Build-once-promote-the-digest is the fix, and it is deferred, not assumed.
  - **Deferred to later A2 packets:** the synthetic corpus, the seed CLI and its fail-closed staging-marker guard (P2); the eval runner, its thresholds and its scorecard signal (P3); and, at A2-CLOSE, the `docs/execution-map.md` L2 track entry (it needs a commit SHA that does not exist while the packet is being written), the `AGENTS.md` §7 live-deploy-path enumeration, and `docs/product/TechSpec.md`'s `/api` build-config bullet.

## L2-DL-002 — Synthetic staging corpus + seed CLI: a fail-closed contour guard, and a clearance gate scoped to egress

- **Date.** 2026-07-28
- **Type.** Runtime-code + corpus decision — A2-P2, the second packet of the A2 l2-staging milestone. *(Namespace note: continues the `L2-DL` namespace opened by `L2-DL-001`; the "Entry format" header is intentionally left unedited, per the `L1-DL-001` / `PWA-DL-001` / `B1-DL-018` precedent.)*
- **Decision.** Commit a synthetic `/export` v1 corpus under `api/corpus/staging/` — invented Russian throughout, one export file per community — and add `theygrow-seed-staging`, which chains `import_export` → `rederive` → `embed_backfill` in a single transaction behind a two-layer fail-closed staging-marker guard. To let the embeddings pass run with both clearance flags unset, the §4 clearance gate is **scoped to egress**: it applies to the real provider and to any injected provider that does not structurally declare `performs_no_egress is True`, and is skipped only for an in-perimeter provider. The basis is that **privacy clearance is per-egress-surface** (ADR-014): an in-perimeter embedder does not satisfy the clearance, it removes the surface the clearance exists to authorize, so there is nothing left to clear. The check is fail-closed on an absent declaration and the real provider path is unchanged.
- **Rationale.** The rule staging dies of is not a subtle one — it is an operator pasting the wrong `DATABASE_URL`. So the guard asks twice, in two different ways: the database name parsed from the URL before a connection is opened (catching the paste with zero cost), and `SELECT current_database()` on the connected server before any write (catching what the string cannot show — a libpq `host=`/socket override, a pooler, a connection that lands somewhere the URL did not name). Both compare against an allowlist of exactly one name, so an unrecognized or absent name refuses too; a denylist of production names would wave through every database nobody thought to name. One transaction wraps the whole seed, so any refusal or failure leaves the target exactly as it was. The corpus is sized by one binding constraint rather than by taste: `FTS_CONFIG` is `'simple'` (no Russian stemming), so `candidate_k = 50` truncates only if more than 50 *eligible* chunks carry one literal surface form — 66 do, which also gives `top_k = 10` a real surplus to cut.
- **Alternatives considered.**
  1. **Synthesize `Settings(embedder_privacy_cleared=True)` inside the seed.** Rejected — it leaves `embeddings_backfill.py` untouched at the cost of asserting a clearance the owner never granted. A later reader would find the staging path claiming clearance with nothing behind it, and the flag would have quietly stopped meaning what it says everywhere else. A dishonest flag is worse than an honestly narrowed gate.
  2. **Give the seed its own embedding writer, bypassing `embed_backfill` entirely.** Rejected — duplicates the `M4-P2-INV-003` mechanics (status-driven idempotency, HNSW-after-population) and, worse, means the seeded contour was populated by a code path production never runs, which is precisely what a staging contour must not be.
  3. **A hash-random deterministic stub.** Rejected — vectors with no similarity structure make the dense leg pure noise, so P3's *semantic-only hit* case class would be unauthorable. The similarity instead comes from a corpus-side lexicon (`concepts.json`), which keeps the shipped package free of staging vocabulary.
  4. **The staging marker as a `parameters.py` knob.** Rejected — `RuntimeParameters` is env-overridable under `THEYGROW_PARAM_`, and an env-overridable safety marker is not fail-closed: one stray variable re-opens the exact hole the constant closes. It is a safety constant, not a qualitative knob.
  5. **One multi-community export file.** Rejected — the importer would accept it (records are not cross-checked against `envelope.scope`), but the engine only ever emits one scoped export per community, and a fixture that misrepresents the wire contract is a poor basis for an eval meant to prove production-shaped behaviour.
  6. **A committed generator script instead of committed JSON.** Rejected — it invites regeneration drift and adds a code surface P3 does not need. The JSON is the artifact of record.
- **Supersedes.** None. Amends `M4-P2-INV-002`'s statement (see Effects).
- **Effects.**
  - **Added:** `api/corpus/staging/export-v1/comm-staging-{alpha,beta,gamma}.json` (136 records), `api/corpus/staging/concepts.json`, `api/corpus/staging/README.md`; `api/theygrow_api/seed_staging.py`; `api/theygrow_api/adapters/embeddings/local_deterministic.py`; `api/tests/test_seed_staging.py` (27 tests); the `theygrow-seed-staging` console script.
  - **Changed (runtime):** `api/theygrow_api/embeddings_backfill.py` — `_performs_no_egress` added and the gate call site restructured; module docstring corrected, since its "with the flag unset NOTHING is written" wording became false for the in-perimeter path.
  - **Changed (image build context):** `api/.dockerignore` gains `corpus/`. Defense-in-depth only — `api/Dockerfile` has no wildcard `COPY` and takes only `pyproject.toml` + `theygrow_api/`, which is the real guarantee. Recorded honestly: **no guard reads `api/.dockerignore`**, the same blindness `A1-DL-008` noted for `app/.dockerignore`.
  - **Corpus composition** (measured, and asserted by the test suite so the README cannot drift): 136 source messages → 295 chunks across 3 communities; 109 `note` / 27 `draft` (80.1% / 19.9%); span 2024-06-15 … 2026-06-11 (726 days ≈ 23.9 months); 101 date-led / 35 fallback-dated; 66 eligible chunks carrying the exact token `сон`; ~23.5% adversarial or no-evidence material. This **closes the ADR-020 residual open question on synthetic-corpus volume for A2**, and the volume is recorded as **revisitable** — if P3 finds a case class it cannot author, grow that class, not the corpus.
  - **Operability: none** (ADR-013 / contract §4.7). No `theygrow_api.parameters` knob (the marker constant is deliberately *not* on the env-overridable surface — alternative 4 above); `PARAMETERS_SCHEMA_VERSION` stays `4`. No `theygrow_api.signals` taxonomy entry and no `emitted_now` change: the seed adds a *caller* of `rederive` / `embed_backfill`, not a kind, so `DERIVATION_COUNTERS` and `EMBEDDING_COUNTERS` emit as they already did and `M4-P3-INV-003`'s kind-set comparison needs no driver extension. No GA4 event.
  - **`docs/INVARIANTS.md`:** adds `L2-P2-INV-001` (two-layer staging-marker refusal) and `L2-P2-INV-002` (the gate's default is egress; the seed crosses no third-party boundary), and **amends `M4-P2-INV-002`** — its statement said the backfill "writes nothing unless the flag is set", which this packet makes literally false on the in-perimeter path. Amendment-in-place follows the `A1-P4-INV-001` precedent.
  - **No `docs/INVARIANTS.md` entry for "the corpus contains no real family data"** — the milestone's hardest rule, and unprovable by any test: a test can assert text exists, never that it was authored rather than derived. It is held by authorship discipline and stated at the top of `api/corpus/staging/README.md`. Claiming it in an enforced-only file would be exactly the wish-list drift `AGENTS.md` §10 exists to prevent.
  - **§4-safe.** The seed's summary and every log line carry counts only; the guard's refusal names the database but never the connection string (asserted by a test, since the URL carries a password). No new egress — the packet *removes* one from the seeded path. The corpus is authored fiction, but the code path is written as if it were not.
  - **Validation-honesty caveat** (M4-CLOSE discipline). What is proven: the guard refuses, the chain populates a contour with clearance unset and zero third-party calls, the seed is idempotent, and the corpus has the composition its README claims. What is **not** proven: that the vectors mean anything. The local provider's similarity is *authored* from `concepts.json`, not learned — it exercises the dense leg's plumbing and makes a semantic-only eval case authorable, and transfers **no** recall or grounding-quality claim to the real embedder. Nothing was run against live staging; that remains owner-run (`docs/RUNBOOK.md` step 11).
  - **Seam-honesty caveat.** `_performs_no_egress` *trusts* a declaration; nothing verifies a declaring provider opens no socket. That is a code-review property, and `L2-P2-INV-002`'s scope says so rather than implying the gate can see it.
  - **Deferred to later A2 packets:** the labeled query set, the eval runner, its thresholds and its scorecard signal (P3 — this packet makes them authorable and stops); and, at A2-CLOSE, the `docs/execution-map.md` L2 track entry, the `AGENTS.md` §7 live-deploy-path enumeration, and `docs/product/TechSpec.md`'s `/api` build-config bullet.

---

## L2-DL-003 — Eval pipeline: a labeled set that is its own baseline, an egress-scoped read path, and a scorecard that states what it does not measure

- **Date.** 2026-07-28
- **Type.** Runtime-code + CI + docs decision — A2-P3, the third packet of the A2 l2-staging milestone. *(Namespace note: continues the `L2-DL` namespace opened by `L2-DL-001`; the "Entry format" header is intentionally left unedited, per the `L1-DL-001` / `PWA-DL-001` / `B1-DL-018` precedent.)*
- **Decision.** Add `api/theygrow_api/evals/` — a labeled query set frozen as code (26 cases, full coverage of the six case classes `api/corpus/staging/README.md` tabulates, one holdout per class), a runner driving each case through both `retrieve()` and `answer_query()`, and a counts-only scorecard — plus `theygrow-eval` with two run modes (CI against the ephemeral pgvector service; owner-run against the staging contour with an identity-token liveness assertion on `/api/health`). Four sub-decisions carry the packet. **(1)** The read-path clearance gates are **scoped to egress**, mirroring what `L2-DL-002` did for the backfill: `retrieve()` and `answer_query()` gated unconditionally *before* provider injection, so an in-perimeter provider could not run at all. The declaration predicate is hoisted from `embeddings_backfill._performs_no_egress` to `ports.provider.performs_no_egress`, beside the two ports it now serves. **(2)** The answers side gets an in-perimeter provider (`adapters/answers/local_scripted.py`) that answers by **policy** — a pure function of the prompt it was handed — never by a per-case script. **(3)** The labeled set **is** the baseline: expectations are recorded per case, with predicates rather than exact ids wherever an id set is not derivable from the corpus. **(4)** The gate criterion is binary and structural, with **no `parameters.py` knob** and no separately recorded baseline file.
- **Rationale.** Three things drove the shape. First, an eval that had to set `embedder_privacy_cleared` / `answers_privacy_cleared` to run would assert a clearance the owner never granted — the same dishonesty `L2-DL-002` alternative 1 rejected — so the gates were narrowed instead, keeping the fail-closed default that an undeclared provider is an egressing provider. Second, the expectations had to be authorable from committed data. For 66 chunks carrying `сон` with `ts_rank_cd` tying across nearly all of them, "which ids land in `top_k`" is decided by RRF and is knowable only by running the pipeline; writing those ids down would be a transcription of current behaviour dressed as a specification, and the gate would then only ever confirm itself. Exact ids are therefore reserved for cases whose match set is complete or unique (the two competing first-utterance records, the verbatim line shared by alpha and beta, the draft twins of note chunks), and the high-competition classes carry rank-robust predicates instead — result count, leg provenance (`sparse_rank is None` is what makes a semantic-only case semantic-only), and term/concept coverage read from `concepts.json`. Third, three of the six classes assert **absence**, so an empty or stale contour would pass them trivially and report green; `check_contour` therefore refuses before any case runs, with its own exit code, and additionally re-embeds one stored chunk to prove the contour was seeded with the lexicon this run holds — nothing in the database records that, and a mismatch would silently turn the dense leg into noise.
- **Alternatives considered.**
  1. **Set the clearance flags in the eval's `Settings`.** Rejected — see rationale; it is `L2-DL-002` alternative 1 in a new costume, and it would have made the flags mean nothing everywhere else.
  2. **Restructure `answer_query`'s gate into the backfill's gate-and-build block.** Rejected — `_build_answers_provider` is deliberately called *after* the grounding gate so no client is constructed below the bar (`M4-P4-INV-002`, `test_no_context_degrades_with_zero_answers_calls`). The gate moved; the build did not.
  3. **A per-case scripted answers provider.** Rejected — if the scripted citations were the case's expected ids, a retrieval miss would surface as a fabricated citation and grade as `parse_failure`, so one root cause would paint two case classes red and send the reader to the wrong leg. A policy citing only what the prompt actually offered keeps the legs independent. It also reaches `parse_failure` and `provider_unavailable`, which no live model can be made to produce on demand.
  4. **A separately recorded baseline file compared each run.** Rejected — with an all-pass criterion the file would say only "everything passed", which the labeled set already says, while adding a second source of truth whose worst affordance is *re-record the baseline until CI is green*. That is precisely what this gate exists to remove. **Confirmed against the upstream decision layer:** the packet handoff's "compare against a recorded baseline rather than an absolute number" is satisfied by per-case recorded expectations — the run is never compared to an aggregate number at all.
  5. **`eval_min_pass_rate` / `eval_max_class_regressions` on `RuntimeParameters`.** Rejected. **Confirmed against the upstream decision layer:** the operability discipline binds *qualitative* knobs, and an all-pass / zero-leak criterion is structural rather than tunable. It also would not be fail-closed — `RuntimeParameters` is env-overridable under `THEYGROW_PARAM_`, so `THEYGROW_PARAM_EVAL_MIN_PASS_RATE=0` would be a green build, the same hole alternative 4 of `L2-DL-002` closed for the staging marker. This supersedes the packet handoff's "thresholds land in `parameters.py`" line, which presupposed numeric thresholds this design does not have. **Reversal trigger, named so it is not rediscovered:** the first genuinely non-binary, tunable threshold — a soft gate with a configurable value — lands in `parameters.py` with `changed_in` provenance and the schema-version bump that implies.
  6. **Run the eval only as a pytest test, with no CI job.** Rejected — the owner runs `theygrow-eval` against staging, so CI must run the *same* entry point or the gate drifts into a local shadow of itself (the argument `scripts/parity-suite.sh` already makes). A pytest end-to-end case exists as well, so `pytest api` still covers the seam; the job is what gates.
  7. **`httpx` for the staging liveness probe.** Rejected — `httpx` is a dev-only dependency, so this would add a runtime dependency and a `.pre-commit-config.yaml` `additional_dependencies` change for one authenticated GET. Stdlib `urllib` does it.
  8. **Let the eval skip the staging-marker guard because it only reads.** Rejected — the seed's guard protects writes; this one protects the **artifact**, whose counts would otherwise describe whatever database it happened to reach. "It only reads" is exactly the reasoning that would later justify deleting the guard, so it is written down in `evals/scorecard.py` instead of assumed.
- **Supersedes.** None. Amends the statements of `M4-P2-INV-002` (predicate relocated; egress scoping extended to the read path) and `M4-P4-INV-001` (answers gate restated as egress-scoped), and the "Enforced by" of `L2-P2-INV-002` (predicate path). Supersedes the packet handoff's threshold-placement instruction, per alternative 5.
- **Effects.**
  - **Added:** `api/theygrow_api/evals/{__init__,queries,scorecard,runner}.py`; `api/theygrow_api/adapters/answers/local_scripted.py`; `api/tests/test_evals.py` (26 tests); the `theygrow-eval` console script; a third CI job, `eval`.
  - **Changed (runtime):** `api/theygrow_api/ports/provider.py` — `performs_no_egress` hoisted here, typed `object` so one predicate serves both ports, semantics byte-for-byte unchanged. `api/theygrow_api/embeddings_backfill.py` — imports it, private copy deleted, call site unchanged. `api/theygrow_api/services/retrieval.py` and `api/theygrow_api/services/query_service.py` — gates narrowed to egress; both module docstrings corrected, since their "the gate runs first even with a provider injected" wording became false for the in-perimeter path. `api/theygrow_api/seed_staging.py` — the two guards take an `action` label so the eval's refusal does not say "refusing to seed"; a message hardcoding the wrong verb is a false statement, and one guard with an accurate verb beats a second drifting copy.
  - **The narrowing grants no clearance.** It changes **who must be cleared, never whether**: `answers_privacy_cleared` remains default-false, the real-provider path is fail-closed exactly as before, and **owner clearance for the answers residency surface (ZDR + DPA + EU) remains an OPEN PREREQUISITE ahead of the chat milestone's go-live**. Stated in the `query_service` module docstring and in `M4-P4-INV-001`, so nothing downstream can read the in-perimeter exemption as that clearance having been obtained.
  - **Labeled set composition:** 26 cases — lexical-hit 4, semantic-only-hit 5 (one per reserved probe), no-evidence 5, ambiguous 4, cross-community-isolation 4, draft-unreachable 4. Adversarial/absence-asserting classes are 13 of 26 (50%), well above the one-fifth floor. Six holdouts, one per class, **gated identically to the rest and reported separately** — a reported-only holdout would manufacture a class of silent failures. Their discipline is a review rule, stated in `evals/queries.py`: if a future packet tunes a retrieval knob to turn a red case green, the holdout cases must pass unchanged and **unedited** in the same commit, and **holdout cases are not to be edited to make a run green**. Any change to the set at all bumps `LABELED_SET_VERSION`.
  - **Two honest-degradation contours, distinguished on purpose.** Neither leg has a similarity threshold — the dense leg always returns its nearest rows — so an off-topic query against `comm-staging-gamma` *does* retrieve chunks, and the honest outcome is the model declaring `no_evidence` and the answer being suppressed. The pre-provider grounding gate (zero answers calls, no client built) is reached only when retrieval returns nothing, so it is exercised by an empty query and by a date window outside the corpus span. Both shapes are asserted; conflating them would have hidden one.
  - **Operability** (ADR-013 / contract §4.7). **Knobs: none**, deliberately — see alternative 5, including the named reversal trigger. `PARAMETERS_SCHEMA_VERSION` stays `4`. The eval *reads* the existing `top_k` / `candidate_k` / RRF knobs and introduces no new retrieval knob — in particular **no `ef_search` and no per-case depth override**, which would be an `ef_search`-shaped knob by another name. **Signal: one new kind**, `EVAL_SCORECARD` (`eval.scorecard`), `producing_stage="A2-P3"`, `emitted_now=True`, emitted through the injectable `SignalSink` by `evals.scorecard.emit_scorecard` — one emission per case class plus a `case_class="total"` roll-up, the `RetrievalLatency` `leg="fused"` pattern (one kind, a bounded label distinguishing the emissions) rather than a second kind. The emitter is a pure function over an already-computed `Scorecard` precisely so `M4-P3-INV-003`'s driver can cover it without seeding a 295-chunk contour. `api/tests/test_signals.py` gains `case_class` in its label-key set and `A2-P3` in its `producing_stage` domain — the descriptor's stage field is milestone-qualified for non-spine tracks, which do not share M4's packet numbering. No GA4 event.
  - **`docs/INVARIANTS.md`:** adds `L2-P3-INV-001` (read-path gates default to egress; the eval crosses no third-party boundary) and `L2-P3-INV-002` (recorded per-case gate; §4-safe reporting), and amends `M4-P2-INV-002`, `M4-P4-INV-001` and `L2-P2-INV-002` in place, following the `A1-P4-INV-001` precedent.
  - **`docs/RUNBOOK.md`:** the `/api` promotion step 5 now names the command and its exit codes — and still says, in the same breath, that **nothing reads them**; the precondition remains an owner convention, and automating it is deferred. Its stale "the eval pipeline itself lands in A2-P3" clause is removed, since this packet makes it false. The staging-contour section gains step 12 (owner-run eval, identity token supplied by the owner — **this repo never invokes `gcloud`**) and records that the liveness probe **proves ingress only**: no deployed route opens a database connection, so a green probe says nothing about the database the eval reads.
  - **§4-safe.** The scorecard and every log line carry case ids, bounded check names, counts and timings only — never query text, chunk text, `community_id`, or `chunk_id` (a synthetic id like `sm-alpha-0067#3` still names its community by substring, so `case_id` carries strictly less). Asserted by `test_scorecard_carries_no_community_id_chunk_id_or_text`. No new egress: both providers are in-perimeter and the only network call in the packet is an authenticated GET to our own staging health endpoint, carrying a token and no family data.
  - **Metric-name guard.** No artifact field is named `recall`, `recall_at_k`, `precision`, `hit_rate`, `mrr`, `ndcg`, `accuracy`, `quality` or `score`, and a test enforces the list. The document instead carries `measures: "contract-conformance"`, `not_measured`, and `port_out_triggers_unaffected: ["M4-DL-001", "M4-DL-004"]` — the last one deliberately searchable, so anyone grepping those ids to justify a port-out lands on the denial rather than on an inferred metric.
  - **Validation-honesty caveat** (M4-CLOSE discipline). What is proven: on a fixed synthetic corpus, retrieval fusion, note-only eligibility, community scoping, the grounding gate and honest degradation still behave as recorded; the gate refuses a wrong or unseeded contour; the artifact is §4-safe. What is **not** proven: Russian lexical recall, embedder semantic quality, or answer faithfulness — the corpus is authored, the embedder's similarity is authored from `concepts.json`, and the answers provider is our own policy over the prompt. **This eval cannot discharge the M4-CLOSE mini-eval and fires neither named port-out trigger** (`M4-DL-001` `'simple'` FTS → `'russian'`/ParadeDB; `M4-DL-004` `ef_search`); both remain gated on a recall metric against the real corpus with cleared real providers. Every lexical case queries a surface form present verbatim in the corpus, so **no case can fail because `'simple'` does no stemming** — a red lexical case is a fusion/scoping/eligibility regression, never evidence about `FTS_CONFIG`. Nothing was run against live staging; that is owner-run (`docs/RUNBOOK.md` step 12).
  - **Determinism caveat, now asserted.** Both legs order by `(rank, created_at, event_index)` with **no `chunk_id` fallback**, so the `LIMIT` cut is deterministic only while `created_at` distinguishes source messages. It does today; `api/tests/test_seed_staging.py::test_corpus_created_at_is_unique_per_source_message` pins it, since the corpus is recorded revisitable and a duplicate added later would make the eval flap for a reason no failure message would explain.
  - **Known duplication, recorded rather than left to be discovered.** `api/tests/test_query_service.py::_FakeAnswers` also parses the answer prompt, so two parsers of that format now exist. Rewiring P4's tests onto the new adapter would widen this diff into a landed packet's tests for no behavioural gain; the new adapter is the single parser going forward, and `ScriptedAnswersProvider` raises `PromptContractError` when it parses zero chunk_ids from a non-empty chunks block — without which a rendering change would silently re-grade every case to `no_evidence` and the eval would go **green for the wrong reason**.
  - **Deferred:** de-duplicating `_FakeAnswers`; automated enforcement of the `/api` promotion precondition; any HTTP surface beyond `GET /api/health` (an eval-only route would build M5's endpoint early under an operational label); and, at A2-CLOSE, the `docs/execution-map.md` L2 track entry, the `AGENTS.md` §7 live-deploy-path enumeration, and `docs/product/TechSpec.md`'s `/api` build-config bullet.

---

## L2-DL-004 — l2-staging (A2) A2-CLOSE: the milestone's two upstream questions answered, its carried debt collected in one place, and the ledger closed

- **Date.** 2026-07-28
- **Type.** Decision record — milestone close-out, A2-P4, the fourth and last packet of the A2 `l2-staging` milestone. Ledger, contract and docs reconciliation only: no product code, no build-config edit, no schema, no migration, no corpus change, no `LABELED_SET_VERSION` bump, nothing under `app/**`. The P1–P3 ladder is frozen as shipped. *(Namespace note: continues the `L2-DL` namespace opened by `L2-DL-001`; the "Entry format" header is intentionally left unedited, per the `L1-DL-001` / `PWA-DL-001` / `B1-DL-018` precedent.)*
- **Decision.** Close milestone A2 `l2-staging` in the repo ledger, record what it settled upstream, and pay off every item the three L2 packets routed here — each one landed in this packet or re-deferred with a stated reason, none silently dropped:

  a. **The three contract/spine edits all three L2 entries deferred, identically, land together.** `L2-DL-001` Effects, `L2-DL-002` Effects and `L2-DL-003` Effects each close with the same triplet, and each named the same reason for waiting: the packet writing them did not yet have what they needed. `docs/execution-map.md`'s L2 track entry needs the commit SHAs of the packets it records, which do not exist while a packet is being written; `AGENTS.md` §7 and `docs/product/TechSpec.md` are contract files whose edits were deliberately batched so they would arrive in one reviewable place rather than half-consistently across three packets with different file sets — the `A1-DL-008` (b) shape. This is that place. **Permission is verified rather than assumed**: `scripts/check-contract-integrity.sh` was run before and after the edits, and `pre-commit run --all-files` — which runs the same gate via its `always_run: true` local hook — is green with zero modified files.

  b. **The `AGENTS.md` §7 enumeration is the third and last copy, and the RUNBOOK sentence that depends on it stops being false.** A2-P1 already added `api/cloudbuild.staging.yaml` to the other two copies — the `scripts/check-contract-integrity.sh` header comment and the guardrail path list in `docs/RUNBOOK.md`. But that RUNBOOK list does not merely repeat the enumeration, it **claims what the contract files keep as guardrails**, so from A2-P1 until this packet the sentence asserted something `AGENTS.md` §7 did not say. This is the `A1-DL-008` (c) coupling discharged in the opposite order: there the token was added to RUNBOOK and §7 together to avoid creating the falsehood; here the falsehood was created deliberately, with the correction owed and recorded, because the alternative was a contract-file edit inside a build-config packet. §7's M1-era framing is **not** restructured — one token is inserted into one list and nothing else in the section moves.

  c. **`docs/product/TechSpec.md`'s "Prod vs dev" bullet is corrected alongside the `/api` bullet, because this branch is what falsified it.** The deferred item was the `/api` build-config bullet (one build-config → two, and "its own Cloud Run service" singular → two services). The neighbouring ADR-008 bullet reads as an exhaustive two-contour claim — production Cloud SQL, dev docker-compose — and A2-P1 added a third: a staging database on the production instance. Correcting one bullet while leaving the adjacent false one in the same section would replace an incomplete statement with an inconsistent pair. The ADR-008 single-source-of-truth claim is untouched: staging is not a second source of truth, it holds no family data.

  d. **The two residual upstream questions this milestone was chartered to answer are recorded as answered, not left as scattered packet detail.** Both belong to vault ADR-020 and neither is visible from the packet entries without reassembly:
     1. **Synthetic-corpus volume and composition — settled.** 136 source messages → 295 chunks across 3 communities; 109 `note` / 27 `draft` (80.1% / 19.9%); span 2024-06-15 … 2026-06-11 (726 days ≈ 23.9 months); 66 eligible chunks **in one community** carrying the exact token `сон`; 32 records ≈ 23.5% adversarial or no-evidence material. The size is not a matter of taste — it is fixed by the one binding constraint, that `FTS_CONFIG` is `'simple'` so `candidate_k = 50` truncates only if more than 50 *eligible* chunks share a literal surface form. It is recorded **revisitable with a named trigger**: if a later packet finds a case class it cannot author, grow **that class**, not the corpus. The composition is asserted by the test suite so the corpus README cannot drift from it.
     2. **The eval-against-staging pipeline shape — settled.** Library seams (`retrieve()` and `answer_query()`) driven against the staging **contour** — its database — plus an identity-token liveness assertion against the staging service, rather than an eval-only HTTP route, which would be M5's endpoint arriving early under an operational label. Deterministic in-perimeter providers on both seams, so the run crosses no third-party boundary and asserts no clearance the owner never granted. A counts-only, §4-safe scorecard whose field names are guarded against metric-shaped claims. Two run modes over the **same entry point** — CI against an ephemeral pgvector service, owner-run against staging — so the gate cannot drift into a local shadow of itself. And it gates promotion **by convention**: `docs/RUNBOOK.md` names the command and its exit codes and says, in the same breath, that nothing reads them.

  e. **The repo decision-log is the working copy, and the upstream decision layer has not been annotated.** Nothing on this branch touched it and nothing could: the vault sits outside this repository. Three outcomes are owed upstream and are named here so the debt has an address — the staging contour's amendment to ADR-020 (for which `L2-DL-001` is the repo-side definition site, not the upstream record), the two residual questions closed in (d), and the **egress-scoped clearance narrowing**, which after A2-P2 and A2-P3 now binds both the write path and the read path and therefore changes how ADR-011 / ADR-014 read at three call sites. That annotation is the **owner-side close task after merge**. Nothing in this entry should be read as it having happened.

  f. **The milestone's carried limitations are collected in one place** — each already stated in an earlier L2 entry, restated here so the milestone has one address rather than three, and none of them softened:
     1. **Artifact identity.** The staging contour validates **the source tree at a commit, not the image bits production will run**: the two triggers run independent builds of a Dockerfile pinned to a mutable base tag with `>=` dependency ranges and an unused lockfile. **Build-once-promote-the-digest** is the named fix (`L2-DL-001` alternative 9, rejected there only because it requires merging the two build-configs and would have broken that packet's "production otherwise byte-untouched" constraint). Deferred, not assumed.
     2. **`performs_no_egress` is a trusted declaration, not a verification of the socket.** Nothing proves a declaring provider opens no connection; that is a code-review property, and `L2-P2-INV-002` / `L2-P3-INV-001` say so in their Scope rather than implying the gate can see it. The **default** is what is enforced, and it is fail-closed: an absent declaration, or any value that is not exactly `True`, is gated exactly as before the narrowing.
     3. **Two parsers of the answer-prompt format exist** — `api/theygrow_api/adapters/answers/local_scripted.py` and `api/tests/test_query_service.py::_FakeAnswers`. The adapter is the single parser going forward; de-duplicating would widen a docs-only close-out into a landed packet's tests for no behavioural gain.
     4. **The `/api` promotion precondition is an owner convention, not a mechanism**, and automating it stays deferred, as does any HTTP surface beyond `GET /api/health`.
     5. **The M4-CLOSE mini-eval and both port-out triggers are untouched and unaffected.** `M4-DL-001` (`'simple'` FTS → `'russian'`/ParadeDB) and `M4-DL-004` (`ef_search`) remain gated on a recall metric against the **real** corpus with **cleared real** providers. A2's eval measures contract conformance on an authored corpus with an authored embedder and our own answers policy; it can discharge none of that, and the scorecard says so in a deliberately greppable field.
     6. **Owner clearance for the answers residency surface (ZDR + DPA + EU) remains an open prerequisite.** The A2-P3 narrowing changed *who must be cleared, never whether*; `answers_privacy_cleared` is still default-false.
     7. **The contour has never been stood up.** No staging service, database, role, secret, trigger or seed exists in the live project. The milestone's deliverable is an executable handoff (`docs/RUNBOOK.md` "Staging contour", steps 1–12), and every step of it is owner-run.

  g. **No `docs/INVARIANTS.md` entry, and no amendment to the four A2 ones.** `AGENTS.md` §10 makes the file enforced-only and a close-out enforces nothing new; A2-P2 and A2-P3 landed `L2-P2-INV-001/002` and `L2-P3-INV-001/002` and amended `M4-P2-INV-002`, `M4-P4-INV-001` and `L2-P2-INV-002` in place. All seven were re-read against the shipped tree in this packet and none carries a residual falsehood. The two A2 artifacts *not* covered — the build-config's step-0 substitution guard and the contour handoff itself — are a Cloud Build step and an owner-run `gcloud`/SQL procedure with no repo-side test, lint rule, schema constraint or runtime guard behind them; `L2-DL-001` already recorded that refusal, citing `L1-DL-001`.

  h. **No new **Current** track is set.** The ladder returns to M1–M5 and the next-track choice remains the standing open owner decision the map already records at the M5 line — the `A1-DL-008` disposition, for the same reason: inventing a successor here would be a planning decision this packet has no authority to make.

  i. **Re-deferred, with reasons — named here so none is lost.** All are false statements this branch did **not** create:
     1. **The broader `AGENTS.md` M1-era refresh** — §6's milestone map has never named a non-spine track, and §7 is still framed around M1 as the live milestone ("in any M1 packet", the `chore/m1-harness` branch, "M1 packets: P1…P4"). Falsified by M2 onward; re-deferred by `A1-DL-008` (g)(5) on exactly this reasoning and not reopened.
     2. **`docs/RUNTIME-INVARIANTS.md`'s Scope paragraph** — "Today it is fully documented, not enforced — no product code exists yet", contradicted by its own last section, which records `M2-P2` enforcement. Falsified by M2-P2.
     3. **`docs/product/BuildPlan.md`** — M1-P2 still marked **Current packet.** and M2-P2 still marked **Current.** Falsified by M1-P3 and M2-P3; the living state map is `docs/execution-map.md`, which is correct.
     4. **`README.md`** — "The repository currently ships a static PWA … the `/app` front end and the `/api` back end lands from M2 onward." Falsified by M2-P1.
     5. **`docs/INVARIANTS.md`'s "Entry format" footer**, which requires "**Landed in.** Packet id + commit SHA" while not one of the file's entries carries a SHA. The *practice* is the deliberate half — an entry cannot cite the SHA of the commit that introduces it — so the footer is the false half, and correcting a convention nineteen landed entries depend on is a contract packet, not a close-out's work.
     6. **The two owner-fill-in stubs in `docs/RUNBOOK.md`** (the `DATABASE_URL` secret name and the runtime service-account address). Owner action by design; inventing a docs default would be the live-identifier leak `M1-P3-INV-002` exists to prevent.
- **Rationale.** The A2 packets bought their reviewability by *not* fixing things: each kept its diff to one change shape, so a red gate would name its cause, and pushed the contract-file edits onto a list. That discipline only pays if the list is settled somewhere, in full, in one place — otherwise "deferred to A2-CLOSE" is a synonym for "dropped". This packet is that settlement, and it has a second job the A1 close did not: A2 was chartered to answer two upstream questions, and the answers currently exist only as measurements buried in two packet entries. A ledger that records what was *built* but not what was *decided* leaves the next planner to re-derive both from the corpus README and the runner's docstrings. The design constraint throughout is boundary honesty: what this milestone falsified is fixed, what an earlier milestone falsified is named and deferred, and what was never done — the contour, the upstream annotation — is stated as not done rather than left to be inferred from silence.
- **No dedicated ADR by design at the repo layer.** The packet closes the repo-side execution of vault **ADR-020 as amended** and decides nothing the vault owns. Ledger shape, deferral disposition, the venue for the batched contract edits, and the decision to record (d) here rather than leave it in the packet entries are repo-level calls.
- **Alternatives considered.**
  1. **Close with no decision-log entry** (a legitimate shape for a decision-free close, and the shape most non-spine tracks in this log use — their close-out is folded into the track's single entry). Rejected — L2 already has three entries, so folding has no home; and the close carries at least five real judgements ((c), (d), (e), (g), (i)), each of which the log exists to make checkable.
  2. **Leave (d) in the packet entries and let the close-out point at them.** Rejected — the two answers are the milestone's actual product at the decision layer, and they currently exist as a composition table in one entry and a scattered set of design notes in another. A reader asking "what did A2 settle?" should not have to reassemble it.
  3. **Fix `docs/RUNTIME-INVARIANTS.md`, `docs/product/BuildPlan.md` and `README.md` while the spine is open.** Rejected — see (i). Each is falsified by an earlier milestone, and "while we are in there" is how a bounded close-out becomes an unscoped contract refresh with no review boundary.
  4. **Leave `docs/product/TechSpec.md`'s "Prod vs dev" bullet alone, since only the `/api` bullet was on the deferred list.** Rejected — see (c). This branch is what made it false, which is the same test that puts the `/api` bullet in scope.
  5. **Name the staging service, registry repository and database in `docs/execution-map.md` and `docs/product/TechSpec.md`**, on the grounds that two of the three tokens match no banned pattern (recorded in `L2-DL-001` Effects). Rejected — the contract gate is the floor, `M1-P3-INV-002`'s intent is the bar, and `docs/RUNBOOK.md` states the discipline as contract files keeping guardrails as unnamed file paths and linking there. The L1 track entry already holds that line; the L2 one matches it.
  6. **Record the staging contour as a `docs/INVARIANTS.md` entry**, since it is the milestone's most safety-relevant artifact. Rejected — see (g). It is an owner-run procedure, and claiming it in an enforced-only file is precisely the wish-list drift `AGENTS.md` §10 prevents.
  7. **Set the next milestone as **Current** in the execution map.** Rejected — see (h).
- **Supersedes.** None. Discharges the close-out deferrals recorded by `L2-DL-001`, `L2-DL-002` and `L2-DL-003`, and closes the milestone opened by `L2-DL-001`. Builds on `A1-DL-008` (the close-out shape, the batched-contract-edit venue and the fixed-versus-deferred boundary rule it established) and `L1-DL-001` (the gate this milestone extended to `/api`, left byte-untouched). Cross-references vault **ADR-020 as amended**; leaves ADR-011 / ADR-014 semantically unchanged while noting in (e) that the egress scoping now binds both paths and is owed an upstream annotation.
- **Effects.**
  - **Changed (contract):** `AGENTS.md` — §7 live-deploy-path enumeration only, gaining `cloudbuild.staging.yaml`. One line; no other line in §7 or anywhere else in the file moves.
  - **Changed (spine):** `docs/execution-map.md` — the `l2-staging (A2)` track added to **Current state** as a closed cross-cutting track, between the A1 block and the M5 line, with P1–P3 at their SHAs (the A2-P2 ruff-format fixup rendered on the P2 line, per the `M4-P4` precedent) and an `A2-CLOSE` bullet. The track header states the **id split** — milestone `A2 l2-staging`, packets `A2-P{k}`, decisions `L2-DL-{NNN}`, invariants `L2-P{k}-INV-{NNN}` — because this is the first track in the map whose packet prefix differs from its decision prefix, and a reader would otherwise take `A2` and `L2` for two tracks. `docs/product/TechSpec.md` — the `/api` monorepo bullet and the ADR-008 **Prod vs dev** bullet (a staging sub-bullet added). This entry.
  - **Untouched, and verified so:** everything under `api/**` and `app/**`; `.pre-commit-config.yaml`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; `scripts/**` (including `scripts/check-contract-integrity.sh`, whose header enumeration A2-P1 already brought current); `docs/RUNBOOK.md` (A2-P1 and A2-P3 reconciled it packet-by-packet, and its guardrail-list claim becomes true as a *consequence* of the `AGENTS.md` §7 edit rather than needing one of its own); `docs/INVARIANTS.md`; `docs/RUNTIME-INVARIANTS.md`; `docs/product/BuildPlan.md`; `README.md`; `CLAUDE.md`; `docker-compose.yml`; `pyproject.toml`.
  - **Operability: none** (ADR-013 / contract §4.7). No `theygrow_api.parameters` knob — the packet edits no Python and introduces no tunable; `PARAMETERS_SCHEMA_VERSION` stays `4`, where A2-P3 left it. No `theygrow_api.signals` taxonomy entry, no `emitted_now` change, no producer added or removed: `EVAL_SCORECARD` and every M4 kind emit exactly as they already did, so `M4-P3-INV-003`'s kind-set comparison needs no driver extension. No GA4 event (the `/app` surface is byte-untouched). Documentation emits nothing.
  - **§4-safe.** No new egress, no origin contacted, no request that did not already happen. No personal or family data touched: no corpus record, no database, no credential, host, user, password or connection string enters the repository, and no live identifier enters a contract or spine file — verified by grepping the three banned patterns out of every file this packet edited.
  - **Validation-honesty caveat** (M4-CLOSE discipline). This packet was validated entirely repo-locally: the real CI gate (`pre-commit run --all-files`) green with **zero modified files**, the contract-integrity gate run directly before and after the edits, and a diff-scope check confirming no product code, no build-config and nothing under `app/` moved. **That proves the docs gate is green and the diff is scoped. It proves nothing about whether the reconciled prose is true** — that a statement now matches reality is a reviewer's judgement, and the owner's review against the vault is where spec conformance is checked, not CI. And the milestone ships a contour that has **never been stood up**: nothing on this branch has run against a real staging service or a real staging database.
  - **Errata (post-close correction, same branch, docs-only).** Two provenance markers this entry's own conventions call for were absent from the close-out commit, and are corrected in a follow-up commit **attributed to A2-CLOSE** — not a fifth packet, so the **Type** field above stands and the correction takes the same shape as the two pinned ruff-format fixups already attributed to A2-P2 and A2-P3. (i) `docs/execution-map.md`'s A2-P3 line gains `(pinned ruff-format fixup @ 63605ad)`, the rendering the **Changed (spine)** bullet above already claims for the P2 line "per the `M4-P4` precedent"; that commit was in history when the close-out was cut, so its absence was an omission rather than the sequencing constraint that keeps a packet from citing its own SHA. (ii) `docs/INVARIANTS.md` `M4-P4-INV-001`'s **Landed in** field now records the A2-P3 amendment its **Statement** and **Scope** already carried, matching how `M4-P2-INV-002` records the same narrowing — that field is the index the (e) upstream ADR-011 / ADR-014 annotation walk uses, and the ADR-014-bearing entry was the one not advertising its own amendment. Neither was a false statement, so (g)'s "none carries a residual falsehood" stands as written; both were silences where a stated convention speaks. **The Untouched bullet above describes the close-out commit**: this errata additionally touches `docs/INVARIANTS.md` (that one field of that one entry) and `docs/execution-map.md` (that one line ending), plus this bullet. Nothing else changes — no new invariant, no new decision entry (nothing here decides anything), **operability still none**, and the three observations judged acceptable at the milestone-decision review (the registry token's occurrences in this log, the project id in the two `/api` build-configs, and the A2-P1 hook-config plus contract-script header edits) stay untouched by design. The errata commit's own SHA is recorded nowhere, for the same no-self-SHA reason the `A2-CLOSE` map bullet carries none.
