## A3-DL-001 — A3-P1 same-origin `/api` proxy (= vault ADR-038 / ADR-007): production `/api` goes private, and the PWA container authenticates service-to-service with a metadata-minted ID token

- **Date.** 2026-07-28
- **Type.** Deploy-topology + container decision — A3-P1, the first packet of the A3 accounts-oauth-same-origin milestone. Build-config, nginx config, container entrypoint, one parity-suite guard and documentation: no product code, no schema, no migration, no `/api` route, and nothing under `app/index.html`, `app/m/`, `app/sw.js` or `app/icons/`. *(Namespace note: this log's "Entry format" header describes ids as `M{N}-DL-{NNN}`; `A3-DL-001` opens a dedicated namespace for the A3 milestone, following the `A0-DL` / `A1-DL` precedent for a milestone whose track letter is its own id. No id split of the `A2`/`L2` kind is needed here — packets are `A3-P{k}`, decisions `A3-DL-{NNN}`, invariants `A3-P{k}-INV-{NNN}`. Fresh per-namespace sequence (`001`); the format header is intentionally left unedited, mirroring `L2-DL-001` / `L1-DL-001` / `PWA-DL-001` / `B1-DL-018`.)*
- **Decision.** Six calls, in the order they bind.
  1. **Production `/api` deploys `--no-allow-unauthenticated`** (the explicit negative, not a deleted flag: dropping `--allow-unauthenticated` would leave an existing `allUsers` binding in place, whereas the negative form actively removes it). The service was already private *in fact*; this makes the build-config honest and keeps it that way.
  2. **The proxy is one `location ^~ /api/` block in `app/nginx.conf`, in the PWA container** — one container, not two. `^~` rather than a plain prefix is load-bearing: a plain `location /api/` loses to the file's existing `~* \.(js|css|…)$` regex location, so a future `/api/<name>.js` would be served as missing static instead of proxied.
  3. **The token mechanism is a `/bin/sh` entrypoint (`app/docker-entrypoint.sh`) that mints the ID token at container start from the metadata server with `audience` = the upstream service URL, polls for rotation, and reloads nginx only when the value actually changes.** The base image stays `nginx:alpine` and no package is added (busybox `wget`, and `envsubst`, are already there).
  4. **The entrypoint fails closed.** Configured upstream + no mintable token after the retry budget ⇒ `exit 1`, and the container never starts. Safe because the deploy is `--no-traffic --tag`: the revision takes zero users and the failure surfaces as a red Cloud Build.
  5. **`app/nginx.conf` keeps its filename and becomes an envsubst template** rendered at container start. The upstream URL and the PWA runtime service account arrive as Cloud Build trigger substitutions with empty defaults and a fail-fast Step 0 — the `api/cloudbuild.staging.yaml` convention, lifted verbatim in shape.
  6. **The PWA service account is pinned** (`_PWA_RUNTIME_SA`) to a dedicated least-privilege identity, explicitly **not** the project-default compute account, because that account is the grantee of `roles/run.invoker` on production `/api`.
- **Rationale.** PDR-002 says the showcase is the only entrance to the core; until this packet that was a statement about intent, because `/api` had its own public URL and the PWA had no `/api` location at all. The packet is what makes it structural. Everything on the client side was already written for it — `app/m/v1/core/kb-boot.js` is the only `fetch()` in the app and it is root-relative, `app/sw.js` has routed `/api/` network-first since PWA-DL-001, and `api/theygrow_api/main.py` bakes the `/api` prefix into the router precisely so the proxy needs no `root_path`. Only the server half was missing.
  The non-obvious part is (3), and it is worth recording why the *simplest-looking* design is the one that fails. Cloud Run throttles CPU between requests, so a background refresher can wake holding an expired token. The margins are what make a timer safe rather than the timer itself: identity tokens live ~1h, the poll is 5 min, and Cloud Run reaps an idle instance after ~15 min — so serving an expired token would require an instance to survive ~55 min without a single CPU slice while still alive, which requires traffic, which grants CPU. The metadata server also returns its cached token until near expiry, so polling costs almost nothing and an actual rotation (and therefore a reload) lands roughly hourly.
- **Alternatives considered.**
  1. **njs (`js_periodic` + `js_shared_dict_zone`), or OpenResty/Lua with a per-request `access_by_lua_block`.** The Lua variant is genuinely the most correct design — it is request-time and immune to the throttling question above. Rejected on cost of carriage: the official `nginx:alpine` image ships no njs module and Alpine's community build is ABI-incompatible with it, so njs means compiling a module into a static-file server; and OpenResty means replacing the base image of the production PWA for a robustness margin (2) already provides by arithmetic.
  2. **A second container** (Cloud Run multi-container sidecar serving the token over localhost). Rejected — a second image to build, version, tag and promote, for one HTTP call to a link-local address.
  3. **`--no-cpu-throttling` on the PWA service**, which would make any timer trivially correct. Rejected — it bills idle CPU continuously on a family-scale app.
  4. **Renaming `app/nginx.conf` to `nginx.conf.template`.** Rejected — that path is enumerated in four places (`AGENTS.md` §7, the never-scanned list in `scripts/check-contract-integrity.sh`, the `.pre-commit-config.yaml` excludes, and `app/tests/delivery-contract.spec.js`), so the rename forces an `AGENTS.md` edit *inside* a packet, which this project's operating rules push to milestone close.
  5. **Generating the whole `/api` block from the entrypoint script.** Rejected — it moves the proxy out of the reviewed, guard-parsed config file into a shell heredoc, where neither the drift guard nor a reviewer would see it.
  6. **A second `location = /_api_unconfigured` for the local/dev fallback.** Rejected — one more location for the drift guard to exempt, for a path that exists only off Cloud Run. The include file that carries the token carries the `return 503` instead, so a single file is the whole configured/unconfigured toggle.
  7. **Dropping `--allow-unauthenticated` rather than passing `--no-allow-unauthenticated`.** Rejected — see Decision (1).
  8. **Leaving the PWA runtime identity implicit** (the project-default compute account, which is what `docs/RUNBOOK.md` records the service as running under today). Rejected — `roles/run.invoker` on a project-shared account grants `/api` to every default-account workload in the project, which would substantially undo the packet. ADR-020's least-privilege staging runtime account is the governing precedent. The substitution forces an explicit choice without forcing a *new* account into existence in the repo.
- **Supersedes.** None. **Amends `M2-DL-002`**, which reassigned origin unification from the M2-P3 misrecording to **M5**: it lands here, in A3-P1, instead. Builds on `L1-DL-001` (the `--no-traffic --tag` gate this packet's smoke runs through, left byte-untouched), `L2-DL-001` (the substitution + Step-0-guard convention, lifted in shape from `api/cloudbuild.staging.yaml`) and `A1-DL-002` (the parity suite that now carries this packet's invariant). Cross-references vault **ADR-038** (the repo-side definition site for the private-`/api`-plus-metadata-token posture; the mechanism was this plan's call, not an owner fork), **ADR-007** (same-origin topology, no CORS to the front end), **ADR-020** (the promotion gate and the least-privilege runtime-account precedent) and **PDR-002** / **PDR-010**.
- **Effects.**
  - **Added:** `app/docker-entrypoint.sh` — renders `app/nginx.conf` from its template, mints and refreshes the `/api` ID token, fails closed, `exec`s nginx. It deliberately replaces the base image's own entrypoint rather than dropping a script into `/docker-entrypoint.d/`: that machinery's ordering and its sourced-vs-executed semantics for `.sh`/`.envsh` are undocumented base-image internals, and nothing it does (IPv6 listen rewriting, local resolver injection) applies to a config that hardcodes `listen 8080` and uses no `resolver`.
  - **Changed (live deploy path):** `api/cloudbuild.yaml` — `--allow-unauthenticated` → `--no-allow-unauthenticated`, plus the Step 3 comment, which asserted "no nginx proxy — that is M5". `app/nginx.conf` — one `location ^~ /api/` block; the file becomes an envsubst template (two placeholders). `app/Dockerfile` — the nginx COPY retargeted to `/etc/nginx/nginx.conf.template`, the entrypoint COPY added, `ENTRYPOINT` added; both COPY lines stay in the plain two-argument form because `shippedPaths()` in the ship-list guard fails closed on any COPY form it does not fully parse, and the `/bin/sh` invocation removes any dependence on a committed executable bit. `app/cloudbuild.yaml` — `substitutions:` (`_API_UPSTREAM_URL`, `_PWA_RUNTIME_SA`), a bash Step 0 fail-fast guard, `--service-account` and `--set-env-vars` on the deploy step; `--allow-unauthenticated` **stays**, because the PWA is the public front door. `app/.dockerignore` — its header enumerates the image's COPY list, which this packet extends; one line.
  - **Changed (RUNBOOK):** a new owner-run section **Same-origin `/api` proxy (A3-P1, owner-run)** carrying the strict ordering (least-privilege account + Cloud Build `actAs` → `roles/run.invoker` on production `/api` → both trigger substitutions → **merge** → tagged-revision smoke → promote), the four smoke assertions including the anonymous-`403` negative, the rollback asymmetry (service-level IAM is not undone by traffic pinning), and the two honesty notes. Plus: the "What runs today" paragraph and the `/api` local-dev section, both of which said origin unification was M5; the `/api` promotion smoke, whose step 4 named the same-origin proxy as its own extension trigger and whose `curl` now needs an identity token; the PWA and production-`/api` build-path bullets; and two new "Live-infra divergence" records (the PWA runtime account, and the upstream URL recorded as deliberately-not-recorded).
  - **Changed (parity suite):** `app/tests/delivery-contract.spec.js` — `^~ /api/` added to the location-drift `exempt` list (the parity server deliberately does not proxy; there is nothing to proxy to locally), plus the three config-shape assertions that enforce `A3-P1-INV-001`.
  - **Side-find, fixed in place: the location-drift guard was matching inside comments.** Its scan is a text match over the whole file and its `[^{]+?` crosses newlines, so any **comment** mentioning the word "location" was reported as a phantom location spanning everything up to the next real `{` — a false RED whose failure message names a chunk of prose rather than a location. This packet's proxy comment triggered it, which is how it surfaced; it has been latent since A1-P1 and would have fired on the next comment to use the word. The scan now strips `#` to end-of-line first. The stripping is a heuristic (it would also strip a `#` inside a quoted directive value, of which this file has none) and it is wrong in the harmless direction: it can only *under*-report a location, which the mirrored-rule assertions above it then catch. `A1-P1-INV-001`'s statement is unchanged — this repairs its enforcement, it does not narrow or widen what it enforces, so the entry takes no amendment marker.
  - **Operability: knobs at the container layer, and stated rather than skipped** (ADR-013 / contract §4.7). This packet introduces two qualitative knobs — `API_TOKEN_POLL_SECONDS` (default `300`) and `API_TOKEN_FETCH_MAX_ATTEMPTS` (default `5`, with a backoff companion) — and they land as named, `changed_in: A3-DL-001`-annotated, env-overridable constants at the top of `app/docker-entrypoint.sh`, **not** in `api/theygrow_api/parameters.py`. The reason is structural, not convenience: `parameters.py` is a Python module inside the `/api` image, read in-process by `RuntimeParameters` (`env_prefix="THEYGROW_PARAM_"`), and the consumer here is a `/bin/sh` loop in a **different container image** with no Python runtime and no import path to it — routing them through the typed surface would mean inventing a cross-container config channel this packet does not otherwise need. This is the same reasoning, in the same shape, that `app/sw.js` already records for `CACHE_VERSION` (`changed_in: PWA-DL-001`), so the packet follows an established in-repo precedent rather than opening a new exemption. `PARAMETERS_SCHEMA_VERSION` stays `4`. `_API_UPSTREAM_URL` and `_PWA_RUNTIME_SA` are **not** knobs — they are live identifiers, and their home is the trigger substitutions plus `docs/RUNBOOK.md`. **Signals: none, and adding one would be wrong** — `SignalSink` and `SIGNAL_TAXONOMY` live in the FastAPI process, which the proxy container cannot reach, so no emitter could be wired; a descriptor with `emitted_now=False` would turn `api/tests/test_signal_emitters.py::test_no_defined_not_emitted_kinds_remain_as_of_p4` red, since it asserts the defined-not-emitted set is exactly `set()`. Refresher failures surface as container stderr → Cloud Logging. No GA4 event.
  - **`docs/INVARIANTS.md`:** adds `A3-P1-INV-001` — no `Access-Control-*` header on any location, and the `/api` proxy setting `Authorization` from the container-minted token variable rather than forwarding the client's. Enforced by `app/tests/delivery-contract.spec.js`.
  - **§4-safe.** No child or family data touched, and no new *third-party* egress: the two new hops are PWA→`/api` inside one GCP project and a link-local metadata call, and the proxied surface is still only `GET /api/health`. No credential, host, user, password, connection string or live identifier enters the repository — the token is minted at runtime and never written anywhere but a container-local include file. The entrypoint's diagnostics are counts and outcomes only: never a token, never a request path, never a body. `app/nginx.conf` keeps `access_log /dev/stdout` unchanged, and the one proxied path today carries no query string and no personal data.
  - **Accepted residual risk — no `resolver`, recorded rather than left silent.** `app/nginx.conf` deliberately declares no `resolver`, so the upstream hostname is resolved **once**, at config load. A change to the upstream's address is picked up only on a new revision or a container restart, not live. Accepted for a stable Cloud Run service URL; the fix would be an `http`-level directive, and `A1-DL-008` records that the delivery-contract drift guard sees only `server`-level ones — so it would be an unguarded directive added to buy a margin nothing in this deployment needs. Every directive this packet adds stays inside the `server` block for that reason. Noted in the proxy block itself and in **Same-origin `/api` proxy**.
  - **Carried forward to A3-P2, stated here so it is a decision rather than a discovery.** The proxy **overwrites** any `Authorization` header the client sent — that is what makes the upstream call a service-to-service one. Therefore A3-P2 sessions must be **cookie-borne (same-site)**, which is the intended posture anyway and which this block does not obstruct: cookies pass through untouched. A browser-supplied bearer token cannot reach `/api` through this proxy at all; it would need a different header, and the decision belongs at this block.
  - **Validation-honesty caveat** (M4-CLOSE discipline). What the local gates prove: that the committed config has the shape claimed (`A3-P1-INV-001`), that the ship-list and location-drift guards still hold against the amended `app/Dockerfile` and `app/nginx.conf`, and that the real CI gate (`pre-commit run --all-files`) is green. **They prove nothing about whether the proxy works.** The metadata server does not exist off Cloud Run, so the ID-token mint, the `audience`↔service-URL match, the `roles/run.invoker` decision and the anonymous-`403` on the direct `/api` URL are established **only** by the owner-run smoke on the tagged revision, before promotion — step 5 of the new RUNBOOK section. A green local run means the mechanics are wired, not that the live authentication succeeds.
  - **Deferred to A3-CLOSE (contract and spine reconciliation; contract edits stay out of packets).** Three files still say origin unification is M5 — `docs/product/BuildPlan.md`, `docs/product/TechSpec.md` and `docs/execution-map.md` — and `AGENTS.md` §7 plus the never-scanned enumeration in `scripts/check-contract-integrity.sh` do not yet name `app/docker-entrypoint.sh` as a live-deploy path (nor, consequently, does the guardrail-list sentence at the end of `docs/RUNBOOK.md` "Live-infra divergence", which describes what the contract files say and would become false if changed alone). The three RUNBOOK occurrences **are** fixed here, because this packet edits that file. Recorded now so milestone close does not have to rediscover it.
  - **Deferred to later A3 packets:** the packet numbering recorded here was provisional and the milestone landed a four-packet shape instead — the database and the deployed service's wiring to it (**A3-P2**, `A3-DL-002`); the OAuth flow, sessions, cookies, account entities and the wait-list (**A3-P3**); the auth entry point and account screen in the SPA (**A3-P4**). No `CACHE_VERSION` bump and no service-worker change: nothing precached changed, and `app/sw.js` has routed `/api/` network-first since PWA-DL-001.

---

## A3-DL-002 — A3-P2 production `/api` gets its database (= vault ADR-039 / ADR-008 / ADR-038): the store is created from scratch, the deployed service is wired to it behind a fail-fast guard, and `GET /api/health/ready` is the first route that connects

- **Date.** 2026-07-29
- **Type.** Infrastructure-enablement + first DB-touching route decision — A3-P2, the second packet of the A3 accounts-oauth-same-origin milestone. Build-config, one new route, the engine/pool layer, the operability surfaces, tests and documentation: **no schema, no migration, no new Alembic revision, no OAuth, and nothing under `app/**`**. *(Namespace note: continues the `A3-DL` namespace opened by `A3-DL-001` — packets `A3-P{k}`, decisions `A3-DL-{NNN}`, invariants `A3-P{k}-INV-{NNN}`; the "Entry format" header is intentionally left unedited, per the `L1-DL-001` / `PWA-DL-001` / `L2-DL-001` precedent.)*
- **Decision.** Seven calls, in the order they bind.
  1. **The Cloud SQL instance is created from scratch, to vault ADR-039's parameters.** There was no instance in this project — none, and no Secret Manager secret, no VPC connector and no environment on `theygrow-api`. So this packet is *create the store, then attach, then migrate*, and `alembic upgrade head` is the **last** owner step, not the first. Parameters are the owner's, taken as given: PostgreSQL 16 + `vector`; europe-west1; Enterprise, zonal; public IPv4 with an **empty** authorized-networks list; daily backups **plus** PITR; one instance for all contours.
  2. **`api/cloudbuild.yaml` gains four flags behind a Step 0 guard**, in the shape `api/cloudbuild.staging.yaml` established: `--add-cloudsql-instances`, `--set-secrets DATABASE_URL`, `--service-account`, `--max-instances`. The three live identifiers arrive as trigger substitutions with empty defaults; `MUST_MATCH` is kept. The guard is load-bearing for the reason the staging config already records: an empty `--add-cloudsql-instances` parses to an empty **list**, so the deploy succeeds with no attachment at all — and on this service `/api/health` needs no environment and would stay green over it.
  3. **A dedicated production runtime service account (`theygrow-api-runtime`), which was not deferrable.** The project has exactly one identity today: the default compute account, carrying `roles/editor` and shared by Cloud Build and both runtimes. Granting it `roles/cloudsql.client` would open the family database to every workload in the project. This closes the asymmetry `docs/RUNBOOK.md` had recorded as "accidental, not designed" since M2-P3.
  4. **`GET /api/health/ready` is a NEW route, not a key added to `/api/health`.** Liveness must not acquire a database dependency, and its exact body is asserted in three places at once (`test_health.py`, the eval runner's `assert_staging_live`, both promotion smokes). Readiness constructs `Settings`, borrows from the process-cached engine, runs `SELECT 1`, and returns a fixed status enum — `{"status":"ready"}` / `503 {"status":"unavailable"}` — and nothing else on either path.
  5. **The failure path is mute in the body and bounded in the signal.** No instance name, no driver version, no timings, no exception text in the response; the `readiness.probe` signal carries `outcome` plus a **closed** `failure_class` (`none` | `config_invalid` | `connect_failed` | `query_failed`) derived from the control-flow branch and never from an exception, because psycopg/SQLAlchemy connection errors carry the host, the user and the socket path. This is what makes a red probe diagnosable at all: production's socket URL form is exercised for the first time by the owner smoke, with no staging contour to rehearse it on.
  6. **Two engine lifecycles, deliberately not one function.** `create_served_engine` / `served_engine` (process-cached, bounded pool) for the deployed service; `create_db_engine` / `get_engine` untouched — same signature, same per-run engine — for the offline CLIs and `alembic/env.py`, which dispose theirs at exit and must not inherit a long-lived pool. **The arithmetic is the decision**: `db_pool_size` (2) x `--max-instances` (2) = 4 connections, hard-capped by `db_max_overflow = 0`, against an application-role `CONNECTION LIMIT` of 10 — leaving 6 for the owner's Auth Proxy sessions and migration runs. Raising `--max-instances` requires raising the role's limit **first**.
  7. **The trigger substitutions are set AFTER merge**, inverting A3-P1's ordering. `MUST_MATCH` fails every build on `main` for a key the config on `main` does not declare, so setting them early breaks `main` until the packet lands. The consequence — the `/api` build failing at Step 0 the moment the packet merges — is labelled *designed, not a defect* in the RUNBOOK, following the two existing precedents.
- **Rationale.** A3-P3 cannot land without a reachable database, and a database the deployed service has never opened a connection to is a claim, not a fact. `--set-secrets` validates only that the secret exists and is readable — never that its value is a usable URL, never that the socket resolves. The readiness route is what converts "declared wiring" into "observed wiring", and it is the cheapest possible such route: one `SELECT 1`, no schema dependency, so it works before the migration as well as after.
- **Alternatives considered.**
  1. **Fold readiness into `GET /api/health`** — *Rejected.* Its exact body is asserted by `test_health.py`, by `evals/runner.py::assert_staging_live` and by both promotion smokes, so any added key is a three-way break; and more fundamentally it would mean a database outage makes the service look *down*, destroying the rollback signal (A3-P2-INV-001 (a)).
  2. **Reuse the default compute service account** — *Rejected*, per ADR-038 as amended: one shared `roles/editor` identity means a database grant is a project-wide grant.
  3. **Put diagnostics in the response body** (instance name, driver, timings, exception text) — *Rejected* on two independent grounds: §4, and the fact that the route is reachable anonymously from the internet through the same-origin proxy with no rate limiting in front of it. The bounded `failure_class` in the signal gives an operator the same discrimination without the exposure.
  4. **Add pooling parameters to `create_db_engine`** as defaulted keyword arguments instead of a second factory — *Rejected.* One function serving two opposite lifecycles is how a short-lived CLI ends up holding a pool open against a `CONNECTION LIMIT` sized for the service.
  5. **`--max-instances` as a fourth trigger substitution** (the approved scope's wording) — *Rejected after escalation, owner-confirmed.* It is not a live identifier; an empty value makes `gcloud` error loudly rather than deploy silently wrong, so the guard buys nothing; and it is one of the two terms in the connection arithmetic, which must be reviewable in the repo next to `db_pool_size` rather than in a console field. `api/cloudbuild.staging.yaml` already hardcodes its `2`.
  6. **`pool_pre_ping` as a knob** — *Rejected.* Off, a server-recycled connection makes readiness report a false negative. That is a correctness setting, not a tuning choice, so it is hardcoded `True` and documented as such.
  7. **Private IP / a VPC connector** — *Rejected by ADR-039*, to avoid a VPC layer and keep the owner-run procedures workable. The empty authorized-networks list is what pays for that choice, which is why it is written as load-bearing rather than as a setting.
- **Supersedes.** None. **Amends `A3-DL-001`** on two points: its provisional A3 packet numbering (OAuth was listed as A3-P2; the milestone landed database → OAuth → SPA as P2/P3/P4), and its premise that production `/api` carries no environment. Builds on `L2-DL-001` (the substitution + Step-0-guard convention, lifted in shape from `api/cloudbuild.staging.yaml`), `L1-DL-001` (the `--no-traffic --tag` gate, left byte-untouched) and `M4-DL-002` (the operability surfaces this packet extends). Cross-references vault **ADR-039** (the instance parameters, owner-gated 2026-07-29 — PITR because the family diary has no source to rebuild from and it cannot be enabled retroactively; private IP explicitly rejected), **ADR-008 §1/§4** (one managed Postgres as SoT; AlloyDB later as a data migration, not an application rewrite), **ADR-038 as amended** (private at the internet boundary but not at the project boundary — hence the dedicated runtime account here, and the standing debt of splitting identities with the default account narrowed last), **ADR-020** (the L1 promotion gate this packet's smoke runs through), **PDR-009** (EU anchor and the DPIA draft), **PDR-017** (the owner-run procedures that will live on this database, and the reason workstation access via the Auth Proxy must stay practical), **ADR-013 / contract §4.7** (operability) and **PDR-002**.
- **Effects.**
  - **Added:** `api/theygrow_api/db/readiness.py` — `probe_readiness` (connect leg / query leg, distinguished so the emitted class is actionable), `check_readiness` (the environment-facing entry, where a missing or unusable `DATABASE_URL` becomes `config_invalid`), the closed `FAILURE_CLASSES` set, and one emission point. `api/tests/test_readiness.py`.
  - **Changed (live deploy path):** `api/cloudbuild.yaml` — a `substitutions:` block (`_CLOUDSQL_INSTANCE`, `_DB_SECRET_NAME`, `_API_RUNTIME_SA`), a bash Step 0 fail-fast guard, and four flags on the deploy step. `--no-allow-unauthenticated`, `--no-traffic` and `--tag` are byte-untouched. The Step 3 comment that asserted "No `--set-env-vars` / `--set-secrets` / `--add-cloudsql-instances`" is rewritten rather than left to rot.
  - **Changed (product):** `api/theygrow_api/db/engine.py` — `create_served_engine` + `lru_cache`d `served_engine` added; `engine_url`, `create_db_engine` and `get_engine` unchanged in signature and behaviour. `api/theygrow_api/main.py` — the readiness route, and `_configure_logging()` (see below). `api/theygrow_api/config.py` — one docstring statement, which claimed no route constructs `Settings`.
  - **`--max-instances 2` is a narrowing, recorded as a change rather than left to be discovered from the console.** The live `theygrow-api` service ran with `maxScale 20`; this caps it at **2**, a tenfold reduction in burst ceiling. It is deliberate — it is the other half of the `CONNECTION LIMIT` arithmetic, and a service whose entire surface is two health routes has no case for twenty instances — but it is a real capacity decision, not a default, and it is named in `api/cloudbuild.yaml`, in the RUNBOOK step that sets it, and under "Live-infra divergence".
  - **Named limitation carried forward: `roles/cloudsql.client` is project-scoped.** It cannot be bound to a single instance, so the runtime account can reach **every** Cloud SQL instance this project will ever have. Acceptable while exactly one exists; **not** acceptable the moment a second appears, at which point the narrowing options are separate projects or Cloud SQL IAM database authentication with per-database grants. Recorded in the RUNBOOK grant step so the next instance does not silently inherit access.
  - **Operability: in-process this time, so the typed surfaces are genuinely available** (ADR-013 / contract §4.7) — unlike A3-P1, whose knobs lived in a container with no Python runtime. **Five knobs**, all in `api/theygrow_api/parameters.py` as `runtime` scope with `changed_in: A3-DL-002` and env-overridable via `THEYGROW_PARAM_`: `db_pool_size` (2), `db_max_overflow` (0), `db_pool_timeout_seconds` (2), `db_pool_recycle_seconds` (1800), `db_connect_timeout_seconds` (5). **`PARAMETERS_SCHEMA_VERSION` 4 → 5** — a knob-count change, not a value change. `pool_pre_ping` is deliberately not a knob (see Alternatives 6). **One signal kind, declared AND emitted in the same packet**: `READINESS_PROBE` (`readiness.probe`), fields `("outcome", "failure_class", "latency_ms")`, `producing_stage="A3-P2"`, `emitted_now=True`, emitter wired in `db/readiness.py` and driven in `api/tests/test_signal_emitters.py` — the defined-not-emitted set stays exactly `set()`. Payload is two bounded labels plus a timing; both label keys join `_LABEL_KEYS` in `test_signals.py`. No GA4 event.
  - **Side-find, fixed in place: the served process configured no logging at all.** Every offline CLI's `main()` calls `logging.basicConfig` + `install_pii_redaction`; the FastAPI app called neither, which was harmless while it emitted nothing and became a defect the moment it emitted a signal — `readiness.probe` would have gone to a logger with no handler and reached nothing, making the RUNBOOK's "read the failure class in Cloud Logging" instruction false, and `LoggingSignalSink`'s §4 guarantee ("emits through the PII-guarded logging boundary") vacuous in the one process that serves users. `create_app()` now calls `_configure_logging()` once (`lru_cache`), at a **fixed** `INFO` — deliberately not `Settings.log_level`, which would hand liveness a configuration dependency and break A3-P2-INV-001 (a). Verified end-to-end under uvicorn: the line appears, uvicorn's access logs are not duplicated, and the redaction filter is asserted present by `test_readiness.py`.
  - **§4-safe.** No child or family data is touched and no new *third-party* egress is created: Cloud SQL is in-project and in-region, and the only new hop is a Unix socket inside the Cloud Run sandbox. The response body carries a fixed status enum on both paths; the signal carries two closed enums and a float; the failure path logs **nothing else at all** — the `except` clauses bind no exception and re-raise none, so there is no path by which connection-error text can reach a sink. Asserted by a needle sweep over the body, the payload and the logging boundary, driven by fakes whose exception text embeds a full connection string. No credential, host, user, password or connection string enters the repository.
  - **PDR-009 record.** This packet is the moment **server-side reachability of family data first exists** in this system: **2026-07-29, A3-P2**. Nothing is stored yet and no route reads family rows — the database is empty until the owner's migration, and `SELECT 1` reads nothing — but the reachability itself is the change, and it is dated here rather than left to be inferred from a commit later.
  - **`docs/INVARIANTS.md`:** adds `A3-P2-INV-001` — liveness environment-free; readiness closed and mute, with the `failure_class` enum asserted **closed** so no exception-derived string can reach a payload.
  - **Changed (RUNBOOK):** a new owner-run section **Production database enablement (A3-P2, owner-run)** — fifteen strictly-ordered steps (create instance → create database → `CREATE EXTENSION vector` as superuser → application role with `CONNECTION LIMIT` → secret with the socket form and its `@/` trap → runtime SA + two grants + Cloud Build `actAs` → **merge** (designed Step 0 failure) → substitutions → re-run → mandatory backup → `alembic upgrade head` → tagged-revision smoke with the `failure_class` triage table → promote → rollback), with the empty-authorized-networks rule and the `status.url` audience rule written as load-bearing. Plus six corrected statements this change falsified (the build-path bullet, the staging honesty note, Restore steps 1 and 5, the local-dev `/api` section, the dev-stack bullet) and the new "Live-infra divergence" records (runtime account, secret name, max-instances, database-wired marker, the instance block and its ADR-039 parameters).
  - **Honesty corrections beyond the enumerated list, owner-confirmed.** Two statements this packet renders conspicuously false in a file it already edits: the project runs **two** Cloud Run services, not three, and the **staging contour has not been raised** — `api/cloudbuild.staging.yaml` and its procedure are committed, but no staging service, trigger or database exists. Both are corrected as statements; a full reconciliation of the Staging-contour section is an A3-CLOSE item.
  - **Validation-honesty caveat** (M4-CLOSE discipline). What the local gates prove: the route's shape on both paths, the closed enum, the §4 needle sweeps, the pool ceiling, the guard script's behaviour when run with empty and populated variables, and green `pre-commit run --all-files`. Verified additionally against a real Postgres and a real uvicorn process, including the failure path with an unusable socket-form URL. **They prove nothing about the production wiring** — not the socket form against a real Cloud SQL mount, not the secret's contents, not the attachment, not `roles/cloudsql.client`, not the `--service-account` pin. CI's pgvector service is TCP. **And unlike A3-P1 there is no staging contour to rehearse on, because none exists**: the owner smoke on the tagged revision (step 13) is the first and only exercise of the socket URL form anywhere.
  - **Deferred to A3-P3:** the entire OAuth flow — OIDC endpoints, state/PKCE/nonce, account entities **and their migration**, the cookie session, the pending/approved status model, the owner-run approval and 12-month retention procedures, the internal registration queue. This packet adds **no** Alembic revision; the owner's migration run brings production to the existing head (`0003`).
  - **Deferred to A3-P4:** the SPA auth entry and account screen. No `app/` change here at all, no `CACHE_VERSION` bump, nothing precached changed — `location ^~ /api/` already proxies the full URI, so `/api/health/ready` is same-origin-reachable with no delivery-side change.
  - **Deferred, standing:** build-once-promote-the-digest (`L2-DL-001`); automated eval gating (`L2-DL-003`); the A3-CLOSE spine reconciliation (`A3-DL-001`), which now also carries the Staging-contour section rewrite; raising the staging contour, a second instance, private IP and anything VPC; and the owner-side infra outside this repo — the Cloudflare bot-rule decision for `/api/*` (measured after promotion), the www redirect restoration, and the dedicated-SA migration for Cloud Build and the PWA.

---

## A3-DL-003 — A3 A3-CLOSE: the milestone is closed by what it contains, not by the title it was opened with; the accounts half becomes A3b, an owner-run ordering error is corrected, and the debt that survives is settled in one place

- **Date.** 2026-07-29
- **Type.** Decision record — milestone close-out, A3-P3, the third and last packet of the A3 milestone. Ledger, contract, spine and docs reconciliation, plus two owner-confirmed zero-behaviour edits (a hook-exclude regex and a build-config comment): no product code, no schema, no migration, no route, no test, no `PARAMETERS_SCHEMA_VERSION` bump, nothing under `app/**`. The P1–P2 ladder is frozen as shipped. *(Namespace note: continues the `A3-DL` namespace opened by `A3-DL-001` — packets `A3-P{k}`, decisions `A3-DL-{NNN}`, invariants `A3-P{k}-INV-{NNN}`; the "Entry format" header is intentionally left unedited, per the `L2-DL-004` / `L1-DL-001` / `PWA-DL-001` precedent. The packet id `A3-P3` is reused for this close-out; `A3-DL-001` had provisionally reserved it for the OAuth flow, which decision (a) below moves out of the milestone entirely.)*
- **Decision.** Close milestone A3 in the repo ledger, record the boundary change that redefined it mid-flight, and settle every item the two A3 packets routed here — each one landed in this packet or re-deferred with a stated reason, none silently dropped:

  a. **The milestone boundary changed, and the closing docs describe A3 by what it actually contains.** A3 was opened as `accounts-oauth-same-origin`. By owner gate on **2026-07-29** it is **same-origin transport + production storage** — packets A3-P1 (`14907ce`) and A3-P2 (`1f2846e`) — and Google OAuth plus the SPA auth entry become a separate milestone, **A3b**, with the rest of the roadmap numbering unshifted. The provisional A3-P3 / A3-P4 numbering `A3-DL-001` recorded, and `A3-DL-002` already corrected once, is superseded by the split rather than renumbered again.

  **The reason is worth recording, because it is a sequencing judgement and not a scope trim.** Four packets, none of them ever exercised against the live project, would have reached their first live execution simultaneously — a private `/api` behind a new proxy, a database that had never been connected to, an OAuth flow, and an SPA entry point — with **no staging contour to rehearse any of it on**, because the contour has never been raised (f)(7). And the Cloudflare bot-rule fork for `/api/*` (f)(1) would then have been decided under the pressure of a broken sign-in rather than on infrastructure, at the moment when the cheapest-looking option is whichever one restores the flow fastest. Splitting the milestone buys the fork a calm decision and gives each half its own promotion.

  b. **`A3-DL-001` and `A3-DL-002` are left byte-untouched; this entry amends them.** Both describe A3 as "the A3 accounts-oauth-same-origin milestone" in their **Type** field, and `A3-DL-001` Effects lists A3-P3 / A3-P4 as OAuth and SPA packets. Those statements were true when written and are the record of what was believed then; the log's own mechanism for a later correction is amendment by reference — `A3-DL-002` amends `A3-DL-001` exactly this way. Rewriting them would edit history to match a later decision. The one **non-ledger** copy of the old title, in `docs/INVARIANTS.md` `A3-P1-INV-001`'s namespace note, **is** corrected in place ("the accounts-oauth-same-origin milestone" → "the A3 milestone"), because that file is a live contract file rather than a ledger, its own convention is in-place amendment, and the phrase is a milestone name, not part of the invariant.

  c. **The A3-P1 owner-run ordering is corrected, and it was an error rather than a drift.** `docs/RUNBOOK.md` "Same-origin `/api` proxy" prescribed setting both PWA trigger substitutions **before** merging. That is wrong for the same reason A3-P2's section already states correctly: Cloud Build's `MUST_MATCH` option is kept deliberately in `app/cloudbuild.yaml`, so a trigger supplying a key the build-config on `main` does not declare fails **every** PWA build until the packet lands — setting them early breaks `main`. The ordering is now `1 SA + Cloud Build actAs → 2 roles/run.invoker → 3 merge → 4 substitutions → 5 re-run the PWA build → 6 smoke → 7 promote`, with the reason stated inline so the next reader does not re-derive it, and the Step 0 failure on merge relabelled from "if the order slips" to the designed, expected outcome — the `A3-P2` wording. **Steps 1–2 stay before merge** and were never wrong: merging flips service-level IAM immediately, and a promoted PWA revision without the invoker binding `403`s every `/api/*` request to real users. Two cross-references that called the two orderings "the inverse" of one another are corrected with it — `docs/RUNBOOK.md`'s build-path bullet, and the header comment in `api/cloudbuild.yaml` (see (e)). **Two pointers in `A3-DL-001` are amended here rather than edited there**, per (b): its Effects bullet renders the ordering as "→ both trigger substitutions → **merge** →", which is now "→ **merge** → both trigger substitutions → re-run →"; and its validation caveat cites the owner smoke as "step 5 of the new RUNBOOK section", which is now **step 6**.

  d. **The `_API_UPSTREAM_URL` / token-audience rule exists exactly once.** The full statement — read it from `gcloud run services describe … --format='value(status.url)'`, because the service answers on more than one URL (a deterministic per-project hostname, a legacy hash-based one, and per-revision `sha-` tags), they are **not** interchangeable when the same string is simultaneously the `proxy_pass` target and the ID-token `audience`, and Cloud Run rejects a token whose `aud` is not exactly the URL being called — is canonical in **Same-origin `/api` proxy**, step 4. The one competing restatement, under "Live-infra divergence", is replaced by a pointer to it. A rule whose failure mode is a `403` indistinguishable from a missing IAM binding cannot be allowed to exist in two versions that could drift apart.

  e. **Two edits outside the docs boundary, both owner-confirmed, both zero-behaviour** — recorded as scope extensions rather than as silent widening:
  1. **`.pre-commit-config.yaml` is the fourth copy of the live-deploy-path enumeration, and the only one where the omission was a live hazard.** A3-P1 added a shipped `app/` file and did not extend the two rewriting-hook excludes, so `app/docker-entrypoint.sh` sat inside `trailing-whitespace` / `end-of-file-fixer`'s reach and the header's claim that "every shipped `app/` file is excluded" was false. Latent — the file is already normalized, so both hooks are no-ops on it — which is exactly why it had to be fixed before an edit made it active. Two regex additions and one comment sentence.
  2. **`api/cloudbuild.yaml`'s header comment asserted the ordering in (c) is "the INVERSE of A3-P1's".** After (c) they are the same, so the comment would have shipped false and the repo and the RUNBOOK would again prescribe opposite orderings — the precise failure (c) exists to end. Comment-only; every executable line, flag and substitution is byte-untouched. The governing precedent is `A1-DL-008`, which fixed three shipped comments a milestone falsified at close.

  f. **The debt that survives this milestone, collected in one place, with what would close each.** Items 1, 3 and 7 already had an address in `A3-DL-002`'s "Deferred, standing" bullet and are promoted here to a settled list; items 2, 4, 5 and 6 have had **no** repo footprint until now and enter the ledger for the first time. None is softened:
  1. **The Cloudflare bot-rule fork for `/api/*` is open, and it is all-or-nothing.** Bot Fight Mode cannot be scoped by path on the current plan, so the three branches are: disable it entirely; buy Pro for a scoped skip rule, which moves the free/paid boundary; or measure the real interference and live with it. **Closed by:** deciding after promotion, on the observed `Content-Type` of proxied `/api` responses — the measurement, not the argument, is the missing input. Nothing is decided here.
  2. **The OAuth consent screen is in Testing, so open sign-up is a decision and not yet a behaviour.** While it stays in Testing only listed test users can complete the flow, which makes PDR-017's Stage 0 access conditions inoperative regardless of what the product intends. **Closed by:** publishing the consent screen — which requires a **privacy-policy page on the apex domain**, and no such page exists. Two dependent steps, in that order.
  3. **The www redirect rule is disabled and `www` answers `525` — an open production regression**, not a pending improvement. **Closed by:** restoring the redirect and confirming the TLS handshake the `525` reports; both are owner-side edge configuration outside this repo.
  4. **Page Rules are a sunsetting mechanism.** The redirect in item 3 rides one. **Closed by:** migrating to Redirect Rules, which is owed independently of item 3 and should not be discovered when the sunset lands.
  5. **The apex managed-certificate renewal risk behind proxying DNS is neither confirmed nor cleared.** A managed certificate whose validation path runs through a proxying resolver can fail to renew silently. **Closed by:** establishing whether this deployment is exposed at all — the honest state is "unknown", and treating unknown as safe is how a certificate expires on a weekend.
  6. **Whether to record the apex domain in `docs/RUNBOOK.md` is an open owner decision, and this packet deliberately does not take it.** The repository is temporarily public, so a live identifier lands in the RUNBOOK only when the owner decides it should (the standing "Live-infra divergence" rule). **Consequence, stated so it is a choice and not an oversight:** items 2–5 are recorded here **without naming the domain**, and the RUNBOOK gains no ingress/edge section at all, because that section cannot be written without the name. **Closed by:** the owner deciding, either way.
  7. **The identity split is the oldest of these and is narrowed last.** Cloud Build and the PWA still run as the project-default compute service account, which carries `roles/editor`; ADR-038 as amended calls production `/api` private at the internet boundary but explicitly **not** at the project boundary while that remains true. A3-P2 pinned a dedicated account for `/api`'s runtime; the other two branches are untouched. **Closed by:** a dedicated least-privilege account for each, with the default account narrowed only after both have moved.

  g. **Nothing in this milestone has been executed against the live project, and the closing docs say so rather than implying otherwise.** Both packets ship build-config, code and an owner-run procedure; neither has merged. No Cloud SQL instance, database, application role or `DATABASE_URL` secret exists, and no runtime service account has been created. Statements that had drifted into asserting otherwise are corrected: `docs/RUNBOOK.md`'s "Live-infra divergence" Cloud SQL block (which read "created in A3-P2"), the two **Restore / disaster recovery** statements claiming the instance has hosted `theygrow_staging` "since A2-P1", the PITR line (a decision as of instance creation, not a present fact), and the production `/api` records for the runtime account, secret, ingress and database wiring — all now phrased as what the build-config declares and what the procedure will create.

  h. **The Staging contour section is rewritten end to end, not marked.** A3-P2 could only land a "not raised" banner over a section whose body still read as description. The whole section now reads as a procedure to execute: an explicit state block enumerating what exists (the build-config `api/cloudbuild.staging.yaml`, and the procedure) against what does not (service, trigger, database, role, secret, runtime account, seed run, eval run), and a second block stating that its instance does not exist either. Steps 1–12 are **kept**: they are still the correct executable handoff, and their four safety properties — pre-creating `vector` as superuser, creating the role in SQL rather than via `gcloud`, revoking `PUBLIC`'s implicit `CONNECT` on production, and **verifying the refusal by observation** — are the milestone's actual product at A2. Deleting the section until the contour is raised would lose them. Two body statements the A3 packets falsified are corrected with it: production is no longer `--allow-unauthenticated` (the ingress honesty note now contrasts the *proxied* production path with staging's direct one), and the shared-instance consequences are conditional on both databases existing. The `/api` promotion gate's eval precondition gains the fact that it **cannot be satisfied today at all**, because there is nothing to run the eval against.

  i. **No `docs/INVARIANTS.md` entry, and no amendment to the two A3 ones.** `AGENTS.md` §10 makes the file enforced-only and a close-out enforces nothing new — the `L2-DL-004` (g) / `A1-DL-008` disposition. `A3-P1-INV-001` and `A3-P2-INV-001` were re-read against the shipped tree in this packet; neither carries a residual falsehood, and the only edit to the file is the milestone-name phrase in (b).

  j. **No new **Current** track is set.** The roadmap queues A3b after A3, but recording the split is this packet's job and opening the next milestone is a separate owner act. The map keeps the standing open next-milestone decision at the M5 line — the `L2-DL-004` (h) / `A1-DL-008` disposition, for the same reason: inventing a successor here would be a planning decision this packet has no authority to make.

  k. **Re-deferred, with reasons — named so none is lost.** All are false or stale statements this milestone did **not** create: the broader `AGENTS.md` M1-era refresh (§6's milestone map has never named a non-spine track, §7 is still framed around M1 as the live milestone); `docs/RUNTIME-INVARIANTS.md`'s "current M1-M5 ladder" scope paragraph, which no A-lane track appears in; `docs/product/BuildPlan.md`'s M1-through-M5 framing, which carries no A-lane section by design; `README.md`; and `docs/INVARIANTS.md`'s "Entry format" footer, which demands a commit SHA no entry carries. Each was re-deferred by `A1-DL-008` (g) or `L2-DL-004` (i) on the same reasoning and is not reopened: "while we are in there" is how a bounded close-out becomes an unscoped contract refresh with no review boundary.
- **Rationale.** A milestone that changed shape mid-flight is the one most likely to leave its documentation describing a plan nobody is executing any more. Three separate readers — the spine, the RUNBOOK and the ledger — would otherwise have said A3 was about accounts, that origin unification was still ahead in M5, and that a staging contour and a Cloud SQL instance existed. The close-out is the only bounded place where all three can be made to agree, and the packet discipline that keeps contract edits out of packets only pays if the deferred list is settled somewhere, in full, in one place — otherwise "deferred to A3-CLOSE" is a synonym for "dropped". The debt list in (f) is the same argument applied to what cannot be fixed here: an open production regression and an all-or-nothing paid-tier fork are worth more written down and unresolved than resolved quietly by whoever hits them first.
- **No dedicated ADR by design at the repo layer.** The close-out records a boundary change the owner made and reconciles documents; it decides nothing architectural. The milestone's two architectural decisions have vault ADRs already (ADR-038 as amended, ADR-039), and the boundary change itself is a roadmap revision, not an ADR.
- **Alternatives considered.**
  1. **Close with no decision-log entry**, folding the close into the two existing A3 entries. Rejected — folding has no home once a milestone has more than one entry, and this close carries at least five real judgements ((a), (b), (c), (e), (f)), each of which the log exists to make checkable. The boundary change in particular is the kind of thing that becomes untraceable within a month.
  2. **Rewrite `A3-DL-001` / `A3-DL-002`'s Type fields** so the log reads consistently. Rejected — see (b). It edits history to match a later decision, and the log's own convention is amendment by reference.
  3. **Fix only the two enumeration sites the handoff named** (`AGENTS.md` §7 and `scripts/check-contract-integrity.sh`), leaving the RUNBOOK's guardrail sentence and `.pre-commit-config.yaml`. Rejected — `A3-DL-001` itself warns the RUNBOOK sentence "would become false if changed alone", and the `.pre-commit-config.yaml` gap is a live hazard, not a documentation asymmetry. Consistency across all four is the point of enumerating at all.
  4. **Leave the A3-P1 ordering and add a caveat** rather than reordering the steps. Rejected — the two sections would then prescribe opposite orders for the same mechanism, which is how the error survives a second reading. It survived one already.
  5. **Delete the Staging contour section** until the contour is raised. Rejected — see (h). Its four safety properties are the A2 milestone's deliverable and would have to be rediscovered.
  6. **Add a Cloudflare / ingress section to `docs/RUNBOOK.md`** carrying items (f)(1)–(f)(5) at their proper operational address, with the domain as a placeholder. Rejected — see (f)(6). It pre-empts an open owner decision, and a placeholder ingress section invites someone to fill it in without the decision having been made.
  7. **Set A3b as the **Current** track in `docs/execution-map.md`**, since the roadmap already queues it. Rejected — see (j).
  8. **Record the debts as a `docs/INVARIANTS.md` entry**, since (f)(3) is an open production regression. Rejected — see (i). Nothing enforces any of them in code, and claiming them in an enforced-only file is the wish-list drift `AGENTS.md` §10 exists to prevent.
- **Supersedes.** None. Discharges the close-out deferrals recorded by `A3-DL-001` ("Deferred to A3-CLOSE") and `A3-DL-002` ("Deferred, standing"), and closes the milestone opened by `A3-DL-001`. **Amends `A3-DL-001`** on its milestone name and on its provisional A3-P3 / A3-P4 packet numbering — both left byte-untouched, per (b) — and **amends `A3-DL-002`** on its milestone name and on the same numbering it had already corrected once. Builds on `A1-DL-008` and `L2-DL-004` (the close-out shape, the batched-contract-edit venue, the fixed-versus-deferred boundary rule and the no-new-**Current**-track disposition), and on `L2-DL-001` (the staging contour whose section (h) rewrites, left byte-untouched as a decision). Cross-references vault **ADR-038 as amended**, **ADR-039**, **ADR-007**, **ADR-020**, **PDR-017** (Stage 0 access conditions, inoperative while (f)(2) holds), **PDR-009** (EU anchor and the DPIA draft in progress) and **ADR-013 / contract §4.7**.
- **Effects.**
  - **Changed (contract):** `AGENTS.md` — §7 live-deploy-path enumeration only, gaining `docker-entrypoint.sh`. One line; no other line in §7 or anywhere else in the file moves. `CLAUDE.md` — the **Decision authority in planning** section gains the escalation-channel rule (see the next bullet); statement-only, no other section touched.
  - **The escalation channel was fixed in the same milestone that exercised it.** Both of this packet's out-of-boundary edits — (e)(1) and (e)(2) — were correctly identified as escalations and then raised through the **interactive question tool** instead of in the plan's Escalations section, which is the wrong channel: the picker gives the owner no way to write text, so any button press is a forced move rather than a judgement, and a question that ends the turn without a plan attached is a process violation however good the question is. The owner's answers were nonetheless to fold both in, and the plan records them as provisional and re-confirmed at plan review rather than as owner decisions taken in-channel. `CLAUDE.md` now states the rule, requires both branches of a design-changing escalation to be described in the plan with a recommendation, and requires an out-of-channel answer to be logged in **Decisions & assumptions** where review can re-open it. The findings were correct; only the channel was not — which is precisely why the rule belongs in the contract rather than in anyone's memory for the right phrasing.
  - **Changed (spine):** `docs/execution-map.md` — the `A3` track added to **Current state** as a closed track, between the A2 block and the M5 line, with P1–P2 at their SHAs and an `A3-CLOSE` bullet; the M2-P3 line's "the proxy is **M5**" corrected; the M5 line noting that A3b is queued but not opened. `docs/product/BuildPlan.md` — the M2-P3 bullet and the M5 **Origin unification** bullet, which moves out of M5's list and becomes a statement that M5 inherits the topology rather than establishing it. `docs/product/TechSpec.md` — the `/api` monorepo bullet, and the **Prod vs dev** and **Staging** bullets under Core store, which this milestone is what falsified. `docs/INVARIANTS.md` — one phrase in `A3-P1-INV-001`'s namespace note, per (b). This entry.
  - **Changed (RUNBOOK):** the A3-P1 ordering per (c) (steps reordered, the `MUST_MATCH` reason inline, the designed Step 0 failure relabelled, the two "inverse" cross-references corrected); the **Staging contour** rewrite per (h); the canonical-statement collapse per (d); the not-executed corrections per (g); the guardrail file-path enumeration gaining `app/docker-entrypoint.sh`; the "three build-configs each on its own trigger" claim narrowed to the two triggers that exist; and the `/api` promotion gate's eval precondition.
  - **Changed (harness / build-config, zero behaviour):** `.pre-commit-config.yaml` — `docker-entrypoint\.sh` added to both rewriting-hook exclude regexes plus the header sentence they make true; no hook, `rev` or `additional_dependencies` change. `scripts/check-contract-integrity.sh` — the never-scanned comment block only; no executable line moves, and the script is not in `CONTRACT_FILES`, so it never self-scans. `api/cloudbuild.yaml` — the header comment per (e)(2); every executable line byte-untouched.
  - **Untouched, and verified so:** everything under `app/**`; everything under `api/**` except the one comment block in `api/cloudbuild.yaml`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; `docs/RUNTIME-INVARIANTS.md`; `README.md`; `docker-compose.yml`; `pyproject.toml`; `scripts/**` except the one comment block; and `A3-DL-001` / `A3-DL-002`, byte-untouched by design per (b).
  - **Operability: none** (ADR-013 / contract §4.7). No `theygrow_api.parameters` knob — the packet edits no Python and introduces no tunable; `PARAMETERS_SCHEMA_VERSION` stays `5`, where A3-P2 left it. The `.pre-commit-config.yaml` change is dev-time harness hygiene, not a runtime knob: it changes which files two auto-fixing hooks may rewrite and nothing the deployed system reads. No `theygrow_api.signals` taxonomy entry, no `emitted_now` change, no producer added or removed — `READINESS_PROBE` and every prior kind emit exactly as they already did, so `M4-P3-INV-003`'s kind-set comparison needs no driver extension. No GA4 event (the `/app` surface is byte-untouched). No knob-shaped literal surfaced by the reconciliation: A3-P1's two entrypoint constants are already recorded as a deliberate placement with `changed_in: A3-DL-001`. Documentation emits nothing.
  - **§4-safe.** No new egress, no origin contacted, no request that did not already happen. No personal or family data touched: no corpus record, no database, no credential, host, user, password or connection string enters the repository. The debts in (f)(2)–(f)(5) describe the apex and `www` surfaces **without naming the domain** — deliberate, per (f)(6), and discipline rather than enforcement, since `docs/decision-log.md` is not in `CONTRACT_FILES` and the contract gate would not have caught it. No live identifier enters a contract or spine file, verified by grepping the banned patterns out of every file this packet edited.
  - **Validation-honesty caveat** (M4-CLOSE discipline). This packet was validated entirely repo-locally: the real CI gate (`pre-commit run --all-files`) green with **zero modified files**, the contract-integrity gate run directly before and after the edits, per-file greps for the reconciled statements, and a diff-scope check confirming no product code moved and that the two out-of-boundary edits are comment- and exclude-only. **That proves the gates are green and the diff is scoped. It proves nothing about whether the reconciled prose is true** — that a statement now matches reality is a reviewer's judgement, and the owner's review against the vault is where spec conformance is checked, not CI. **And the corrected ordering in (c) has not been executed**: A3-P1 has not merged, the ordering has never been run against live Cloud Build in either form, and the correction rests on the `MUST_MATCH` semantics `api/cloudbuild.yaml` already relies on rather than on an observation. The first real test of it is the owner's run.
  - **The repo decision-log is the working copy, and the upstream decision layer is not annotated.** Annotating the vault — ADR-038 as amended, ADR-039, ADR-007, ADR-020, PDR-017 and PDR-009, and the Roadmap §0 revision that produced (a) — is the **owner-side close task after merge**. Nothing in this entry should be read as it having happened. The `L2-DL-004` (e) posture, restated because it still holds.
