## A3-DL-004 — A3-ERRATA: the production store exists, and it came back different from what the repo prescribes — the connection ceiling is instance-derived, the connection policy is hardened, and three operational rules learned in execution enter the repo

- **Date.** 2026-07-29
- **Type.** Errata / execution record — A3-ERRATA, an out-of-band correction packet on the A3 branch **before** PR #19 merges. Documentation, plus three text-only sites in `api/**` (see (g)): no schema, no migration, no route, no build-config, no default, no assertion, no `PARAMETERS_SCHEMA_VERSION` bump, nothing under `app/**`. It records what the owner did; it does nothing. *(Namespace note: continues the `A3-DL` namespace opened by `A3-DL-001`; the "Entry format" header is intentionally left unedited, per the `A3-DL-003` / `L2-DL-004` / `L1-DL-001` precedent. This is a fourth **decision** in the A3 namespace, not a fourth packet: the milestone closed at `A3-DL-003` and stays closed.)*
- **Decision.** Correct the repository against the executed procedure, before merging a procedure that contradicts the infrastructure it describes. On **2026-07-29**, after `A3-DL-003` landed, the owner executed **steps 1–7** of `docs/RUNBOOK.md` "Production database enablement" against the live project. The instance, the production database, the `vector` extension (0.8.5), the application role, the `DATABASE_URL` secret, the runtime service account and its two grants, and the Cloud Build `actAs` grant all exist. Steps 8–15 do not. What came back different is corrected here:

  a. **The application role's `CONNECTION LIMIT` is 30, not 10, and the arithmetic changes direction with it.** A3-P2 derived the number from the application — `db_pool_size` × `--max-instances` plus a guess at headroom — without knowing the instance. The executed number is derived from the instance: `max_connections` **50** (verified by `SHOW`), `superuser_reserved_connections` **3** (verified), roughly **5** more for Cloud SQL's own agents, leaving ~42; **12** held back for the owner's Auth Proxy sessions, `alembic` runs and incident work; **30** for the role. **This supersedes the 10 without contradicting ADR-039**, which fixes that the role carries an explicit `CONNECTION LIMIT` and does not fix its value. The derived rule the RUNBOOK now states is `--max-instances` × `db_pool_size` ≤ **30**, which the shipped `2 × 2 = 4` satisfies with wide headroom. **Reconciling the live role down to 10 was rejected** on two grounds: it is an owner infrastructure action this packet has no authority to take, and it would replace an instance-derived number with an application-derived guess.

  b. **The two sides are not consistent today, and one flag is what makes them consistent — stated where it can be acted on.** The live service still carries `maxScale 20`, which at `db_pool_size` 2 demands **40** connections against a role limited to 30. The `--max-instances 2` that A3-P2 ships is what brings it under the ceiling, and it takes effect only on the first deploy that carries the substitutions. **The flag and the pool move together**; changing either alone silently re-opens the gap. This is recorded in the RUNBOOK's step 4, in its step 10 narrowing note, and in the "Live-infra divergence" max-instances record.

  c. **`max_connections` is deliberately NOT pinned as a database flag. That is a decision, not an omission.** Unpinned, it tracks the instance's memory and therefore survives a tier change; pinned, it would freeze at a value chosen for today's tier and have to be remembered by whoever resizes the instance. The cost is stated with it: the 30 in (a) is derived from an **observed** value, so a tier change means re-running the two `SHOW`s and revisiting the role's limit.

  d. **`sslMode ENCRYPTED_ONLY` is a hardening beyond the written procedure, and becomes part of it.** The instance was created with `ALLOW_UNENCRYPTED_AND_ENCRYPTED` — creation does not default to the required state — and was patched to `ENCRYPTED_ONLY` while the database was still empty, which is the only moment the change is free. It lands both as a **required parameter** in step 1's decided-parameter list and as an explicit **sub-step**, because a parameter that a creation flag does not deliver is a step or it is nothing. It is a sub-step rather than a new numbered step deliberately: a new step would renumber 1–15 and break every cross-reference to steps 4 / 5 / 6 / 9–10 / 12 / 13 in this file, `docs/INVARIANTS.md` (`A3-P2-INV-001` Scope), `docs/RUNBOOK.md` itself and `api/cloudbuild.yaml`'s Step-0 message. A cross-reference break is a worse defect than a sub-step.

  e. **Three operational rules that existed nowhere in the repo, each placed at the point of failure and stated once** (the `A3-DL-003` (d) rule: a rule whose failure mode is ambiguous must not exist in two versions that can drift):
  1. **The Cloud SQL Auth Proxy runs on 5433, not 5432** — because 5432 is taken by the local dev container (`docker-compose.yml`, `pgvector/pgvector:pg16`). Same major version, same extension, so a mistaken connection **succeeds** and looks right: a migration reported as applied to production, applied to a throwaway container. A wrong port that refused would be free; this one does not refuse. Stated at the step-5 TCP URL form, where the port is typed.
  2. **Never `gcloud sql connect`** — it temporarily adds the caller's own IP to the instance's authorized networks, which is precisely the entry ADR-039's permanently-empty list forbids, written silently, on the path an operator reaches for when in a hurry. Stated as a sub-bullet of the empty-authorized-networks rule it violates.
  3. **Run `alembic upgrade head` as the application role, never as `postgres`** — table ownership follows the creator, so a superuser migration **succeeds**, reads `head`, and then breaks the application at runtime on a database that looks correctly migrated. Stated at step 12, with a pointer from the standing "Before every migration" pre-step; it is the deliberate mirror of step 3, where the extension **does** need the superuser.

  f. **The identity-split debt is three branches across FOUR identities, not three.** `A3-DL-003` (f)(7) counted the default compute account, the `/api` runtime account and the PWA runtime account. A fourth exists: a **legacy Cloud Build agent account holding `roles/cloudbuild.builds.builder`**. No trigger runs under it — the builds run under the default account, whose `roles/editor` already covers the same ground — so it is **inert rather than absent**, which is exactly the kind of identity that survives a narrowing pass by not being counted. `A3-DL-003` is left byte-untouched and amended here by reference; the RUNBOOK's step 6 sentence claiming "exactly one identity" **is** corrected in place, because that is a live procedure statement rather than a ledger record — the `A3-DL-003` (b) rule applied in both directions.

  g. **Three `api/**` sites carried the false number, and fixing them is an owner-confirmed scope extension** — recorded as one rather than absorbed silently. The approved scope put code changes out. The deciding reason is narrower than the `A3-DL-003` (e)(2) / `A1-DL-008` shipped-comment precedent: `api/theygrow_api/parameters.py`'s `db_pool_size` **`Parameter.note` is the emitted parameter surface**, so "shipped 2 x 2 = 4 against a limit of 10" is **machine-readable output**, not a comment — a false number a reader could reasonably treat as authoritative. The other two sites (the module's arithmetic comment block, and `api/tests/test_parameters.py`'s docstring) are corrected with it because leaving them would put the repo's own explanation at odds with the value it explains. **Text only:** no default, no flag, no assertion, no behaviour; `test_parameters.py`'s `"CONNECTION LIMIT" in note` assertion is untouched and still green. **The diff therefore leaves `docs/` by design**, and this bullet is why.

  **A fourth site, in `api/cloudbuild.yaml`'s header comment, is corrected on its own ground** — owner-confirmed, and it is the most consequential of the four because it is a **shipped build-config** rather than a module. Two statements: "the project has exactly one identity today" (four, per (f)) and "against the application role's `CONNECTION LIMIT` of 10, leaving 6" (30, per (a)). Left as written, **the build-config and the RUNBOOK would prescribe two different connection ceilings — the exact defect (a) exists to end**, and the one an operator is most likely to meet at the moment they are reading the deploy config to decide whether a scaling change is safe. The same file was already corrected on this ground in A3-CLOSE (`A3-DL-003` (e)(2), on the `A1-DL-008` shipped-comment reasoning), so this is that precedent applied a second time rather than a new exemption. **Comment lines only:** no substitution, no flag, no step, no executable line — verified by reading the diff with zero context, not by inspection of the file.

  h. **Statements `A3-DL-003` wrote in the conditional, now false in the other direction, corrected — without swapping one wrong tense for another.** The close-out was right that nothing existed; asserting the same today would be the mirror image of the falsehood this packet removes. Corrected: the enablement section's "creates the **first** instance — there was none" opener; the staging contour's "its instance does not exist yet either" and "once **Production database enablement** has created it"; the `/api` production records for the runtime account and the secret; the whole "Live-infra divergence" Cloud SQL block; the instance-parameters paragraph's "no instance exists yet" closer; the **Restore / disaster recovery → Targets** PITR caveat and retention window, which become **observations with a date on them** while keeping the verify-do-not-assume instruction, because a decision and an observation are still not a mechanism. Two **spine** statements go with them, corrected in place as live-doc convention allows: `docs/execution-map.md`'s A3 present-state claim, and `docs/product/TechSpec.md`'s "procedure that has **not been executed**".

  i. **"The store exists" is not "the wiring works", and the docs are built so nobody can read it that way.** A new execution-state block at the head of the procedure says which steps ran and which did not, in those terms: the build-config flags, the `GET /api/health/ready` route and the served engine's pool policy that A3-P2 ships have still never executed **anywhere** — not in production, not on staging (there is none), and CI proves only route and pool mechanics over TCP. Step 13 remains the first exercise of the socket form. **That distinction is the reason A3 was split from A3b** (`A3-DL-003` (a)), and it is the one this errata could most easily have blurred.

  j. **The procedure stays in the imperative; it is not rewritten as history.** It is a reusable artifact — a disaster-recovery rebuild runs it again against a restored instance, and **Staging contour** borrows its shape — so past-tensing fifteen steps to record one run would destroy the artifact to document the event. The execution-state block carries the event instead.

  k. **The identifier values stay owner-fill, and that is the conservative branch rather than an omission.** The block records that each resource **exists** and when, plus the state facts, and leaves instance id, instance connection name, zone, database name, application role name and secret name as "created 2026-07-29 — value to be recorded by the owner", matching the convention the file already uses for the PWA and `/api` runtime accounts. Disclosure of a live identifier into a temporarily-public repository is an owner decision, not a documentation default, and the vault holds no identifier values to check this against. Two consequences: `docs/RUNBOOK.md`'s "identifiers are not recorded in this repo yet" statements (the **Restore / disaster recovery** blockquote and the closing paragraph of "Live-infra divergence") remain **true as written and byte-untouched**; and a restore still reads the names from the console under stress, which is a real cost the owner is choosing knowingly.

  l. **The state facts are recorded in full, because they are configuration and not identifiers** — the line this file already draws for the ADR-039 parameters: `RUNNABLE`; PostgreSQL 16 / Enterprise / zonal / europe-west1; deletion protection on; `authorizedNetworks` absent; `sslMode ENCRYPTED_ONLY`; PITR on with a 7-day window and transaction logs in Cloud Storage; backups on, 02:00 UTC, 7 retained; `vector` 0.8.5. The application role's executed attributes go with them (`LOGIN NOSUPERUSER NOCREATEDB NOCREATEROLE NOINHERIT`, `CONNECTION LIMIT 30`, owns the database, `PUBLIC` holds nothing, create/insert/select/drop smoke passed under the role).

  m. **`psycopg` is settled and is not touched.** `api/pyproject.toml` pins psycopg 3 and `theygrow_api.db.engine.engine_url()` normalizes the drivername to `postgresql+psycopg` regardless of input, so neither the secret's value nor the code needs anything. Recorded so the next reader does not "improve" it.

  n. **Named findings this packet does NOT fix, so none is silent.** (1) **The staging contour's `CONNECTION LIMIT 8` no longer fits**: ADR-039 puts both databases on one instance, and 30 + 8 + the 12 held back exceeds the ~42-connection budget — raising that contour means revisiting **both** numbers. Named in the RUNBOOK's step 4 and in the staging contour's state block; decided nowhere, because the contour is not raised and is out of this packet's scope. (2) The staging procedure's Auth Proxy line still says **5432** and inherits the same dev-container collision; left byte-untouched for the same reason, with the collision named in its state block. (3) `docs/execution-map.md`'s **A3-P2 packet record** still says "a `CONNECTION LIMIT` of 10" — it records what that packet shipped, attributed to a SHA, which was true then; it is a packet record rather than a state claim, and is amended by reference here rather than edited. (4) `A3-DL-002` and `A3-DL-003` carry the same superseded number and the same "nothing exists" statements in the ledger, and stay byte-untouched by the same convention.
- **Rationale.** A procedure and the infrastructure it describes disagreeing is a defect with a short half-life: the moment PR #19 merges, the repo prescribes `CONNECTION LIMIT 10` for a role that has 30, omits the `sslMode` patch that was actually required, and tells the next operator to run the Auth Proxy on a port occupied by a look-alike database. Correcting before merge costs one docs packet; correcting after costs an incident with a plausible-looking cause. The deeper reason to write it down at all is that **the corrections are not stylistic** — each one encodes a fact the repository had no way to know: what the instance's real connection budget is, what creation does and does not default to, and which of three ways into the database are safe. And the errata is the right venue rather than an amendment to `A3-DL-002`: that entry recorded a decision that was correct on the information available, and the log's convention for a later correction is a new entry that amends by reference, not a rewrite of history.
- **No dedicated ADR by design at the repo layer.** Nothing architectural is decided. ADR-039 already governs the instance parameters and deliberately leaves the role's `CONNECTION LIMIT` **value** unfixed, which is the whole reason 30 can supersede 10 without an ADR amendment; the vault-side annotation of what the execution produced is the owner's post-merge task, exactly as `A3-DL-003` records for its own cross-references.
- **Alternatives considered.**
  1. **Reconcile the live role down to 10** so the repo needs no change. Rejected — it is an owner infrastructure action this packet has no authority to take, and it would discard an instance-derived number in favour of an application-derived guess. See (a).
  2. **Fold the corrections into `A3-DL-002` / `A3-DL-003`** rather than opening a fourth entry. Rejected — both were true when written; the log's own mechanism is amendment by reference, and `A3-DL-003` (b) rejected exactly this for exactly this reason.
  3. **Rewrite the enablement procedure in the past tense**, as a record of the run. Rejected — see (j). It would destroy a reusable procedure to document a single execution.
  4. **Add `sslMode` as a new numbered step 2**, renumbering the rest. Rejected — see (d). It breaks cross-references in four files to fix a formatting preference.
  5. **Record the identifier values now**, since the resources exist. Rejected — see (k). Disclosure into a temporarily-public repository is an owner decision; existence and state facts are recordable without it.
  6. **Leave the three `api/**` sites and record them as debt.** Rejected on owner confirmation — see (g). The `Parameter.note` one is emitted output, not a comment, so the falsehood would be machine-readable.
  7. **Fix the staging contour's connection limit and port while in the file.** Rejected — see (n). The contour is not raised, it is outside the approved scope, and its number cannot be chosen without the production one being settled first, which it now is; naming the finding is what this packet owes.
  8. **Correct `docs/execution-map.md`'s A3-P2 packet record too.** Rejected — see (n)(3). It is a record of what a packet shipped, not a claim about the live project.
- **Supersedes.** None. **Amends `A3-DL-002`** on the application role's `CONNECTION LIMIT` (its Decision (6) and its Effects, which record 10) and on its premise that no store exists — left byte-untouched. **Amends `A3-DL-003`** on (f)(7)'s identity count (three branches across **four** identities, per (f)) and on (g)'s "no Cloud SQL instance, database, application role or `DATABASE_URL` secret exists", which was true when written and is now false — also left byte-untouched. Cross-references vault **ADR-039** (the instance parameters, the empty authorized-networks list as load-bearing, the snapshot-before-migration rule, and the explicit-`CONNECTION LIMIT`-without-a-fixed-value reading that lets (a) stand), **ADR-038 as amended** (the identity split, the default compute account narrowed last, and `roles/editor` lacking `secretmanager.versions.access` — which is why the deployed service cannot read its secret until it runs as the dedicated account), **ADR-008** (one managed Postgres as SoT), **ADR-020** (the L1 promotion gate, unchanged by this packet), **PDR-009** (EU anchor; the DPIA draft records 2026-07-29 as the date server-side storage of family data first existed) and **ADR-013 / contract §4.7** (operability).
- **Effects.**
  - **Changed (RUNBOOK):** "Production database enablement" — the section blockquote's opener; a new **execution-state block** per (i)/(j); step 1's parameter list and two new sub-bullets (the `gcloud sql connect` ban, the `sslMode` hardening) plus the zone in its record instruction; step 4's `CONNECTION LIMIT` value and its arithmetic paragraph, rewritten instance-first with the ceiling rule, the `maxScale 20` consistency fact, the unpinned-`max_connections` decision and the staging-budget finding; step 5's TCP form (5433) and the collision rule; step 6's identity count; step 10's pointer to the ceiling rule; step 12's run-as-application-role rule. Elsewhere: the **Staging contour** instance block and its canonical-names line; **Restore / disaster recovery → Targets** (RPO/PITR and the retention window as dated observations) and the standing pre-migration block's role pointer; and under **Live-infra divergence** the `/api` production runtime-account, secret and max-instances records, the Cloud SQL block (existence, the role's executed attributes, a new observed-state sub-block), and the instance-parameters paragraph.
  - **Changed (spine):** `docs/execution-map.md` — the A3 track's present-state sentence only, per (h); its P1/P2 packet records, its A3-CLOSE bullet and every other track are byte-untouched. `docs/product/TechSpec.md` — the "not been executed" clause in the **Prod vs dev** bullet only; the **Staging** bullet is untouched and still true.
  - **Changed (`api/**`, text only, per (g)):** `api/theygrow_api/parameters.py` — the served-engine arithmetic comment block and the `db_pool_size` `Parameter.note` string. `api/tests/test_parameters.py` — one docstring. `api/cloudbuild.yaml` — the two header-comment statements per (g), the fourth and most consequential site: **every executable line, flag, step and substitution is byte-untouched**, as `A3-DL-003` (e)(2) recorded for the same file. Across all four sites: no default, no flag, no assertion, no signature, no import.
  - **Untouched, and verified so:** everything under `app/**`; `api/cloudbuild.staging.yaml`, `api/Dockerfile`, `api/alembic/**` and every other file under `api/**` (and `api/cloudbuild.yaml` outside its header comment); `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; `.pre-commit-config.yaml`; `scripts/**`; `AGENTS.md`; `CLAUDE.md`; `docs/INVARIANTS.md`; `docs/RUNTIME-INVARIANTS.md`; `docs/product/BuildPlan.md`; `README.md`; `docker-compose.yml`; and `A3-DL-001` / `A3-DL-002` / `A3-DL-003`, byte-untouched by design.
  - **No `docs/INVARIANTS.md` entry.** `AGENTS.md` §10 makes that file enforced-only and an errata enforces nothing new — the `A3-DL-003` (i) / `L2-DL-004` (g) disposition. `A3-P2-INV-001` was re-read against this diff: its Scope cites "**Production database enablement**, step 13", which (d)'s no-renumbering decision deliberately preserves, and its statement carries no number this errata changes.
  - **Operability: none** (ADR-013 / contract §4.7). No knob and no signal is expected or added. `PARAMETERS_SCHEMA_VERSION` stays **5**, where A3-P2 left it; `db_pool_size` (2) and `db_max_overflow` (0) keep their values and their `changed_in: A3-DL-002`, since they satisfy the new ceiling with wide headroom and (g) changes only prose. No `theygrow_api.signals` taxonomy entry, no `emitted_now` change, no producer added or removed — `READINESS_PROBE` and every prior kind emit exactly as before, so `M4-P3-INV-003`'s kind-set comparison needs no driver extension. No GA4 event (`app/**` is byte-untouched). **Knob-shaped literals surfaced by the reconciliation, named rather than converted:** the role's `CONNECTION LIMIT` is a database-role property set by owner SQL and is not a candidate for the config surface — no process reads it, and a knob that cannot be read is a lie about where the value lives; `max_connections` is likewise instance configuration, deliberately unpinned per (c). Documentation emits nothing.
  - **§4-safe.** No new egress, no origin contacted, no request that did not already happen. No personal or family data touched: no corpus record, no database row, and **no credential, host, user, password or connection string** enters the repository — the two URL forms stay placeholders and the only change to one of them is a port number. No live identifier enters a contract or spine file; the identifier **values** enter nowhere at all, per (k). The state facts recorded in (l) are instance configuration and carry nothing family-identifying.
  - **Validation-honesty caveat** (M4-CLOSE discipline). Validated entirely repo-locally: `pre-commit run --all-files` green with zero modified files, the contract-integrity gate run before and after, per-statement greps for every corrected site, and a diff-scope check confirming the three `api/**` edits are text-only. **That proves the gates are green and the diff is scoped. It proves nothing about whether the corrected prose is true** — the facts come from the owner's run against the live project, which no gate in this repository can see, and the owner's review against the vault is where conformance is checked. **And the corrected procedure has not been run in its corrected form**: steps 8–15 have never executed, the `sslMode` sub-step and the three operational rules are written from one execution rather than from a repeated one, and the ceiling rule's live half (`--max-instances 2`) still has not reached the service.
  - **The upstream decision layer is not annotated.** Annotating the vault — ADR-039 with the executed instance's state and the 30, ADR-038 with the fourth identity, and the PDR-009 DPIA draft with 2026-07-29 as the date server-side storage of family data first existed — is the **owner-side task after merge**. Nothing here should be read as it having happened. The `A3-DL-003` / `L2-DL-004` (e) posture, restated because it still holds.

## LSC-DL-001 — L1-P1 `capacitor-shell-parity`: the app gets a native container before it gets a native store, the WebView storage seam is compiled into a gate, and two false claims are corrected against what the repository can actually verify

- **Date.** 2026-08-12
- **Type.** Packet — L1-P1, the first packet of milestone **L1 "local structured core"**. Adds a Capacitor Android shell, two enforced invariants and a fourth CI job; corrects a false claim in three build-configs and `docs/RUNBOOK.md`. **Zero product change:** nothing under `app/index.html`, `app/sw.js`, `app/manifest.json`, `app/offline.html`, `app/kb-v1.json`, `app/icons/` or `app/m/` is touched, and the parity suite's committed baselines are unchanged and unre-blessed. *(Namespace note, and it is load-bearing rather than clerical: PDR-026's ladder id `L1` **collides** with the shipped **L1 Promotion Gate** track, which already owns `L1-DL-001`. Decisions and invariants for this milestone therefore take the `LSC-` prefix — `LSC-DL-{NNN}`, `LSC-P{k}-INV-{NNN}` — while packets keep `L1-P{k}` as the approved ladder names them. This is the `A2`/`L2` split precedent applied to a genuine clash rather than a stylistic one, and it is declared in `docs/execution-map.md`. The "Entry format" header is intentionally left unedited.)*

- **Decision.**

  a. **The shell wraps the web app; it does not fork it.** The APK's web root is **assembled**, not authored: `native/tools/stage-webdir.js` reads the `COPY` list out of `app/Dockerfile` and copies exactly those 39 files into `native/www/`. The ship list becomes the single source of truth for **both** delivery channels, so a file added to one is added to the other or CI reds. Two files are excluded by the parser's existing rule rather than by a special case — `app/nginx.conf` and `app/docker-entrypoint.sh` `COPY` outside the web root, and the entrypoint mints the `/api` proxy's ID token, which has no business in a distributed artifact. `webDir` pointing at `app/` was rejected outright: it would sweep `node_modules/`, `tests/` and the parity baselines into the APK.

  b. **One parser, two consumers.** `shippedPaths` and the module-graph walker moved verbatim from `app/tests/delivery-contract.spec.js` into `app/tests/support/ship-list.js`, so the stager and the drift guard cannot disagree about what is shipped. The extraction is proven behaviour-preserving by test count: **162 before, 162 after**, the same cases. A second, independently written parser was rejected as a second thing to forget.

  c. **`LSC-P1-INV-001` enforces the seam that L1-P2 replaces, not the inversion L1-P2 delivers.** ADR-043 §1 makes native storage the source of truth and demotes WebView storage to a losable cache. That cannot be enforced today — the native store is P2 and the app still writes family data to `localStorage`. What is enforceable, and now gated, is the fail-closed half: Web Storage is reached through `app/m/v1/core/storage.js` plus **two individually declared** shell accesses, and any undeclared site is red. Enforcing it now is what keeps the P2 swap tractable: a seam is only replaceable in one place if the set of call sites cannot quietly grow first (ADR-030 §2). The guard matches **property-access form** rather than stripping comments, because stripping `//` to end-of-line also eats anything after a `https://` literal — a guard that fails **open**, which is worse than none.

  d. **`LSC-P1-INV-002` makes "zero product change" and "no egress" checkable rather than asserted.** Byte-identity of all 39 staged files against their `app/` sources, both directions of set equality, the application id, the web root, the pinned WebView origin, and the absence of any bundler in the native toolchain. Plus the backup hardening in (e). The spec deliberately does **not** stage before asserting — staging inside the test would wipe a hand-added file moments before checking for hand-added files, leaving a tautology; `scripts/parity-suite.sh` stages, which is why the staging step lives in the gate.

  e. **Android Auto Backup is turned off, and this was raised as an escalation before it was decided.** The Capacitor template ships `android:allowBackup="true"`. Left alone, the platform would upload the app sandbox — the WebView's `localStorage` today, the native store from L1-P2 — to the user's Google Drive: **family data crossing a network boundary by platform default**, with no export contour, no key scope and no grant model behind it (PDR-027 §2, privacy and any new egress surface). The fail-closed branch was taken and the plan carried both: `allowBackup="false"`, plus `dataExtractionRules` (API 31+, and the **only** one that also covers device-to-device transfer, which `allowBackup` does not disable) and `fullBackupContent` (API 24–30, in range at `minSdkVersion 24`). All three are pinned by test, because the manifest is a generated file whose hand edits a toolchain upgrade silently reverts. The deliberate export contour is **L1-P4**.

  f. **The WebView origin is pinned, not left to Capacitor's defaults.** `https` / `localhost`, written explicitly. Scheme and hostname *are* the origin, and the origin is what Web Storage is keyed by — an upgrade that changed either would orphan everything in WebView storage: invisible in P1, destructive in **L1-P3**, where the migration bridge (ADR-043 §5) reads exactly that origin. Pinning converts a silent data loss into a red test.

  g. **The parity suite covers the Capacitor channel, and asserts the web channel's own baselines.** A second server serves the staged web root under a **Capacitor-shaped** profile — no nginx cache/MIME headers, no `try_files` SPA fallback, no `/api` — and the new `native` project runs the existing `dom-parity` and `behavior` specs at the mobile viewport against the committed **`dom-mobile`** baselines. A separate baseline set was rejected as tautological given (d) and as doubling the re-blessing cost; pointing both channels at the same bytes is what makes a divergence visible. The absent SPA fallback is the point rather than an omission: a shell that had come to depend on `try_files` boots in a browser and fails in the APK, and under this profile that dependency is a red test instead of a device-only surprise.

  h. **The service-worker update channel is INERT in the Capacitor shell. This is a finding, and it is what the PR-#12 reconciliation actually turned up** — see (l). What ships today: `app/sw.js` (`CACHE_VERSION` `v9`) is network-first for navigation and `/api/`, cache-first for other statics, and carries **no** `skipWaiting()` — a new worker parks in `waiting` until a `SKIP_WAITING` message drives it, whereupon `activate()` purges every non-current cache and calls `clients.claim()`. `app/m/v1/sw-register.js` shows `#updateBanner` on both discovery paths (already-waiting, and `updatefound` while a `controller` exists — that check is what keeps a first install silent) and reloads exactly once, only on user accept. nginx serving `/sw.js` `no-cache, must-revalidate` is what makes a bumped worker discoverable at all. **In the APK none of that can fire:** assets are read from local storage inside the package, `/sw.js` is never re-fetched from a network origin, so no bumped `CACHE_VERSION` is ever discovered and the banner can never appear. The only update path is **APK replacement**. The three update tests therefore skip under the `capacitor` profile, with the reason written in the spec rather than hidden in a `testMatch`.

  i. **The consequence of (h) that is *not* inert is named, bounded, and assigned to L1-P2.** If registration succeeds inside the WebView, Cache Storage becomes a **second copy of the shell held in WebView storage**, and the cache-first branch could serve stale assets after an APK update. It is bounded — each release ships a new `sw.js`, and `activate()` purges every non-current cache — but it is exactly the persistence surface this milestone inverts. **L1-P1 changes nothing about the registration**, because the packet makes zero product changes; the disposition belongs to **L1-P2**, which owns the native store, and it is declared **knowingly uncovered** in `LSC-P1-INV-001`'s Scope rather than left out of frame. What the repository *can* assert, and now does, is the weaker checkable thing: the app boots without depending on the service worker, which the `native` project proves by running every other flow green.

  j. **The `MUST_MATCH` correction states only what the repository can verify.** Three build-configs and three places in `docs/RUNBOOK.md` claimed Cloud Build's `MUST_MATCH` substitution option was "kept deliberately" and caught a substitution-name typo. **Verified fact: no build-config declares `substitutionOption` at all** — `options:` in all three carries only `logging: CLOUD_LOGGING_ONLY`. So the repository asserts nothing and the described protection does not exist. What does exist is each config's **Step 0 guard**, which fails on an **empty** substitution — which is what a mis-named key leaves the declared one as. **The option is deliberately not switched**: the PWA delivery channel is currently the only one and a mismatch would break it outright; the switch is recorded as open debt with what would close it.

  k. **The `ALLOW_LOOSE` half is recorded as an UNVERIFIED UPSTREAM CLAIM, not as an observation — and that distinction is the same defect class this packet is correcting.** The handoff's revision note held that trigger-invoked builds run under `ALLOW_LOOSE`. It has been confirmed neither in this repository nor by the owner, and it is being re-checked owner-side. Writing it into a build-config comment as an owner-supplied fact would have replaced one falsely-provenanced claim with another. Every site that mentions it labels it unverified and points at the open-debt entry.

  l. **The "PR #12 sync debt" does not exist in this repository, and the finding is recorded rather than worked around.** Verified: **PR #12 is `feat/skill-link-from-activities`**, merged **2026-06-30**, and it *is* reconciled — `SLA-DL-001`, an execution-map block, and a RUNBOOK cache-version correction, all in tree. The only PR merged on **2026-07-04** is **#13 `feat/vendor-domain-kb-artifact`**, also reconciled (`VDK-DL-001`). **No PR — merged, open or closed — is named `pwa-installed-update-fix`.** What carries that name is a **stale local branch** with **zero** commits not already on `main` (`git rev-list --count main..feat/pwa-installed-update-fix` = 0; it points at `5a69041`, the PR-#12 merge commit) and an empty diff against `main`. There is no unreconciled code from it. The installed-app update path on `main` comes from **PR #6** `feat/pwa-update-delivery` (2026-06-26), recorded as `PWA-DL-001`. The substantive half of the ask — what that path does and how the shell sits on it — is (h) and (i), delivered in full.

  m. **The merge-trigger expectation is split per config, because the two answers differ and lumping them would launder one into the other.** Editing `api/cloudbuild.yaml` / `api/cloudbuild.staging.yaml` fires the production `/api` trigger, whose build **is expected to fail at Step 0 — designed, not a defect**: its three substitutions were never set (**Production database enablement** steps 8–15 have never run), and since **2026-08-11** it is additionally moot, production `/api` being deliberately decommissioned with its Cloud SQL instance deleted. Editing `app/cloudbuild.yaml` — or any `app/**` file, which this packet does — fires the PWA trigger, and **that expectation is OPEN**: it depends on whether `_API_UPSTREAM_URL` and `_PWA_RUNTIME_SA` were set after A3-P1 merged, which **this repository does not record** ("Live-infra divergence" still reads *not recorded yet*). Both branches are written into the RUNBOOK, and the difference that matters is stated there: for the PWA a Step 0 failure would be a **delivery-channel regression to act on**, not a designed state, because the PWA is the live front door and this is the path it is promoted through. The owner reads the trigger's substitutions in the console before merging.

  n. **Stack pins, decided and logged rather than escalated** (they are engineering choices). **Capacitor 8.5.0**, the current major — pinned over the two-majors-older 6.x that this box's Node 18 could have run, because letting a dev machine's accidental Node version pin the product's native platform for L2–L7 is backwards. The consequence is stated rather than hidden: `@capacitor/cli` declares `engines.node >= 22` and **hard-fails** below it, so `native/android/` was generated under a hermetically fetched Node 22 (`npx node@22`), and CI pins Node 22. **JDK 21**, matching the template's `capacitor.build.gradle`. **`native/android/` is committed** — it carries the application id, the (e) hardening and the Gradle pins, none of which survives regeneration. **Debug signing only**; no release keystore exists in the repository or in CI, and key custody is a later explicit decision. **`native/` is a new top-level subtree** rather than `app/native/`: under `app/` it would enter the Docker build context and land inside the "every shipped `app/` file is excluded" reasoning `.pre-commit-config.yaml` documents, making correctness depend on `.dockerignore` — a file the ship-list guard explicitly does not consult.

  o. **The GA4 egress is inherited unchanged into the Android channel, and it is given a permanent address rather than being called a later-packet question.** `app/index.html` loads `googletagmanager.com` and emits through `trackEvent`; the APK carries the same bytes and therefore the same behaviour. Payloads are already §4-safe and cleared under `GA-DL-001`, so this is not new *data* — but it is the same egress over a **new distribution channel**, so it was raised in the plan's Escalations. Removing or gating it was rejected: it is a product change this packet forbids, and it would fork the two channels' bytes and destroy the byte-identity guarantee (d) rests on. **The standing decision, stated so it cannot be read as settled:** whether the native channel should carry web analytics **at all** is an owner decision under the escalation list (public exposure + egress, PDR-027 §2), and its **deadline is before any APK is distributed to anyone outside the author's family** — not before this packet, and not at the end of this milestone. It is open until that decision is made.

- **Rationale.** L1's whole ladder — schema, journal, write path, export — is unreachable until the app has a native container to hold a native store, so P1 buys exactly one thing and buys it cleanly: a container that changes nothing. That constraint is what makes byte-identity the right acceptance criterion rather than a nicety; "the Android build behaves identically" is either a property a test can check or a sentence somebody wrote. The two invariants are chosen by the same test: each states the fail-closed default it actually enforces, and each names in its Scope what it knowingly does not cover — which is how (i) stays visible instead of becoming the thing everyone assumed someone else had handled. The two corrections are one defect in two costumes: a claim with a false provenance. `MUST_MATCH` was described as a deliberate choice by configs that declare nothing, and the `ALLOW_LOOSE` replacement arrived as an unverified note that would have read as an observation. Correcting the first while committing the second would have been the more expensive mistake, because it would have looked like diligence.

- **No dedicated ADR by design at the repo layer.** The architecture is decided upstream — **ADR-043** §1 (platform is Capacitor; native storage is the source of truth; WebView storage forbidden for persistent data), §3 (Android-first, direct APK) and §5 (origin storage is not inherited, so migration needs an explicit bridge — P3, not here); **PDR-026** §2 (the L1..L7 ladder); **ADR-037** (buildless hybrid parity and the parity-suite precedent this packet extends rather than replaces); **ADR-030** §2 (invariants compiled into structural checks); **ADR-024** (versioning by filename — untouched: no published asset changes); **ADR-020** (the promotion gate — untouched); **PDR-019** (dark build — direct APK is compatible, and nothing here makes the app publicly reachable). This entry records what the repo did with those, not a new architectural decision.

- **Alternatives considered.**
  1. **Point `webDir` at `app/`.** Rejected — it packages `node_modules/`, `tests/` and the baselines into the APK. See (a).
  2. **Hand-maintain the APK's file list in `capacitor.config.json`.** Rejected — a second ship list is a second thing to forget on every extraction packet, and it fails silently in both directions. See (a).
  3. **Write a second `COPY` parser for the stager.** Rejected — see (b).
  4. **Record the full ADR-043 §1 inversion as an enforced invariant now.** Rejected — the native store is L1-P2 and family data is still in `localStorage`; claiming it would be exactly the wish-list drift `AGENTS.md` §10 exists to prevent. See (c).
  5. **Strip comments before scanning for storage access.** Rejected — it fails open on any line carrying a `https://` literal. See (c).
  6. **Let the native spec stage the web root itself.** Rejected — it makes its own assertion tautological. See (d).
  7. **Leave Android Auto Backup at the template default.** Rejected, and escalated first with both branches written out. See (e).
  8. **Give the `native` project its own baseline set.** Rejected — tautological given byte-identity, and it doubles the re-blessing cost. See (g).
  9. **Disable service-worker registration in the native build.** Rejected twice over: it is a product change this packet forbids, and it would fork the two channels' bytes. See (i).
  10. **Pin Capacitor 6.x** so the dev box's Node 18 could run the CLI. Rejected — see (n).
  11. **Generate `native/android/` in CI instead of committing it.** Rejected — the (e) hardening and the application id do not survive regeneration, and the invariant that pins them would have nothing to read.
  12. **State the `ALLOW_LOOSE` claim as an owner-supplied fact.** Rejected on owner correction — see (k).
  13. **Switch the three configs to an explicit `MUST_MATCH` while in the files.** Rejected — the delivery channel is the only one and a mismatch would break it. Recorded as open debt. See (j).
  14. **Correct only the two `cloudbuild.yaml` files the handoff named**, leaving `api/cloudbuild.staging.yaml`. Rejected — it is the identical claim in the identical class of file; a partial correction manufactures a new inconsistency. Flagged in the plan as a deliberate widening of a literal instruction.
  15. **Reconcile `AGENTS.md` §8's layout block and §3's intent-level entry now.** Rejected — contract edits stay out of packets and land at milestone close.

- **Supersedes.** None. **Corrects** the `MUST_MATCH` claim in `app/cloudbuild.yaml`, `api/cloudbuild.yaml`, `api/cloudbuild.staging.yaml` and `docs/RUNBOOK.md` (three sites), per (j). **Amends by reference, leaving the records byte-untouched:** `L2-DL-001` (4) and (6), `A3-DL-002` (2) and (7), and `A3-DL-003` (c) — each states or relies on the `MUST_MATCH` reading; each was written in good faith on the same false premise, and this log's convention is amendment by reference rather than rewriting history. `docs/execution-map.md`'s A3-CLOSE bullet carries the same reading and is likewise left byte-untouched: it is a record of what a packet shipped, the `A3-DL-004` (n)(3) disposition.

- **Effects.**
  - **Added (`native/`):** `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, `capacitor.config.json`, `.gitignore`, `tools/stage-webdir.js`, and the generated-and-committed `android/` project, whose `AndroidManifest.xml` carries the (e) hand edit and whose `res/xml/{data_extraction_rules,backup_rules}.xml` are new.
  - **Added (`app/tests/`):** `support/ship-list.js` (extracted, per (b)), `storage-seam.spec.js`, `native-shell.spec.js`.
  - **Changed (`app/`, dev/CI-only files only):** `tests/delivery-contract.spec.js` (requires the extracted parser; assertions and test count unchanged), `tests/server.js` (two delivery profiles, a servable root), `tests/support/seed.js` (a `parityProfile` option), `tests/behavior.spec.js` (the service-worker block skips under `capacitor`, with the (h) reason inline), `playwright.config.js` (the `native` project, a second `webServer`, the widened `contract` `testMatch`). **No shipped `app/` file is touched** — not `index.html`, `sw.js`, `manifest.json`, `offline.html`, `kb-v1.json`, `icons/` or `m/` — and no baseline in `tests/__baselines__/` is rewritten.
  - **Changed (harness):** `scripts/parity-suite.sh` stages the web root before running; `.github/workflows/ci.yml` gains the `android` job; `.pre-commit-config.yaml` excludes `native/android/` from the two auto-fixing hooks and states why. The read-only validators still run repo-wide.
  - **Changed (build-configs, COMMENT-ONLY):** `app/cloudbuild.yaml`, `api/cloudbuild.yaml`, `api/cloudbuild.staging.yaml` — per (j)/(k). **Every executable line, flag, step and substitution is byte-untouched**, the `A3-DL-003` (e)(2) / `A3-DL-004` (g) posture.
  - **Changed (spine):** `docs/RUNBOOK.md` — the three corrected `MUST_MATCH` sites, a new **Substitution matching — what is not protected** section carrying the (m) split and the open debt, a new **Android shell (Capacitor)** section with the numbered owner-run APK procedure and the on-device smoke, and two additions to the parity-suite section. `docs/INVARIANTS.md` — `LSC-P1-INV-001`, `LSC-P1-INV-002`. `docs/execution-map.md` — the milestone block and the namespace split.
  - **Untouched, and verified so:** `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/RUNTIME-INVARIANTS.md`, `docs/product/**`, `README.md`, `docker-compose.yml`, everything under `api/**` except the two build-config header comments, `app/Dockerfile`, `app/nginx.conf`, `app/docker-entrypoint.sh`, and every prior decision-log entry.
  - **Operability: none this packet** (ADR-013 / contract §4.7). No knob and no signal is added. Nothing qualitative or tunable is introduced: the application id, `webDir`, scheme and hostname are **build identity**, not policy, and they are pinned by `LSC-P1-INV-002` — a test that reds on change is a stronger guarantee than a `changed_in` field, and the typed surface (`api/theygrow_api/parameters.py`) is Python-side and untouched. `PARAMETERS_SCHEMA_VERSION` stays **5**; no `theygrow_api.signals` taxonomy entry, no `emitted_now` change, no producer added or removed; no GA4 event, since no shipped `app/` file changes. The toolchain versions follow the existing pin-and-refuse precedent (`scripts/parity-suite.sh` ↔ `app/package.json`). **No new Python runtime import**, so the mypy hook's `additional_dependencies` are unchanged.
  - **§4-safe, with one thing named rather than waved past.** No corpus record, database row, credential, host, user, password or connection string enters the repository; no live identifier enters a contract or spine file; the parity suite still blocks the analytics beacon, so CI generates no third-party egress. The APK inherits the shell's existing GA4 egress unchanged — that is (o), and it is open with a stated deadline, not settled. The backup hardening in (e) **removes** an egress the platform would otherwise have performed by default.
  - **Debt recorded, not fixed:** the `substitutionOption` switch (j), with what would close it; the unverified `ALLOW_LOOSE` claim (k); the open PWA merge-trigger expectation (m); a release signing config and key custody (n); the analytics decision (o) with its deadline; the service-worker disposition (i), assigned to L1-P2; and `AGENTS.md` §8 / §3 reconciliation, assigned to milestone close.
  - **Validation-honesty caveat** (M4-CLOSE discipline). `pre-commit run --all-files` green; the full non-visual parity suite green (**294 passed, 6 skipped**, zero failures) across `contract`, `dom-desktop`, `dom-mobile`, `behavior` and `native`; the extraction proven behaviour-preserving by an unchanged test count (162 → 162); and both new invariants proven to **bite** by deliberate negative checks — an undeclared `localStorage` read in a surface module, and a stray file in the staged web root, each producing the intended red before being reverted. **What that does not prove.** The **visual** projects were not run: they are valid only inside the pinned container and the Docker daemon needs an owner `sudo`. **The APK has never been built** — this machine has no JDK, no Android SDK and no Gradle, and the `android` CI job's first run is the first proof the shell compiles. **Nothing has run on a device:** that the app launches, that data survives a cold restart, that the backup exclusions are honoured by the OS, and what the service worker actually does inside the WebView are all owner-run observations (`docs/RUNBOOK.md`, "Android shell (Capacitor)"), and (i) stays formally open until the last of them is made.
